# BC Shooting Location Checker — build notes (v99)

## What it is
A self-contained HTML/JS tool (Leaflet map + live queries to BC's open geospatial data, plus a couple of other free services). Click a spot in BC and it reports:
- **Parcel ownership & type** (Crown provincial / federal / private / municipal / untitled provincial / etc.), parcel class/status, regional district — from ParcelMap BC.
- **Whether the point is inside an incorporated municipality** — from BC Legal Administrative Boundaries.
- **Whether it overlaps a Provincial Park / Ecological Reserve / Protected Area** — from BC Parks & Protected Areas data.
- **Forestry cutblocks within 2 km** (widened from 1 km as of v78 — see the v78 section below) — merges RESULTS Openings (silviculture records) with FTEN's cutting-permit blocks (which exist as soon as a block is *planned*, before RESULTS has anything), so a block that's authorized but not yet harvested still shows up. Condensed on screen to ID/block #, life cycle, and whichever date is most relevant (completed / started / planned); click any one for the full planned/start/completion breakdown, area, licensee, and raw logging-phase status in a popup modal. As of v26, a record whose polygon or claimed area is implausibly large for a single cutblock is flagged and excluded from the map draw (so it can't blot out everything else nearby) while staying listed in the report — see the v26 section below. As of v27, the abbreviated status code (e.g. "FG", "RET") is decoded with a plain-English meaning where BC's own documentation confirms one — see the v27 section below. As of v29, a separate "Life Cycle" field (PENDING/ACTIVE/RETIRED, from FTEN's cutting-permit data) is shown in the cutblock detail popup when known — see the v29 section below. As of v30, clicking a spot where two or more cutblock polygons genuinely overlap in BC's own data shows all of them to choose from, instead of only whichever one happened to be drawn on top — see the v30 section below. As of v31, the condensed report-card line (and the v30 overlap chooser's rows) show that Life Cycle info in place of the raw status code, with the raw status still one click away via "Details" — see the v31 section below. As of v32, clicking a cutblock polygon on the map no longer silently pans the map underneath the selected point — see the v32 section below. As of v33, clicking any plotted feature on the map (cutblocks included) no longer re-selects that point as a brand-new location and relocates the marker — see the v33 section below (v32's fix addressed a real but minor issue; v33 is the actual, root-cause fix for what was being reported). As of v34, the cutblock detail popup's closing "where this data comes from" paragraph is no longer printed inline every time — it's one click away behind a small "i" button next to the block/opening ID — see the v34 section below. As of v35, the map's three cutblock colors are green (closed out), red (started/active but not closed out), and blue (planned, not started) — see the v35 section below. As of v36, a third basemap option, Topo (Esri World Topographic Map), sits alongside Satellite and Street — see the v36 section below. As of v37, cutblocks with a disturbance start date before 1990 are excluded at the query level (blocks that are only planned/not yet started, with no start date at all, are still included) — see the v37 section below. As of v38, forest service road lines are drawn solid instead of dashed; a request to also pull road-condition ratings, gates, and impassable sections from backroadstatus.com was declined on ToS grounds — see the v38 section below. As of v39, motor vehicle closures/restrictions from BC's own Wildlife Act Motor Vehicle Prohibition Regulation data are shown on the map and in a dedicated report card, as a public-data substitute for the "roadblocks" part of v38's declined request — see the v39 section below. As of v40, the app is generally less text-heavy (the 10 manual checklist items are now a one-line summary each, full explanation one click away), and the map supports onX-style personal markup — your own pins, lines and areas, drawn/edited/deleted with the draw toolbar and auto-saved in your browser (Export/Import to move them elsewhere) — see the v40 section below. As of v41, the whole screen is the map: the location report lives in a collapsible drawer along the bottom and the full checklist lives in a collapsible, translucent drawer along the right edge — see the v41 section below. As of v42, the app can optionally use Mapbox tiles for all three basemaps instead of Esri/OpenStreetMap, once Karim pastes his own free Mapbox access token into one clearly marked line in the file — see the v42 section below. As of v43, the bottom report drawer can be dragged taller/shorter by its grip handle (with the chosen height remembered for next time), and both the bottom drawer and the side checklist drawer now share the same translucency level so they read as one consistent "floating over the map" look — see the v43 section below. As of v44, both the single-click report and Potential Spots now treat a point with no ParcelMap record at all as a viable, clearly-labeled "presumed Crown" result instead of an inconclusive one, and Mapbox (if a token is filled in) now tracks its own tile usage locally and automatically falls back to the Esri/OpenStreetMap basemaps if it ever hits a safety cap Karim set — see the v44 section below. As of v45, Potential Spots' building/highway buffers were widened to Karim's own larger safety margin, the same buffer now also applies to private-titled land, and any spot within 500m of an *active* cutblock gets a red proximity-warning overlay instead of being excluded — see the v45 section below. As of v46, the Potential Spots map popups show only high-yield info with everything else moved behind an "i" button, and every active cutblock is now drawn on the map as its own outline (plus the exact block ID and distance in the popup detail) so the 500m warning is checkable against real data, not just trusted — see the v46 section below. As of v47, the filled red 500m cutblock-proximity squares (added in v45) were removed entirely after Karim reported them covering far more of the map than seemed right — the dashed active-cutblock outline (v46) plus the popup's warning pill/detail were, briefly, the only way that warning was shown. As of v48, Karim traced that report to its actual root cause — one unusually large active cutblock, whose 500m halo was being recomputed independently by every nearby spot — and asked for the fix to match: instead of any per-spot proximity check, a single 500m buffer zone is now drawn directly around each active cutblock's own real shape, once, so the shaded area always matches what the cutblock actually looks like on the map. As of v49, an active cutblock record flagged by the app's existing "implausibly large for a single cutblock" sanity check (already used to keep the single-click report's map from being swallowed by a mislabeled licence/operating-area boundary, see the v26 section below) is now excluded from Potential Spots' cutblock buffer feature entirely, not just drawn more carefully. As of v50, the buffer distance was reduced from 500m to 400m and the shaded zone is noticeably more solid (fill opacity roughly doubled) at Karim's request. As of v51, that opacity was bumped again, from 0.35 to 0.5. As of v52, the two visual layers were rebalanced — the real cutblock's own dashed outline is now fully opaque, and the approximate buffer-zone fill was brought back down to 0.20. As of v53, a "Clear Potential Spots" button sits next to "Show Potential Spots (this view)" so its shapes can be removed without re-running the query. As of v54, the 400m buffer zone's own outer boundary is now traced with a solid line (distinct from the real cutblock's dashed outline), so the zone's true edge is visible rather than just implied by where the light fill happens to stop. As of v55, the location pin can be dragged to a new spot, fixing a real usability gap: Potential Spots' shapes intentionally block a plain click from moving the pin (same fix v33 relied on to stop the opposite bug), so once those shapes cover most of the view there was no reliable empty area left to click — see the v55 section below. As of v56, Potential Spots runs noticeably faster: its gap-detection sweep now skips a parcel entirely with a cheap bounding-box check before doing the expensive point-in-polygon walk, and its Crown-parcel query now fires in parallel with the other layer queries instead of being awaited alone first — see the v56 section below. As of v57, Potential Spots no longer leaves silent gaps: a Crown/untitled parcel that overlaps a park, a municipality, or (when zoomed in enough) the road/building/private-land buffer is now always drawn — the restricted portion in red with its reason on click, the rest (if any) in the same teal as any other potential spot — instead of the whole parcel simply vanishing from the map; the teal fill was also made noticeably more transparent — see the v57 section below. As of v58, the fixed top bar no longer overlaps the side checklist drawer (or the map's basemap/action buttons) on window sizes where the bar wraps to two lines, and Potential Spots now draws every nearby side road (BC's Digital Road Atlas — the general public road network, not the forest-service-road layer) directly on the map, colored by whether the wider named-highway buffer applies, with a hover tooltip for road class and buffer distance — see the v58 section below. As of v59, the purple "no parcel record" gap fill was made as transparent as the teal spot fill (0.3 → 0.16), so the two read as the same visual weight. As of v60, the header's own long inline "how to select a location" sentence moved behind an "i" icon next to the Lat/Lng inputs, shrinking the header's permanent content rather than just reacting to it wrapping — v58's ResizeObserver fix stays in place too, as a safety net for whatever content remains. As of v61, the app shows a clear on-page message instead of a silent blank map if Leaflet (loaded live from cdnjs.cloudflare.com) ever fails to load — previously that failure mode left every other UI element rendering normally while the map area was just empty, with nothing a non-technical user would ever see explaining why. As of v62, a longstanding layout bug (predating this session's changes) was found on a self-review pass and fixed: the basemap toggle and map-action buttons sat under the collapsed side-checklist tab by a constant ~23px at every window width, clipping the "Topo" button and the top of the action-button box. As of v81, this card also specifically flags when the picked point itself falls within the same 400m active-cutblock buffer zone Potential Spots already shades on the map — distinct from, and stronger than, the generic "N records within 2km" flag every hit already got — see the v81 section below. As of v83, this flag is red, not amber — Karim corrected the app's own design judgment on this, since active logging traffic inside that buffer is a real, direct hazard rather than a background advisory — see the v83 section below.
- **Crown land tenures (leases/licences/permits)** overlapping the point — from TANTALIS Crown Tenures. As of v81, a tenure whose purpose is the kind that often carries its own seasonal closure/operating-period condition (recreation/ski/guide-outfitting/grazing and similar — Karim's own example was a Squamish heli-ski tenure closed Dec 1–Apr 1) gets an explicit caveat that BC doesn't publish those dates as open data, so this can't be checked automatically — see the v81 section below.
- **Forest managed licences (woodlots, community forests, etc.)** overlapping the point — from Forest Tenure Managed Licence.
- **Road access & conditions** — forest service road tenure sections within 1 km (active vs. retired, maintaining licensee) from Forest Tenure Road Section Lines — each road shown in full plus any roads that connect to it at a junction within 1 km — plus live official highway events within ~10 km from DriveBC's Open511 API. A link out to backroadstatus.com, deep-linked to the exact coordinates, is always shown for community-reported conditions no government feed captures.
- **Motor vehicle closures/restrictions within 1 km** — from BC's Wildlife Act Motor Vehicle Prohibition Regulation data (routes and closed areas), as of v39 (see the v39 section below). This is a legal closure/restriction feed, not a physical road-condition report — it won't show washouts, deactivations, or gates on a road that's otherwise legally open.
- **Recreation sites, trails, and recreation reserves within 1 km** — from Recreation Sites and Trails BC (RSTBC), as of v28 (see the v28 section below).
- **Whether the point is inside a Wildlife Management Area** — from TANTALIS Wildlife Management Areas (B.C. Reg 161/87), as of v68 (see the v68 section below). Advisory only, not exclusionary: some WMAs carry their own gazetted Wildlife Act access/discharge restrictions and some don't, so a hit prompts a manual check of that WMA's own Order in Council rather than an automatic red flag.
- **An on-the-spot municipal/regional-district bylaw lookup** — for a growing list of verified jurisdictions (Chilliwack, Abbotsford, Kelowna as of v13), the app fetches the actual bylaw document live in the browser, parses it, and shows the real prohibition clause (confirmed working in Karim's own browser); everywhere else, a dynamically generated search link for whichever jurisdiction the click landed in.
- **Personal map markup, onX-style** — as of v40, drop pins and draw lines/areas of your own (name, notes, colour), separate from every BC data layer, edited/reshaped/deleted with a draw toolbar on the map and browsable in a "My Markup" panel; auto-saved to your browser plus an Export/Import file round-trip — see the v40 section below. As of v93, a pin can also carry one of 17 curated Lucide icons (grouped by category: hunting/shooting infrastructure, access & terrain, camp & shelter, alerts & navigation) drawn over its colour dot, entirely optional — see the v93 section below. As of v96, the draw toolbar's own delete ("trash can") mode no longer offers a one-click "Clear All" that could wipe every pin/line/area with no confirmation — see the v96 section below.

It combines these into a plain-language, colour-coded preliminary verdict (red/amber/green/gray) — as of v76, condensed to just the state plus a clickable chip per flagged section, with the actual explanation living once, in that section's own report card, rather than repeated in a long list up top (see the v76 section below). As of v78, every individual report card also gets that same red/amber/green/gray tint consistently — a "no overlap"/"none found" clean result now tints its card green (previously only cards with an actual flag were tinted at all, so a clean municipality/park/WMA/recreation/MVPR/cutblocks/tenures/woodlot/road card rendered untinted white instead of reassuringly green), and clicking anywhere on a card's own title bar — not just its "Details ▾" button — expands or collapses it, matching how the checklist's jump buttons already land on cards people then have to open themselves — see the v78 section below. As of v80, the verdict's flag-chip list is laid out in three columns by severity — clear (green), caution (amber), flagged (red), with a small separate row underneath for anything whose data simply couldn't be retrieved (gray) — instead of one long wrapped row, now that v78 flags almost every section (not just the exceptional ones); see the v80 section below. Plus a **full checklist** split into:
- **Auto items** (8 as of v28, 9 as of v68, 10 as of v77 — the bylaw lookup moved in from the manual list) — a green "Done"/red "Incomplete" pill saying whether that check actually ran for the picked location, plus (once Done) a "See report ›" button that jumps to, and expands, the exact report card with what it found — see the v77 section below. No inline result text or checkbox anymore; what a check found lives once, in its own card. As of v78, each item's "i" and "See report ›" buttons are grouped and right-aligned to the row's edge instead of flowing inline right after the title text — see the v78 section below.
- **Manual items** (8 as of v77) — things no province-wide open dataset covers. Each is a checkbox, a short title, and an "i" button with the full explanation and links; check them off yourself, every time — these can never be fully automated. "Lawful access" moved to a Disclaimers blurb and "hunting scope" was dropped entirely as of v77 — see below.

The map defaults to a **satellite hybrid basemap** — Esri World Imagery with road lines and place-name labels overlaid on top (v11), the standard "satellite + labels" look, with a one-click toggle back to an OpenStreetMap street view (top-right of the map). On top of whichever base is showing, the map **highlights everything it finds** once a spot is clicked: parcel filled/coloured by ownership type, municipal boundary as a dashed outline, park as a teal fill, cutblocks coloured by best-effort closed-out / not-closed-out / planned-not-started status (as of v26, an implausibly large cutblock record is flagged and skipped when drawing, rather than blotting out the whole map — see the v26 section below), the two tenure layers as dotted outlines, forest service roads as dashed lines, and (as of v28) recreation sites/trails/areas in pink (points, dashed lines, and filled polygons respectively) — every shape opens a popup on click, and roads (as of v79, in addition to their existing hover tooltip) do too. Parcels are drawn at the *bottom* of the overlay stacking order (so a smaller shape drawn later is what Leaflet delivers the click event to, when one is actually there to receive it — see the v81 note on why that alone wasn't enough). As of v23, every other parcel within 1 km of the click is also drawn, faded, for context — see the v23 section below. As of v79, a click that lands where two or more of these shapes genuinely overlap or sit right next to each other — of any mix of types, not just two records of the same kind — opens one popup listing all of them instead of only whichever one happened to be drawn on top; pick a row for its full detail, or (as of v89) just hover a row to see that record's own shape highlighted on the map before picking. This generalizes what v30 originally built for cutblock-vs-cutblock overlaps alone into one mechanism every layer shares — see the v79 section below. As of v80, parcels (and municipality/park) briefly opted back out of that shared chooser, on the theory that a parcel almost always geometrically underlies whatever smaller feature you're actually trying to click and so was turning nearly every click into an unwanted "2 records here" chooser. As of v81, that turned out to be the wrong diagnosis — the real problem was that tenure/woodlot/WMA/municipality are drawn with `fill:false` (outline only), and per the SVG click-hit-testing spec an unfilled shape's interior never receives a click at all, so every click inside one of those shapes was *always* falling straight through to the parcel underneath regardless of chooser membership. Fixed at the root (a small non-zero fill makes the interior genuinely clickable) and parcels/municipality/park were restored to the shared chooser exactly as before — see the v81 section below. Also as of v81, a polygon's own click hit-test (used by every layer above) now counts a click on its visible boundary line as a hit, not just strictly inside it — most of these shapes are drawn with a near-invisible fill, so the dashed outline is the only thing a user can actually see to click on — see the v81 section below. As of v83, that shared chooser turned out to only ever have covered the single-click report's own shapes (`overlayLayer`) — Shooting Spots' gap/spot/restricted polygons and active-cutblock outlines, View Parcels' parcels, and Reveal Road's roads had each always used their own private, per-shape popup instead, entirely bypassing the chooser mechanism v79-v81 built, so a click where one of those shapes sat on top of a cutblock or a road never offered a choice at all. All four now share the same chooser, and a `source` tag on every registered entry lets them each run and reset independently without one clearing another's entries out from under it — see the v83 section below.

**Collapsible tools drawer**, on the **right edge as of v76** (moved off the left edge entirely — see the v76 section below), stacked directly under the checklist tab: a slim "Tools" tab (its checkmark emoji dropped in the same v76 pass, and its hammer-and-wrench emoji dropped too as of v82 — see the v82 section below), click to expand into a panel. Top to bottom: **Map type** — the Satellite/Street/Topo basemap picker, a 3-way segmented slider (v75). **Mapping Functions** (renamed from "Map layers" in v76, and now the *only* action group — the old separate "Potential spots" group was folded into it, see below):
- **"View Parcels"** (renamed from "Crown Land" in v76) — used to query only Crown provincial / untitled provincial parcels; now queries and draws **every** parcel currently in view, colour-coded by ownership type using the same `OWNER_COLORS` palette the report/legend already use (red private, green Crown provincial, gold untitled provincial, purple municipal, blue First Nation, brown Crown federal) — see the v76 section below. Still button-triggered only, not automatic on pan/zoom (v19/v20's original reasoning — an always-on query was too slow — still applies, more so now that every parcel draws instead of just two categories).
- **"Shooting Spots"** — a further-filtered version of View Parcels' old Crown-only query: a Crown/untitled parcel only counts as a potential spot if it also clears the same hard-exclusion checks the click report already applies (not inside a park, not inside a municipal boundary). Crown tenures, forest managed licences, and nearby forestry openings — the report's *amber*, non-exclusionary checks — are shown as ⚠ advisories in each spot's popup instead of excluding it, mirroring the click report's own logic exactly. Drawn in teal, dashed border. As of v24, it also excludes/trims anything too close to a road or a building; as of v25 the actual distances used are the real Wildlife Act numbers (100 m buildings, 25 m default road allowance, 400 m near a specific named list of highways) instead of v24's placeholder flat 400 m — see the v25 section below. **Runs two ways as of v71**: automatically, within a fixed 2 km `DWITHIN` circle around wherever a location was just picked (v69/v70 — click, drag the pin, drag the header pin, search, or coordinate entry, all route through this); or on demand, via this button, for whatever's currently in view (an `INTERSECTS` viewport query, zoom-gated the same way the original v19 button was).
- **"Reveal Road"** (new in v82) — the same on-demand, view-scoped pattern as View Parcels, querying and drawing every road currently in view. As of v85, that's two BC road datasets together, not one — the Digital Road Atlas (general public roads: rust/orange for a listed major highway's wider Wildlife Act buffer, slate for the 25 m default) plus FTEN Road Section Lines (forest service and resource roads, including branches: tan, grey if retired) — see the v85 section below for why DRA alone had been missing exactly the roads Karim was looking for. Hover any road for its class, status, and (for public roads) exact buffer distance. Lets you see the full road picture on its own without running the full Crown-land/park/municipality check too — see the v82 section below. As of v84, its zoom gate is zoom 9 (was 11 — see the v84 section below), matching Shooting Spots' own gate, so it now runs at the same normal scanning zoom the other two buttons already work at.
- **"Clear ▾"** (was two separate buttons — "Clear Potential Spots" and "Clear All" — through v85; replaced in v86 with a single dropdown) — one clear action per section of the single-click location report below (Parcel, Municipal Boundary, Bylaw Lookup, Park/Protected Area, Wildlife Management Area, Recreation Sites & Trails, Motor Vehicle Closures, Forestry Activity, Crown Land Tenures, Forest Tenure/Woodlot, Road Access — the same 11 sections the report itself is built from; v86 briefly listed the three Mapping Functions buttons here instead, corrected in v87 — see the v87 section below). Picking one removes just that section's shapes from the map and its card from the report, leaving the pin, every other section, and View Parcels/Shooting Spots/Reveal Road (each already replaces its own contents the next time its own button is pressed) untouched. A status note sits underneath, shared by all three Mapping Functions buttons above — as of v96, only actually present once one of them has said something, rather than sitting there as a visibly empty card beforehand. Deliberately never touches your own drawn markup (My Markup, below). (v95 briefly removed this whole dropdown by mistake, thinking it was the thing Karim wanted gone; reverted in full as of v96 — see the v96 section below for what he actually meant.)

**My Markup**, moved in from a floating map-corner widget as of v75 — the same pin/line/area list, Export/Import, and draw-toolbar instructions as before, living in the drawer as its own group. As of v76, the scrollable drawer body (`.td-scroll`) picks up `min-height:0` — same fix `.bp-body` and `.checklist-scroll` already needed for the identical flexbox bug — so a tall My Markup list is actually reachable/scrollable instead of being silently clipped by the drawer's own `overflow:hidden`; see the v76 section below.

Legend pinned in the map's bottom-left corner (collapsed by default as of v22 — see below). As of v76, moving the tools drawer off the left edge (see above) also resolved a standing complaint that the legend sat layered behind the left-edge toolbar/action buttons — there's nothing left on the left edge to overlap it with. Map auto-zooms to the parcel after each single-point lookup.

The page chrome is compact: the header's explanatory blurb, the legal disclaimer's full text, the footer's data-source list, and (as of v22) the checklist's how-it-works blurb are each tucked behind a small "i"/"⚠️" button that pops up a modal on click, rather than sitting permanently on the page. As of v22, each report card's field-by-field detail is similarly tucked behind a per-card "Details" toggle rather than always shown — see the v22 section below.

Delivered via SendUserFile as a standalone HTML file (works fully offline-of-Claude once downloaded — open directly in any browser). A desktop-linked Cowork artifact copy was created early on but the sync tool has been intermittently unavailable this session — **that copy may be stale**; the file sent via chat is always the current version.

## Scope decided with Karim
- Recreational target shooting (not hunting) is the primary use case.
- Web map first; script/CLI form could follow later using the same query logic.
- Municipal bylaws: no province-wide API/dataset of bylaw *text* exists. v12 added a dynamic per-click search link; v13 added genuine live client-side fetch-and-parse of the actual bylaw document for a growing, hand-verified list of jurisdictions (confirmed working live by Karim).
- Crown land: BC doesn't publish a positive province-wide "this is Crown land" layer as a single dataset, but ParcelMap BC's own `OWNER_TYPE` attribute *does* classify individual parcels as Crown Provincial or Untitled Provincial — real per-parcel data, not an inference. Five iterations have now happened: a click-report presumption (v14, reverted), an inversion-style always-on tint (v15, tuned v16, removed v17), a genuine always-on positive query (v18), and finally converting that positive query to on-demand buttons plus a derived "potential spots" filter (v19). v20 and v21 were bug-fix rounds against v19's real-world behaviour, not new iterations of the feature itself — see the dedicated history sections below.
- "Potential shooting spots" is explicitly defined, at Karim's direction, as satisfying **every automated condition the checklist already checks** — not just ownership type. In practice that means: Crown/untitled ownership (hard requirement), not inside a park (hard exclude, matches the click report's red/prohibited reasoning), not inside a municipality (hard exclude, matches the click report's red/bylaws-apply reasoning). Crown tenures, forest managed licences, and nearby forestry openings stay as non-exclusionary ⚠ advisories, because that's how the click report itself already treats them ("usually still fine, check for posted restrictions") — excluding on those would leave almost nothing shown, since BC backcountry Crown land very often has some tenure or forestry record nearby. As of v24, Karim also required a buffer around major roads/highways and buildings, with **partial** exclusion (clip the affected portion of a parcel, not the whole thing) rather than an all-or-nothing overlap test — v24 shipped this with a flat, invented 400 m distance for everything; v25 replaced that with the actual regulation numbers after Karim supplied the real BC Hunting and Trapping Regulations Synopsis — see the v25 section below.
- No specific test region requested — built to work anywhere in BC.
- Development priorities chosen so far, in order: Crown land tenure overlay → layout/overlap bug fixes → cutblock harvest completion date → road access/conditions → wider search radius + connected-road network + hover-for-roads → parcel z-order fix → UI condensation (info modals + collapsed cutblock lines) → planned cutblocks (FTEN merge) → satellite basemap → satellite hybrid (roads + labels) → on-the-spot bylaw search link → live bylaw fetch+parse → presumed Crown land in the click report (v14, reverted) → always-on Crown land tint/inversion on the map (v15, tuned v16, removed and Trapping Regulations Synopsis (v25) → flagging/skipping implausibly large cutblock polygons that were blotting out the map (v26) → a sourced plain-English glossary for the abbreviated logging-phase status codes (v27) → auto-checking nearby recreation sites/trails via Recreation Sites and Trails BC, chosen by Karim from a menu of researched automation candidates (v28) → adding the cutblock `LIFE_CYCLE_STATUS_CODE` ("Life Cycle") field, sourced from BC's own Open Government Portal dataset description (v29) → fixing overlapping cutblock polygons so a click surfaces every genuinely-overlapping record instead of only whichever one Leaflet routes the click to (v30) → replacing the condensed cutblock list's raw logging-phase status with Life Cycle info, keeping the raw status one click away in "Details" (v31) → stopping the map from auto-panning (and appearing to move the selected location) every time a cutblock is clicked (v32) → stopping clicks on any plotted map feature from also re-selecting that point as a new location and relocating the marker, the actual root cause behind v32's report (v33) → moving the cutblock popup's sourcing blurb behind a small "i" button instead of always printing it inline (v34) → recoloring the three cutblock map states to green/red/blue (v35) → adding an Esri Topo basemap option after a request to match backroadstatus.com's map (v36) → filtering cutblock data at the query level to exclude pre-1990 start dates, keeping planned-only blocks (v37) → solid (not dashed) road lines; a request to pull backroadstatus.com's road-condition/gate/impasse data was declined on ToS grounds (v38) → motor vehicle closures/restrictions from BC's own Wildlife Act Motor Vehicle Prohibition Regulation data, as a public-data substitute for the "roadblocks" part of that declined request (v39) → a general text/density cleanup pass (10 manual checklist items condensed to a one-line summary each) plus onX-style personal map markup — pins/lines/areas, drawn/edited/deleted with a map toolbar, auto-saved locally with Export/Import (v40) → a full-screen map layout, with the location report and checklist moved into ## v99: Module split, slice four (pass 1 of 3) — the single-click report's map-drawing code moves to src/map/render.js

*"Let's continue building, start on map render."* — `docs/PLAN.md`'s "Suggested order" names step 4 as `map/render.js`, "the biggest slice," explicitly flagged to be done "in a few passes (report-click rendering, then Shooting Spots, then View Parcels/Reveal Road), not all at once." This is that first pass.

Moved, verbatim, comments included: `OVERLAY_CATEGORY_KEYS`/`overlayCategoryLayers` (the per-report-category sub-layer-groups the Clear ▾ dropdown and this rendering code both key off), `renderForestServiceRoads()` (the road-drawing helper shared with Reveal Road, per v85), `renderMapOverlays()` itself — the function that takes the single-click report's query results and actually draws whichever of parcel/municipality/park/WMA/tenure/woodlot/cutblocks/recreation/MVPR/road came back, registering each drawn shape with the shared chooser from v98 — and `cutblockColor()`/`cutblockStyle()`/`cutblockHighlightStyle()`, the small pure colour/style helpers `renderMapOverlays()` uses for cutblocks. All now live in `src/map/render.js`, loaded via `<script src>` after `src/map/chooser.js` (`renderMapOverlays()` calls `pushClickable()`/`resetClickableSource()`/`openNearbyFeaturesPopupAt()`, all defined there) and before the main inline script.

**The v98 lesson held up on the first real test.** `docs/PLAN.md` predicted, ahead of this slice, that `render.js` would hit the same "can't move code whose top-level statement touches `map` immediately" wall `chooser.js` hit with `chooserHighlightLayer` — and it did: `overlayLayer`, the parent layer group every category in `renderMapOverlays()` draws into, is `const overlayLayer = L.layerGroup().addTo(map)`, which runs the instant its script loads, before the main script has created `map`. It stays inline, confirmed by the new test below. Also stayed inline, for a different reason: every `*Popup()` builder this code calls (`parcelPopup`, `tenurePopup`, `woodlotPopup`, `wmaPopup`, `muniPopup`, `parkPopup`, `cutblockPopup`, `recreationPopup`, `mvprPopup`, `roadPopup`) — those build each report card's popup HTML content, one per section, and belong with a future `ui/report.js` (`renderReport` + report cards, already on `docs/PLAN.md`'s target list) rather than with the map-drawing logic itself; likewise the general-purpose `esc()`/`ownerCategory()`/`OWNER_COLORS`/`fmtDate()`. None of that matters for correctness here — classic scripts sharing one global scope resolve a name at call time, not at the calling file's own load time, so `render.js`'s functions call all of these exactly as before regardless of which file they end up living in.

Deliberately **not** part of this pass, per the plan's own three-pass split: `renderActiveCutblockOutlines`/`renderActiveCutblockBuffers`/`renderNearbyRoads`/`revealPotentialSpots*` (Shooting Spots, pass 2) and `drawParcelViewLayer`/`revealAllParcels`/`revealAllRoads` (View Parcels/Reveal Road, pass 3) all stay inline for now — `docs/PLAN.md` has the full breakdown.

**Verification.** Syntax-checked `src/map/render.js` and the main inline script (both clean); `<div>`-substring balance still even (306/306 — unchanged from v98, since this pass moved no template-literal HTML, unlike v98's own drop from 309). Confirmed no duplicate declarations of any of the seven moved names remain anywhere in `index.html`. New test `tests/verify_v99_render_split.js` confirms all seven are defined at runtime; the three cutblock style/colour helpers are exercised directly and produce the right output; a real `renderMapOverlays()` call with a fake parcel result runs to completion without throwing and correctly registers that parcel with the shared chooser (`currentClickableFeatures`) — the same "prove the call chain actually works, not just that the function exists" approach v98's test used, adapted here since the Playwright leaflet-stub's generic chainable Proxy can't track real drawn shapes to assert against directly; `overlayLayer` and `parcelPopup` are confirmed still inline (not accidentally dropped, and correctly not claimed as moved); script tag ordered correctly; disk-level check that old declarations are gone and the new file has them. Existing `tests/verify_v79.js`, `verify_v81.js`, the `verify_v82_*` family, `verify_v83.js`, and `verify_v91.js` — this exact function's own much deeper, pre-existing end-to-end coverage — all still pass completely unchanged, which is the real evidence this extraction didn't alter `renderMapOverlays()`'s actual behaviour. Full suite: 24/24 passing.

## v98: Module split, slice three — the map-click chooser moves to src/map/chooser.js, and a real ordering constraint gets documented before it bites the next slice

*"Okay let's work on the next step of the road map."* — `docs/PLAN.md`'s "Suggested order" names step 3 as `map/chooser.js`: "small, self-contained, already has the best test coverage (`verify_v79`, `verify_v83`)."

Moved, verbatim, comments included: `currentClickableFeatures`/`currentPushSource`/`currentNearbyMatches` (the shared state), `resetClickableSource()`, `pushClickable()` (v79's registration point every clickable overlay layer calls), `pixelToleranceMeters()` + the two `NEARBY_CLICK_*_TOLERANCE_PX` constants, `featureNearPoint()`, `openNearbyFeaturesPopupAt()` (the shared click handler itself), and `showNearbyFeatureDetail()`. All now live in `src/map/chooser.js`, loaded via `<script src>` after `src/data/wfs.js` and before the main inline script.

**A real constraint surfaced doing this one, worth calling out plainly.** `chooserHighlightLayer` (v89's hover-highlight-on-the-map layer) and its two functions, `highlightNearbyMatch()`/`clearNearbyMatchHighlight()`, look like part of the same system and were considered for this slice too — but `chooserHighlightLayer` is declared as `const chooserHighlightLayer = L.layerGroup().addTo(map)`, a statement that runs immediately when its script loads, not inside a function body. Every `<script src>` file in this migration loads *before* the main inline script gets to `const map = L.map(...)` (line ~1603) — that's what let v92's and v97's slices sit safely before the main script despite referencing things it defines, since everything in those files only touches those names from inside a function, resolved later at call time, never at load time. `chooserHighlightLayer`'s own initializer doesn't have that luxury: it needs `map` to already exist the instant its file is parsed, which it doesn't yet at that point in the load order. Moving it would have meant either splitting the main inline script around its own `map` creation (a materially bigger, riskier change than "one coherent slice") or turning it into a lazy getter nobody asked for — so it, `CHOOSER_HIGHLIGHT_STYLE`, and its two functions stay inline, confirmed via the new test below. `docs/PLAN.md`'s own next item (`map/render.js`, the biggest remaining slice, with several top-level layer groups of its own — `overlayLayer`, `parcelViewLayer`, `spotLayer`, `roadViewLayer`) is going to run into this same wall more than once, so the plan document now spells this out explicitly ahead of that slice instead of leaving it to be rediscovered. `featureNearPoint()`'s own geometry math (`pointToGeomMeters`/`pointToLineGeomMeters`/`distanceMeters`) stayed inline too, for an unrelated reason — those are general-purpose helpers used well beyond the chooser (Potential Spots' road/building buffers, among others), not part of this system specifically.

**Verification.** Syntax-checked `src/map/chooser.js` and the main inline script (both clean); `<div>`-substring balance still even (306/306 — the count itself dropped from 309, since the moved code includes template-literal HTML strings that grep's plain substring match also counts; the point is that it's still balanced, not that it matches the prior number). Confirmed no duplicate declarations of any of the eight moved names remain anywhere in `index.html`. New test `tests/verify_v98_chooser_split.js` confirms: all eight are defined at runtime; a functional exercise — `resetClickableSource()` + two `pushClickable()` calls at the same point, then a real `openNearbyFeaturesPopupAt()` call — actually produces a genuine 2-record chooser match (not just "the function exists"), and a single registered feature leaves `currentNearbyMatches` untouched, confirming the call chain works correctly even though `chooser.js` loads before `map` exists; `chooserHighlightLayer`/`highlightNearbyMatch` are confirmed still defined and still inline (not accidentally dropped, and correctly not claimed as moved); the `<script src>` tag is ordered correctly in the DOM; and, checked directly against the files on disk, that the old inline declarations are gone and the new file has them. Existing `tests/verify_v79.js`/`verify_v83.js` (this system's original, deeper functional coverage) still pass unchanged. Full suite: 23/23 passing.

## v97: Module split, slice two — the WFS query layer moves to src/data/wfs.js

*"Okay let's work on the next step of the road map. That was all pushed and works."* — `docs/PLAN.md`'s "Suggested order" names step 2 as `data/wfs.js`: "the query layer every feature depends on; get this right early," picked deliberately as the next-least-risky slice after v92's pure-data `config/constants.js`/`config/layers.js` split.

Moved, verbatim, comments included: `WFS_BASE` (the DataBC WFS endpoint), `wfsUrl()` (builds a GetFeature URL from a typeName/CQL/output-format), `jsonpCounter`/`jsonpRequest()` (DataBC's WFS server sends no CORS headers, so a plain `fetch()` from anywhere but a DataBC-approved origin is blocked outright — `queryLayer()` tries `fetch()` first, in case that ever changes, then falls back to loading GeoServer's JSONP output format via a `<script>` tag, which was never subject to the same-origin policy), `queryLayer()` itself, `pt()` (lat/lng → the `SRID=4326;POINT(...)` WKT this app's point queries all use), `cqlFor()` (per-layer DWITHIN/INTERSECTS CQL, keyed off `LAYERS`), and the two viewport-query builders `viewportPolygonWkt()`/`viewportBboxCql()` (View Parcels/Reveal Road's `INTERSECTS`-over-a-rectangle queries — chosen, per that code's own comment, only after two earlier attempts at CQL's `BBOX()` broke on GeoServer's axis-order/CRS handling). All eight now live in `src/data/wfs.js`, loaded via `<script src>` in `<head>` after `src/config/layers.js` (`cqlFor()` references `LAYERS` by bare name) and before the main inline script — same classic-script, shared-global-scope mechanism v92 established, for the same file://-CORS and standalone-offline reasons (see `src/config/constants.js`'s and `docs/PLAN.md`'s own write-ups).

**Scope decisions**, made explicitly rather than by default: DriveBC's Open511 API (`queryOpen511()`/`OPEN511_BASE`) stayed inline — it's a separate REST/JSON service with no CORS restriction and none of the CQL/GetFeature machinery above, and `docs/PLAN.md`'s target structure already earmarks it for its own future `data/open511.js`. `parcelViewBboxCql()` stayed inline too — it's a one-line, feature-specific wrapper around `viewportBboxCql()` for View Parcels specifically, not part of the shared query layer itself. `radiusCql()` (a DWITHIN-CQL builder in the same spirit as `cqlFor()`/`viewportBboxCql()`, used by the automatic per-click Potential Spots sweep) also stayed inline — it's structurally similar but wasn't named in this slice's original scope, and folding it in without being asked would have broken the "one coherent slice at a time" rule this migration has followed since v92. All three are called out explicitly in `src/data/wfs.js`'s own top comment and confirmed still-inline (not accidentally dropped) by the new test below.

**Verification.** `node --check`-equivalent syntax check on `src/data/wfs.js` and the main inline script (both clean); `<div>` tag balance unchanged (309/309); confirmed no duplicate declarations of any of the eight moved names remain anywhere in `index.html`. New test `tests/verify_v97_wfs_split.js` (mirroring `verify_v92_config_split.js`'s pattern) confirms: all eight are defined at runtime and actually produce correct output (`wfsUrl()`'s query string, `pt()`'s WKT point, `cqlFor()`'s DWITHIN clause, the viewport builders' WKT polygon/INTERSECTS clause, and — as a downstream caller that still works through these moved functions — `parcelViewBboxCql()`'s own output); `src/data/wfs.js`'s `<script src>` tag appears in the DOM after `src/config/layers.js` and before the main inline script; `queryOpen511`/`OPEN511_BASE`/`radiusCql` are still defined (guarding against an accidental drop) while explicitly not asserted as moved; and, checked directly against the files on disk rather than just runtime behaviour, that the old inline declarations are gone from `index.html` and the new file genuinely contains them. Full suite: 22/22 passing.

## v96: v95 reverted — the real request was leaflet.draw's own "Clear All", plus hiding the empty status card

*"No that's not the clear button I was talking about. Revert back to previous version and then remove the 'Clear all' button that's live in the pop out menu from the trash can icon on the left side of the app."* / *"Also that blank container under reveal road should not be there. It should only be present if there is information inside of it."*

v95's diagnosis was wrong. Both times Karim said "the trash can icon on the left," that was the actual location he meant — the draw toolbar (`position: 'topleft'` in `markupDrawControl`, literally top-left of the map, matching the app's own "Use the draw tools (top-left of the map)" copy) — not the Tools drawer's broom-icon "Clear ▾" dropdown, which sits on the *right* edge (v76). That mismatch should have been the tell the first time.

**Revert.** `index.html` restored byte-for-byte from the v93 commit (confirmed via `git diff` that v94 never touched `index.html` at all, so the file's state right before v95 and right after v93 are identical) — bringing back `#clearMenuRow`'s button/dropdown, its CSS, its JS wiring, `clearReportCategory()`, and `setMapActionsNote()` exactly as they were. `tests/verify_v87_report_clear_menu.js` restored from the same commit; the split-out `tests/verify_v87_no_forced_drawer_open.js` and the v95-specific `tests/verify_v95_clear_menu_removed.js` removed, since both existed only to document v95's (wrong) change.

**The actual fix.** The real "Clear all" Karim meant is a native leaflet-draw feature, not anything this app's own HTML defines: clicking the trash-can icon enters delete mode, and leaflet-draw's `L.EditToolbar.getActions()` appends a "Clear All" action to that mode's popout (alongside Save/Cancel) whenever the active handler defines `removeAllLayers()` — which only `L.EditToolbar.Delete` does (confirmed by reading `Leaflet/Leaflet.draw`'s own v1.0.4 source directly, not guessed: [`EditToolbar.js`](https://github.com/Leaflet/Leaflet.draw/blob/v1.0.4/src/edit/EditToolbar.js), [`EditToolbar.Delete.js`](https://github.com/Leaflet/Leaflet.draw/blob/v1.0.4/src/edit/handler/EditToolbar.Delete.js)). One click, no confirmation, wipes every pin/line/area in "My Markup" — a real footgun sitting right next to "Cancel," for data this app has always treated as deliberately not temporary. leaflet-draw ships no option to hide just that one action while keeping Save/Cancel for delete mode, and the library itself loads live from cdnjs rather than living in this repo, so it's patched at runtime instead: `L.EditToolbar.prototype.getActions` is wrapped to filter out the action whose `callback` is the instance's own `_clearAllLayers`, matching by callback identity rather than button text/title (robust against a `L.drawLocal` locale override, which only translates the text, not the callback reference). Guarded the same way the rest of the draw-toolbar block already is (`typeof L.Control.Draw !== 'undefined'`, etc.) plus its own idempotency flag so a duplicate script load can't wrap `getActions` twice.

**Also fixed**, from a follow-up in the same request: the `#mapActionsNote` status card under the three Mapping Functions buttons used to render as a bordered, padded box that just sat there empty until a button was actually pressed. Every real call site already only ever sets it to a real message and nothing clears it back to `""`, so a CSS-only rule was enough — `.td-status:has(#mapActionsNote:empty){display:none}` — no JS changes, no new state to track.

**Verification.** New `tests/verify_v96_leafletdraw_clearall.js` (plain Node, no browser — the suite's `leaflet-stub.js` is a generic chainable Proxy with no real `L.EditToolbar` to patch against, so this rebuilds leaflet-draw v1.0.4's actual `getActions()` verbatim from its own source and runs the literal patch block extracted from `index.html` against it) confirms: the delete-mode handler loses "Clear All" while keeping Save/Cancel, the edit-mode handler is unaffected (it never had "Clear All" to begin with), an *unpatched* copy of the same library function would have shown it (proving the test scenario is real), and a second application of the patch is a no-op. New `tests/verify_v96_status_note_and_clear_restore.js` (Playwright) confirms the Clear ▾ dropdown is back in full (button, all 11 menu items) and that the status card is hidden when `#mapActionsNote` is empty, visible the moment it's populated, and hidden again if it's ever cleared back to empty. `<div>` balance 309/309 (back to its v93/v94 count, confirming the revert undid v95's exact removal with nothing left over). Full suite: 21/21 passing.

## v95: The "Clear ▾" dropdown is gone

*"Clear all markings should be removed"* — clarified, when the phrasing was ambiguous (it could have meant My Markup, or the dropdown, or the pre-v86 "Clear All" button by an old name), as: the popout menu next to the Mapping Functions buttons in the Tools drawer — the broom-icon "Clear ▾" button (v86, reworked in v87) that listed one clear action per location-report section.

Removed entirely rather than disabled or hidden: the `#clearMenuRow` button/dropdown/info-button markup, its `.clear-menu`/`.clear-menu-item` CSS, the open/close and delegated-click JS wiring, `CLEAR_BLURB_HTML`, and `clearReportCategory()` itself. `setMapActionsNote()` — the one-line status helper under `clearReportCategory()`'s own hood — went with it too, once grep confirmed it had no other caller; every other Mapping Functions button already writes to `#mapActionsNote` directly rather than through that wrapper, so the status line itself stays exactly as it was for View Parcels/Shooting Spots/Reveal Road. `overlayCategoryLayers` (the per-category shape-group data structure `clearReportCategory()` called `.clearLayers()` on) predates this feature and is still very much in use by `renderMapOverlays()` — untouched.

`tests/verify_v87_report_clear_menu.js` tested two unrelated things at once (the Clear dropdown's category list, and a separate fix stopping the Tools drawer from force-opening on every automatic Potential Spots sweep) — the first no longer applies, so that file is retired and its still-valid second half lives on as `tests/verify_v87_no_forced_drawer_open.js`.

**Verification.** New `tests/verify_v95_clear_menu_removed.js` confirms every id/class/function/global this feature owned is gone (`clearMenuBtn`, `clearMenu`, `clearMenuRow`, `clearInfoBtn`, `.clear-menu-item`, `clearReportCategory`, `setMapActionsNote`, `CLEAR_BLURB_HTML`, plus a raw-source grep for any leftover string reference), that the three remaining Mapping Functions buttons and their shared status line are untouched, and that `overlayCategoryLayers` still exists and works. `node --check` on the extracted inline script (clean) and a `<div>` tag balance count (305/305, down from 309 by exactly the two divs the dropdown's markup added). Full suite: 20/20 passing.

## v94: Production has been silently building this with Vite since v92 — fixed with a static-deploy vercel.json

*"the markup functions are gone"* / *"The loading bar is also gone"*

Both symptoms traced to the browser console (screenshot from the live site): all three `src/config/*.js` files (`constants.js`, `layers.js`, `icons.js`) were 404ing in production, and the main script died at the `MARKUP_ICONS is not defined` `ReferenceError` this caused. That 404 wasn't a Vercel routing quirk — it's what Vercel is *supposed* to do here. `package.json` + `vite.config.mjs` (written ahead of the module split, per `docs/PLAN.md`, purely so `npm run build` had something to point at eventually) are exactly the fingerprint Vercel's zero-config detection uses to identify a project as Vite — so every deploy since v92 has been running `npm run build` and serving only its `dist/` output, without anyone asking it to. Vite's HTML processing bundles/copies only what it can see via the `type="module"` import graph (or `public/`); a classic `<script src="src/config/whatever.js">` — the entire point of v92's "why classic scripts, not ES modules" finding — is invisible to that graph, so Vite left the `<script>` tag in place but never copied the file it pointed at.

The reason this took two versions to notice: `LAYERS`/`SEARCH_RADIUS_M`/etc. are only ever read from inside function bodies that run on a real user interaction (clicking the map, running a query), so v92 alone 404'd in total silence — nothing throws until a lookup actually runs, and this session's own tests never would have caught it either, since `tests/verify_*.js` load `index.html` straight off disk via `file://`, never through an actual Vercel build. v93 is what made it loud: `MARKUP_ICONS` is read at the *top level* of the main script (populating the icon picker immediately on page load, not inside a function), so the exact same missing-file problem that had already been silently breaking every real location lookup in production for a full version instead threw immediately at load and killed every remaining top-level statement in that one `<script>` block — which is why My Markup *and* the header progress bar (v88) vanished together: both happen to be wired later in the file than the crash point. Put plainly: the live site's actual core functionality — clicking a spot and getting a report — has likely been broken since v92 shipped; v93 just turned an invisible failure into a visible one.

**Fix**: added `vercel.json` at the repo root — `{"framework": null, "buildCommand": null, "installCommand": null, "outputDirectory": "."}` — telling Vercel this is a plain static site: skip framework auto-detection and the build step entirely, serve the repo root byte-for-byte, exactly what `file://` and a plain static server already do (confirmed locally against a real `python3 -m http.server` on Karim's own machine before this diagnosis — no error, everything defined — the discrepancy was 100% the Vercel build step, not the code). `api/bylaw-fetch.js` (v90) is unaffected — Vercel Functions under `api/` are detected independently of the framework/build settings. **This can't be fully verified from inside this session** (no Vercel dashboard access, and this sandbox's `npm install` is still blocked by its own network policy, so the actual `vite build` this diagnosis is built on couldn't be reproduced directly here either) — if Karim's Vercel project has an explicit per-setting *override* toggled on for Framework Preset or Build Command (Project Settings → Build & Development Settings), that dashboard value wins over `vercel.json` and needs clearing there too. After deploying this, worth a real end-to-end check: click a location and confirm a report actually comes back, not just that My Markup's icon picker renders.

## v93: My Markup pins can carry a custom icon — a curated, offline-vendored Lucide set

*"Great that's committed, can you add a feature to the marker system that allows you to give it an icon. I'd like more than just the emoji library, what do you have access to?"*

Emoji were the obvious first answer to "what do you have access to" but a poor fit for an actual glyph library: rendering isn't controllable (font, colour and size vary by OS/browser, so the same pin can look different for Karim than for anyone he shares an export with) and several genuinely relevant glyphs — a proper crosshair, a plain gate — don't exist as emoji at all. Asked to choose between Lucide, Tabler Icons and Font Awesome Free instead, Karim picked Lucide (ISC-licensed — confirmed against `lucide-icons/lucide`'s own `LICENSE` file, permissive, no attribution requirement, safe to vendor inline); asked which categories mattered most for his own markup pins, he picked all four offered: hunting/shooting infrastructure, access & terrain, camp & shelter, and alerts & navigation.

17 icons were curated across those four categories (crosshair, target, camera, eye, binoculars; door-open, fence, car, footprints, droplet; tent, flame, house; triangle-alert, compass, flag, star — "gate" doesn't exist in Lucide, so door-open stands in for it) and vendored as raw path/circle/line markup fetched verbatim from Lucide's own source, in `src/config/icons.js` — a third classic-script config file loaded alongside `constants.js`/`layers.js` (v92), not from a CDN. That's not a style preference: this app is explicitly a standalone file that "works fully offline-of-Claude once downloaded, open directly in any browser," and v92 already found that even an ES-module `import` breaks under `file://`; a CDN-hosted icon set would silently lose every icon the moment the page is opened offline or the CDN is unreachable, turning a cosmetic feature into a broken one for no benefit over vendoring ~9 KB of path data once.

`markupPinIcon(color, icon)` — previously just `(color)` — now optionally draws the chosen icon's `<svg>` (white stroke, for legibility over any of the 8 `MARKUP_COLORS`) centered over the existing colour dot; passing no icon (the default for every pin created before this version, and for lines/areas, which never render one) reproduces the exact plain-dot markup this function always produced. The editor modal gained an icon picker — a "None" button plus one button per icon, grouped under the same four category labels Karim chose, built from `MARKUP_ICONS` data rather than hand-written HTML — that only appears when editing a pin; lines/areas hide the whole section, since nothing would happen if you picked one. The saved/exported item schema gained one additive `icon` field (`markupItemToFeature`/`loadMarkupFromFeatureCollection`), following the same pattern `color`/`name`/`notes` already use: a GeoJSON file exported by any version before v93 has no `icon` property at all, and imports exactly as a plain, icon-less pin, exactly as it always has; an icon id that isn't recognized (a foreign export, or an icon added in a future version and opened in an older one) degrades the same way — no icon, not a thrown error or a silently broken pin.

**Verification.** New test `tests/verify_v93_marker_icons.js` covers: the `MARKUP_ICONS`/`MARKUP_ICONS_BY_ID` data (4 categories, 17 unique icons, cross-checked against each other); the three config `<script src>` tags loading in dependency order before the main inline script; `markupIconSvg()`'s stroke-colour override and its empty-string result for an unknown or absent icon; `markupPinIcon()`'s actual `L.divIcon` markup (captured the same way v89/v91 captured `L.popup` content, since the network-stubbed Leaflet proxy never touches the real DOM) confirming the icon `<svg>` is present when one's set and absent when it isn't; `addMarkupItem()` accepting a valid icon, silently degrading an invalid one to `null`, defaulting to `null` with no icon option at all, and storing (but never rendering) an icon passed for a line; the full editor round-trip — opening a marker's editor pre-selects its current icon and shows the picker, opening a line's editor hides it entirely, clicking a different icon then Save updates the item and re-renders its pin; and, explicitly, the backward/forward-compatibility cases: a feature with no `icon` key at all imports as `icon:null`, and a feature with an unrecognized icon id also degrades to `null`, both without throwing. Full suite: 18/18 passing.

## v92: Module split begins — LAYERS and config moved out, and a real conflict in the original plan got caught first

*"Let's move onto the next step in the roadmap."* — Phase 1's remaining P0: "Split into an organized source tree with a real build step (Vite/esbuild)," per `docs/audit.md`. `docs/PLAN.md` (written in an earlier session) already had a target file structure and order for this, built around real ES `import`/`export` modules bundled by Vite.

Before extracting anything, that plan's core assumption was worth checking against two things this app already has: every `tests/verify_*.js` loads `index.html` via `file://`, and the shipped file is explicitly documented as workable "fully offline-of-Claude once downloaded — open directly in any browser." Testing an ES module's `import` under `file://` confirmed it fails outright — Chromium blocks it with a CORS error ("Cross origin requests are only supported for protocol schemes: chrome, … http, https"), full stop, regardless of how careful the extraction is. Building the module split around real ES modules would have silently broken both of those the moment it shipped, and neither the existing test suite nor `vite build` in CI would ever catch it, since CI never opens the *built* output via `file://` to check.

Plain classic `<script src="...">` tags don't have this restriction (also confirmed directly), and — since every classic script on a page already shares one global scope for top-level `const`/`let`, the same way this file's single inline script has always made every function visible to every generated `onclick="..."` handler — they get the same real, separate, per-concern files without that risk, and without needing to hand-export every function referenced from an inline event handler onto `window` (the extra step true ES modules would have forced). `docs/PLAN.md` is updated with the full reasoning and a corrected plan; `vite.config.mjs`/`package.json` are unaffected, since Vite can still bundle/minify these same classic-script files later as a separate, purely-optional concern.

With that settled, did the plan's own first slice: `SEARCH_RADIUS_M`, `CUTBLOCK_SEARCH_RADIUS_M`, `NEARBY_PARCEL_MAX_FEATURES`, and `CUTBLOCK_START_DATE_CQL` moved to `src/config/constants.js`; the 16-entry `LAYERS` object moved to `src/config/layers.js` (loaded second, since it references those constants by name). Both are loaded via `<script src>` tags in `<head>`, before the main inline script, in that order. The zoom-gate constants (`PARCEL_VIEW_MIN_ZOOM`, `ROAD_VIEW_MIN_ZOOM`, `SPOT_MIN_ZOOM`, `INFRA_MIN_ZOOM`) were deliberately left where they are rather than folded in here — each sits next to a comment explaining why that specific value was picked (one with its own version history), and this codebase leans on that local context more than a generic constants file would give back.

**Verification.** `node --check` on both new files and the main script (clean); `<div>` tag balance unchanged (302/302); confirmed no duplicate `LAYERS`/`SEARCH_RADIUS_M` declarations remain anywhere in `index.html`. New test `tests/verify_v92_config_split.js` confirms the values are defined at runtime, `LAYERS` has all 16 expected keys, the two config `<script src>` tags appear in the DOM in the correct dependency order before the main inline script, and — checked directly against the files on disk, not just runtime behaviour — that the old inline declarations are actually gone and the new files actually contain them. Full suite: 17/17 passing.

## v91: Clicking a cutblock in the report list now always opens exactly that cutblock — never the multi-record chooser

*"When clicking on something from the report list it should never open up the pop up with multiple elements it should take you to exactly what you're clicking on (what's being highlighted)."*

v76 wired a cutblock report-list click through `openNearbyFeaturesPopupAt(latlng)` — the same shared handler a real map click runs — specifically so the list click would open "the identical popup a real map click would produce," rather than the app's own separate info-modal UI. That reasoning was right for *content*, but that function's whole job is to re-test the click point against every registered feature and open a chooser when 2+ match — exactly the ambiguity a list click should never introduce, since picking a specific row already told the app precisely which record is meant. If that cutblock's centroid happened to genuinely overlap something else — another cutblock, a tenure, a road — the list click could open the "N records here" chooser instead of the one record actually clicked, the opposite of the intended behaviour, and worse, inconsistent with the row's own hover-highlight (v76) which always highlights that exact shape and no other.

Fixed by no longer routing the list click through the shared re-test at all: `openCutblockFromList()` now opens a plain Leaflet popup directly with `cutblockPopup(entry)` — the same content `pushClickable()` registered for this cutblock's real on-map shape at draw time, so it's still visually identical to clicking the shape on the map — and brings that shape to front, matching what's already highlighted on hover. The fallback path for an entry with no drawn shape (an oversized/flagged cutblock, which never gets its own map layer) is unchanged — it still opens the info modal, since there's no real on-map popup for that one to mirror.

**Verification.** `node --check` on the extracted script (clean) and a div-tag balance count (302/302, unchanged). A new test, `tests/verify_v91_cutblock_list_click_exact.js`, registers a second feature genuinely overlapping a fake cutblock at the same point (confirming, via the same real hit-test the map itself uses, that this scenario really would trigger the chooser — `realOverlapCount: 2`), then confirms `showCutblockDetail()` opens exactly that cutblock's own popup content — matching `cutblockPopup(entry)` verbatim, containing its own ID, mentioning neither "records here" nor the overlapping tenure — and that the no-mapLayer fallback path still opens the info modal unchanged. Full suite: 16/16 passing.

## v90: Live bylaw fetch now goes through our own serverless proxy, not a public third-party one

*"Let's continue with the roadmap."* — continuing Phase 1 of `docs/audit.md`'s roadmap: "Add a minimal backend to proxy WFS/Nominatim/Overpass/bylaw calls (fixes the CORS-proxy dependency, hides the Mapbox key, unlocks caching/rate-limiting)."

That roadmap line bundles four things together, but only one of them actually needed a backend. The DataBC WFS calls already have their own CORS-free path — GeoServer's JSONP output format (`queryLayer()`'s existing fallback, no change here). Nominatim and Overpass both send permissive CORS headers and are called directly; the app's Overpass fallback to the public CORS proxy is a rarely-hit last resort, not a real dependency. The Mapbox token is a public (`pk.`) token, designed by Mapbox to be shipped client-side — its own fix is a one-click domain restriction in the Mapbox account dashboard (Phase 1's own P1 item), not a code change or a backend. The one piece with a genuine, unmitigated third-party dependency was the live bylaw fetch: municipal sites don't send CORS headers and there's no JSONP equivalent for arbitrary PDF/HTML hosting, so it was falling straight through to `api.allorigins.win` — a free, keyless, public proxy neither Karim nor this app controls, for every jurisdiction it's ever looked up.

Added `api/bylaw-fetch.js`, a small Vercel serverless function (zero dependencies — just the platform's own Node runtime and native `fetch`) that fetches a bylaw document server-side and returns it, deployed alongside `index.html` on the same Vercel project set up for hosting. It's deliberately not a general-purpose open proxy: it only forwards to the exact hosts already listed in `BYLAW_SOURCES` (`www.chilliwack.com`, `laws.abbotsford.ca`, `apps.kelowna.ca`), refuses non-https targets, caps response size, and sets cache headers (`s-maxage=86400, stale-while-revalidate=604800`) since these documents change rarely. `fetchBylawSource()` now tries, in order: the direct fetch (unchanged), then — only when the page is actually served over http/https, not opened as a local file — this new endpoint, then the old public proxy as a last-resort fallback either way. That last tier stays in place on purpose: it keeps the flat, offline-friendly `index.html` file working for local testing (where a relative `/api/...` call has nothing to answer), and it's a safety net if the new function itself is ever down.

**Verification.** `node --check` on the extracted script (clean) and a div-tag balance count (302/302, unchanged). Two new tests: `tests/verify_v90_bylaw_proxy.js`, a plain Node test (no browser) that calls `api/bylaw-fetch.js`'s handler directly with mock req/res objects and a stubbed `fetch`, covering missing/malformed/disallowed/non-https URLs, a successful proxy with the right headers, an upstream error, a network failure, and an oversized-response guard; and `tests/verify_v90_fetchBylawSource_fallback.js`, a Playwright test that (since the behaviour under test differs between `file://` and a real origin) spins up a throwaway local http server to serve the repo alongside the usual `file://` load, confirming the new endpoint is tried and used when reachable over http, that a failing new-endpoint call still falls through to the old public proxy, and that opening the page as a local file skips the new endpoint entirely and goes straight to the public proxy. Full suite: 15/15 passing.

## v89: Hovering a row in the map-click chooser popup highlights that record on the map

*"Also In the list in the pop up when clicking on a spot on that map after its produced results, it should highlight the element on the map that your mouse is hovering over in the list."*

The report already had this for one specific case — hovering a cutblock's row in the location report's own cutblock list restyles that cutblock's shape on the map (v76). What Karim was asking for here is the same idea applied to a different popup entirely: the "N records here" chooser (v79) that opens when a *map click itself* lands where two or more registered shapes overlap or sit right next to each other — a tenure and a cutblock, a road and a park, any mix, up to ~20 different layer types feeding it. Hovering any row in that list now highlights its shape too, before you commit to clicking "details ›" on it.

The cutblock-list version works by restyling the real Leaflet layer already on the map and reverting it to its own known style (`cutblockStyle()`) on mouseout — that only works because every cutblock shares one revert style. The chooser popup has no such single revert style to fall back to: its rows can be a tenure polygon, a woodlot boundary, a recreation point marker, a road line, a park fill — each drawn with its own distinct native styling in `renderMapOverlays()`, and the chooser itself doesn't keep a live reference to those Leaflet layers, only the bare GeoJSON geometry each was registered with (`pushClickable()`'s stored `geometry`, same format `L.geoJSON()` accepts directly). Threading a live layer reference and a per-type revert style through all ~20 `pushClickable()` call sites just to support a hover effect wasn't worth the coupling, so this instead draws a *fresh* highlight shape from that stored geometry into its own dedicated layer group (`chooserHighlightLayer`) on hover, and simply clears that group on mouseout — one code path works for every layer type since it never touches the original shape at all. A `popupclose` listener clears it too, as a safety net in case the popup closes without a mouseout ever firing (e.g. clicking elsewhere on the map, or pressing Escape).

**Verification.** `node --check` on the extracted script (clean) and a div-tag balance count (302/302, unchanged). A new network-stubbed Playwright test (`tests/verify_v89_chooser_hover_highlight.js`) confirms `highlightNearbyMatch()`/`clearNearbyMatchHighlight()` exist and don't throw for both a polygon and a point match (exercising both the plain-style and `pointToLayer` branches), that an out-of-range index is a harmless no-op, and — capturing the exact HTML string `openNearbyFeaturesPopupAt()` builds (the stubbed `L.popup()` never touches the real DOM, so this wraps `window.L.popup` for the one call rather than trying to read the popup back out of the page) — that a real two-match chooser popup's row markup actually wires in `onmouseover="highlightNearbyMatch(i)"` and `onmouseout="clearNearbyMatchHighlight()"`, plus that firing `popupclose` doesn't throw. Full suite: 13/13 passing.

## v88: Potential Spots progress bar moved into the header
*"Let's move the loading bar to the header and push that to git once it's done."*

The Potential Spots progress bar (percent readout + the "Fetching X…" live label) lived in the Tools drawer's Mapping Functions group since v71. As of v87 that drawer is no longer force-opened for the automatic per-click sweep — which is correct (Karim didn't want the drawer popping open unprompted), but it meant the bar itself was now only ever visible when the drawer happened to already be open, i.e. effectively never for the automatic sweep. Moving it to the header puts it somewhere always visible regardless of drawer state, for every trigger.

New home: directly under the header's search bar, in the same column as `#searchStatus` ("No location selected yet." / "Checking …") — the two now share one wrapper (`.tb-status-wrap`) so they stack together instead of each needing its own absolute-position guess. Restyled for the header's dark, translucent background (light track/fill/text) instead of the Tools drawer's light card background, but otherwise unchanged: same element ids (`spotsProgress`/`spotsProgressFill`/`spotsProgressPct`/`spotsProgressLabel`), so `startSpotsProgress()`/`trackSpotsProgress()`/`endSpotsProgress()` needed zero changes — this was a markup/CSS move, not a behavioural one. The Tools drawer keeps its status note (`#mapActionsNote`) since that's shared by View Parcels/Reveal Road too, not just Shooting Spots.

**Verification.** `node --check` on the extracted script (clean) and a div-tag balance count (302/302). A new network-stubbed Playwright test (`tests/verify_v88_header_progress_bar.js`) confirms the bar now lives inside `#topBar`/`.tb-status-wrap` and not `#toolsDrawer`, starts hidden, shares its wrapper with `#searchStatus`, and that the old `.spots-progress` class is gone from the page entirely; `startSpotsProgress()`/`trackSpotsProgress()`/`endSpotsProgress()` were exercised end-to-end (by id, same as production code) to confirm the show → percent-update → hide lifecycle still works unchanged. Screenshots at both desktop (1400px) and mobile (600px) widths confirmed the bar renders cleanly in both the centred-header and stacked-mobile layouts. Full suite: 12/12 passing.

## v87: Clear dropdown corrected to list the report's own sections, plus the Potential Spots loading bar no longer pops the drawer open on its own
*"Sorry when I said the clear button should have one for every category I didn't mean the category on the mapping functions I meant the categories in the report. Also the loading bar should not show up until a function is clicked. When it's finished loading it should disappear and then come back anytime another function is clicked that requires loading."*

Two fixes from the same message.

**1) The Clear dropdown's categories.** v86 read "one for every category" as the three Mapping Functions buttons (View Parcels/Shooting Spots/Reveal Road) plus a fourth catch-all for the pin. Karim clarified he meant the single-click location report's own sections instead — the 11 cards `renderReport()` builds (Parcel, Municipal Boundary, Bylaw Lookup, Park/Protected Area, Wildlife Management Area, Recreation Sites & Trails, Motor Vehicle Closures, Forestry Activity, Crown Land Tenures, Forest Tenure/Woodlot, Road Access).

Implementation: `renderMapOverlays()` used to draw every one of those sections' shapes into one shared `overlayLayer`, and tag every `currentClickableFeatures` entry it pushed with one shared `'overlay'` source (v83) — fine for resetting the whole thing on a new click, but with no way to touch just one section. As of v87 it instead creates one child `L.layerGroup()` per section (`overlayCategoryLayers`, keyed the same as `pushCard()`'s own keys) at the top of every call, draws each section into its own group, and tags each section's `pushClickable()` calls with its own source (`'overlay:parcel'`, `'overlay:road'`, etc.) instead of one shared `'overlay'`. `clearReportCategory(key, label)` then does exactly three things for one key: `overlayCategoryLayers[key].clearLayers()`, drop that key's `currentClickableFeatures` entries, and remove `#card-<key>` (every `cardHTML()` call already writes that id) from the live report — nothing else on the map or in the report is touched, and the pin/marker is never referenced at all. The dropdown itself is now generated from a static 11-item list with a `data-key` per button and one delegated click handler, instead of the four individually-wired buttons v86 had.

`bylaw` is the one report card with no map shape of its own (a plain text/link card, only shown when a jurisdiction was actually found) — it's not in `overlayCategoryLayers`, and its clear action is a no-op on the map side, just dropping its card if present.

**2) The Potential Spots progress bar forcing the Tools drawer open.** v72 added an unconditional `openToolsDrawer()` call at the top of `runPotentialSpotsSearch()`, reasoning that otherwise the progress bar sat invisible behind the (then still left-edge, later right-edge) drawer's collapsed panel for every location pick that didn't go through the "Shooting Spots" button itself — since v69/v70, *every* location pick (a plain map click, dragging the pin, search, typed coordinates) triggers an automatic 2&nbsp;km radius sweep through this same shared function, not just the button. That meant the drawer — and the loading bar inside it — popped open on every single map click, whether or not Karim had touched anything in it, which is exactly what he reported as "showing up on its own."

Root cause understood, not just the symptom: the fix is to remove that forced-open call, not to hide the bar differently. `openToolsDrawer()` is now entirely unused (the Tools tab's own click handler already opens the drawer inline) and was deleted along with it, rather than left as dead code. The progress bar's own show/hide lifecycle (`startSpotsProgress()`/`endSpotsProgress()` toggling `#spotsProgress`'s `hidden` attribute) was never the problem and is unchanged — it still appears exactly while a run is in flight and disappears the moment it finishes, for every trigger. The only behavioural change: it's no longer force-surfaced by opening the drawer for you. If the Tools drawer happens to already be open (you opened it, or you just pressed a button inside it), you see it update normally; if it's closed, an automatic background sweep now runs invisibly, the same way it would have before v72 ever existed.
