**Verification.** `node --check` on the extracted script (clean) and a div-tag balance count (302/302). A new network-stubbed Playwright test (`tests/verify_v87_report_clear_menu.js`, replacing `verify_v86_clear_menu.js`) confirms: the dropdown's 11 `data-key` items match the report's own category keys exactly, and the old per-mapping-function buttons are gone; a faked report with two categories (tenures, park) drawn via the real `renderMapOverlays()`/`renderReport()` shows both cards and both categories' chooser entries present, then clearing "tenures" removes its card and chooser entries and calls `clearLayers()` on its own layer group while "park" (and the pin) are provably untouched (spied, since the network-stubbed Leaflet's generic Proxy has no real per-group shape tracking to assert counts against directly — see the test's own comment); clearing a category with nothing to clear (bylaw, not rendered in the fake report) is a harmless no-op; and `openToolsDrawer` no longer exists as a function at all, with `runPotentialSpotsSearch()`'s own source confirmed not to reference it. Full suite: 11/11 passing.

## v86: "Clear All"/"Clear Potential Spots" replaced with a single "Clear ▾" dropdown, one action per category
*"Lets change the clear function. I want to remove both clear all and clear shooting spots. Instead I want a clear button that drops down and has a list of individual categories you can clear. There should be one for each Category."*

Through v85 the Mapping Functions group had two separate clear controls: "Clear Potential Spots" (v53, hidden until Shooting Spots actually drew something) and "Clear All" (v82, always visible, reset all four categories — View Parcels, Shooting Spots, Reveal Road, the selected location — in one press). Karim asked for both to go, replaced by one dropdown listing an individual clear action per category.

The four categories map directly onto the `source` tags the shared click-chooser mechanism already uses per shape (`'parcelview'`, `'spots'`, `'roadview'`, `'overlay'` — see the v83 section above), so each new clear function is a narrow, symmetrical slice of what `clearAllTemporary()` used to do all at once:
- `clearViewParcels()` — clears `parcelViewLayer` + `parcelViewFeatures`, and only the `'parcelview'`-tagged `currentClickableFeatures` entries.
- `clearPotentialSpots()` — unchanged in substance (still clears `spotLayer`, the spot/gap/restricted detail arrays, and the `'spots'`-tagged chooser entries); only the now-removed always-hidden-until-first-run button toggle went away, since the dropdown item has no reason to hide itself.
- `clearRevealRoad()` — clears `roadViewLayer` and the `'roadview'`-tagged chooser entries.
- `clearSelectedLocation()` — clears `overlayLayer`, removes the pin (`marker`), the `'overlay'`-tagged chooser entries, `currentNearbyMatches`/`currentCutOpenings`, and resets the report drawer/bottom-panel summary/search status back to their empty-state text — the same "run `runLookup()`'s setup in reverse" `clearAllTemporary()` already did for this part, just now standalone.

None of the four ever reference `drawnItems`/`markupItems`/`localStorage` — Karim's own drawn markup was never in scope for the old buttons and stays out of scope for all four new actions, same as always.

UI: the two old buttons are replaced by one "🧹 Clear ▾" trigger (same `.ma-btn` styling as every other Mapping Functions button) that opens a small floating menu of four items, styled as a narrower variant of the header search bar's existing `.tb-search-results` dropdown pattern (same floating panel look — border, radius, shadow — toggled via the `hidden` attribute, closes on an outside click or after any item runs). The "i" info button now opens a single blurb explaining the dropdown instead of the old "Clear All" description.

**Verification.** `node --check` on the extracted script (clean) and a div-tag balance count (302/302). A new network-stubbed Playwright test (`tests/verify_v86_clear_menu.js`) confirms: the old `clearAllBtn`/`clearSpotsBtn`/`clearAllInfoBtn` elements are gone and the new trigger/menu/four items exist; the menu opens on trigger click and closes on an outside click; and — the actual behavioural point of the redesign — clearing one category (faked state seeded in all four at once) removes only that category's layer contents and `currentClickableFeatures` entries while the other three categories' entries survive untouched, ending with `clearSelectedLocation()` correctly resetting the pin/report/bottom-panel/search-status and a byte-for-byte-unchanged `markupItems` throughout. Full suite: 11/11 passing.

## v85: "Reveal Road" was only ever showing BC's general public road network, never forest service/branch roads
*"Reveal roads is not working properly it's only revealing main roads. This should be revealing smaller roads such as branches and other forestry roads. Really all the roads in the view."*

Root cause: Reveal Road (`revealAllRoads()`) only ever queried one of BC's two road datasets — the Digital Road Atlas (DRA), which is the *general public* road network (highways, arterials, local streets). Forest service and resource roads — including the branches Karim was specifically looking for — live in a separate government dataset, FTEN Road Section Lines (`WHSE_FOREST_TENURE.FTEN_ROAD_SECTION_LINES_SVW`, `LAYERS.road`). That second layer isn't new to this app — it's what the single-click report's own "Road access" card has queried and drawn since long before Reveal Road existed — Reveal Road itself simply never queried it. Not a rendering bug or a styling bug: the forestry roads were never being asked for in the first place, so "only main roads" was actually the DRA-only query working exactly as written, just against the wrong assumption about what "all the roads in view" required.

Fixed by having `revealAllRoads()` fire both queries in parallel against the current viewport (`Promise.all`, same zoom-gated, on-demand shape as before) and draw whichever succeed — DRA roads via the existing `classifyDraRoads()`/`renderNearbyRoads()` path unchanged, forest service roads via a new `renderForestServiceRoads()`. That function isn't new logic either: it's the single-click report's own forest-service-road drawing (colour, popup, click registration) pulled out of `renderMapOverlays()` into a shared function, so Reveal Road draws forest service roads exactly the way the click report always has — tan for active, grey for retired — rather than inventing a second, slightly-different way to show the same data. The status note now reports both layers together and names either one specifically if it fails or hits its feature cap, instead of a single undifferentiated "N roads" count.

**Verification.** `node --check` (clean) and div-balance (301/301, unchanged). A new test, `verify_v85_road_fsr_merge.js`, confirms `revealAllRoads()`'s own source now queries both `LAYERS.draRoad` and `LAYERS.road` in parallel, that the extracted `renderForestServiceRoads()` correctly registers a clickable entry from real GeoJSON, and that the single-click report's own road block now delegates to that same shared function rather than a second copy of the drawing logic. The full regression suite (11 scripts total, all prior ones plus this one) passes with no console/page errors.

## v84: "Reveal Road" wasn't highlighting anything — a zoom-gate typo three notches tighter than intended
*"Roads are not being highlighted with the reveal road function"*

Reveal Road's own zoom gate, `ROAD_VIEW_MIN_ZOOM`, was set to 11 — a "look at one street" scale. Its own code comment said it should be "one notch tighter than `PARCEL_VIEW_MIN_ZOOM`" (8), i.e. 9 — and 9 is exactly what Shooting Spots' equivalent gate, `SPOT_MIN_ZOOM`, already uses, for that identical stated reason. 11 never matched either the comment sitting directly above it or either sibling button's own threshold; it was a plain authoring slip from v82; nothing else about it was reasoned deliberately. In practice this meant View Parcels and Shooting Spots both ran fine at a normal "scanning a hunting/shooting area" zoom (9 or 10), while Reveal Road silently declined and only left a small "Zoom in further before revealing roads" note — easy to miss, especially right after two other buttons on the same row had just worked. Fixed by changing `ROAD_VIEW_MIN_ZOOM` from 11 to 9, matching the comment's own stated intent and `SPOT_MIN_ZOOM` exactly, so all three view-scoped buttons now behave consistently at the same normal zoom range.

**Verification.** `node --check` (clean) and div-balance (301/301, unchanged — a one-constant fix). The full existing regression suite (9 scripts: v79, v81, both v82 tenure scripts, v82 cutblock-buffer, v82 UI, v82 road-core, v82 Clear All, v83) was re-run afterward with zero console/page errors and no changed results, confirming the fix didn't touch anything else. The actual live-query path (does BC's WFS server return road features once the zoom gate is cleared) still can't be exercised from this sandbox — no network route to openmaps.gov.bc.ca here — so if roads still don't appear once you're zoomed to 9+, that's the next thing to check for; but the gate itself, which was silently blocking every normal-zoom attempt regardless of what the server would have returned, is confirmed fixed.

## v83: forestry flagged red instead of amber, and the real fix for "click only shows advisories" (Shooting Spots/View Parcels/Reveal Road were never wired into the shared chooser at all)
Two bug reports, sent with screenshots.

*"This should flag forestry as red and it's only making it a caution (see attached)"*

*"The problem still persists (see second attached). When I click there it should bring up the info on the cut block under it and the road near it as designed but instead it only brings up the advisories."*

**1) Forestry-in-active-cutblock-buffer flag changed from amber to red.** When v81 added this flag, the app's own design judgment kept it amber, reasoning it should match the app's established convention (red = a legal exclusion, amber = a non-exclusionary advisory) since being inside the 400m buffer isn't against any regulation. Karim corrected this directly — the point of the flag is that active logging traffic should be expected there, which is a real hazard, not a background advisory, and he wants it treated with the same weight as any other red flag. Changed `addReason('amber', …)` to `addReason('red', …)`, updated the pill and per-row colour to the app's canonical red (`#9a2a20`, matching MVPR's), and dropped the old "non-exclusionary… not a legal restriction" framing from the copy since it no longer applies. This overrides the app's own prior judgment at Karim's explicit instruction — worth remembering if a future round is tempted to "clean up" the severity back to amber for consistency with the exclusion/advisory convention elsewhere; don't, this was a deliberate correction, not an oversight.

**2) The real root cause of "click only shows advisories" — a second, entirely separate popup system that the v79-v81 chooser fixes never touched.** Karim's second report showed the same symptom v81 was supposed to have fixed (a click landing on a cutblock/road, showing only the generic advisory list instead of that feature's own detail) still happening in a different spot. Re-diagnosing rather than assuming v81's fix was simply incomplete: v81's fix (giving `fill:false` shapes a small real fill, and adding boundary-line tolerance to polygon hit-testing) was correct and necessary, but it only applies to the single-click report's own drawn shapes (`overlayLayer`) — the ones registered via `pushClickable()` into the shared `currentClickableFeatures` array that `openNearbyFeaturesPopupAt()` reads from. It turns out Shooting Spots' entire `spotLayer` (gap squares, spot squares, restricted squares, active-cutblock outlines, and its own road draw) and View Parcels'/Reveal Road's `parcelViewLayer`/`roadViewLayer` had *never* used that mechanism at all — since the day each was built, they attached their own private `.bindPopup(...)` directly to each shape instead, a second, parallel click/popup system running alongside the v79 chooser, completely invisible to it. So a click at a point covered by both a Shooting Spots gap square (with its 2 advisories) and, underneath it, a cutblock outline or a road: Leaflet delivers the click to whichever shape happens to be on top of the DOM stacking order, and since the gap square was `bindPopup`-based it just showed its own popup directly — the cutblock and road never got a chance to be offered as alternatives, because they were never in the same list to begin with. This was distinct from, and deeper than, both the v80 diagnosis (parcels blocking clicks via z-order/chooser-membership) and the v81 diagnosis (unfilled shapes not registering clicks) — both of those were real, correctly-fixed bugs, just for a system that turned out to be only half the picture.

Fixed by extending the same shared-chooser mechanism to cover all four systems, with one addition needed to make that safe: `renderMapOverlays()` (the click report), `runPotentialSpotsSearch()` (Shooting Spots), `drawParcelViewLayer()` (View Parcels), and `revealAllRoads()` (Reveal Road) all run independently — Shooting Spots' 2km auto-sweep fires in parallel with the click report on every location pick (v69/v70), and View Parcels/Reveal Road can be run and re-run at any time against whatever's currently drawn. A single shared array with no way to tell entries apart meant any one of these clearing and rebuilding its own shapes would wipe out every other system's entries too. Added a `source` tag: `pushClickable()` now stamps each entry with a module-level `currentPushSource`, and a new `resetClickableSource(source)` helper clears only that source's own entries before a redraw and sets `currentPushSource` for what follows — so `resetClickableSource('overlay')`, `resetClickableSource('spots')`, `resetClickableSource('parcelview')`, and `resetClickableSource('roadview')` each own their own slice of the array without touching anyone else's. Every shape in all four systems was converted from its private `.bindPopup(...)` to the shared `.on('click', e => openNearbyFeaturesPopupAt(e.latlng))` handler plus a `pushClickable(...)` registration carrying that shape's real geometry, icon, and popup content — covering Shooting Spots' whole-parcel and mixed-parcel allowed/restricted squares, its gap squares, its active-cutblock outlines (`renderActiveCutblockOutlines()`), its road draw (`renderNearbyRoads()`, also shared by Reveal Road), View Parcels' parcels (`drawParcelViewLayer()`), and Reveal Road's own call into `renderNearbyRoads()`. The active-cutblock *buffer* squares (the 400m halo, not the cutblock outline itself) were deliberately left alone — they're `interactive:false`, a visual-only halo, so clicks already pass straight through to whatever's underneath and there was nothing to register.

**Verification.** `node --check` on the extracted script (clean) and a div-tag balance count (301/301, unchanged — this was a JS-only fix). A new Playwright script, `verify_v83.js`, confirmed: an 'overlay' entry and a 'spots' entry registered at the same point both survive a re-run of `renderMapOverlays()` (proving one source's reset no longer wipes another's), and a click at that point now returns `matchCount: 2` with `matchSources: ["overlay", "spots"]` — the actual merge Karim asked for; `drawParcelViewLayer()` and `renderNearbyRoads()` (via Reveal Road's `resetClickableSource('roadview')`) each register a correctly-tagged entry; `renderActiveCutblockOutlines()` registers a `'spots'`-tagged entry with the 🪓 icon; and `renderReport()` with an active cutblock at the picked point now produces a Cutblocks card with the `card-flag-red` class and a red "Forestry" flag chip, not amber. The full pre-existing regression suite (verify_v79 through verify_v82, all 8 scripts) was re-run afterward and still passes with no errors, confirming the chooser refactor introduced no regressions to any previously-verified click behaviour.

## v82: two emoji removed, a "Reveal Road" button, and a one-press "Clear All"
Karim's requests, verbatim (four separate, short requests sent together):

*"Remove the tools emoji from the tab and from the top of the expanded tool menu"*

*"Remove emoji from search bar"*

*"Create a function called 'Reveal road' that highlights all the roads in the view"*

*"Create a 'Clear all' button that clears all temporary elements on the map (this does not include drawn lines, shapes or anything else manually annotated)."*

**1) Tools tab/panel emoji removed.** The 🛠️ hammer-and-wrench dropped from both the collapsed drawer tab (`#toolsDrawerTab`) and the expanded panel's own header (`.td-head h2`) — plain "Tools" in both places now. The one other place that emoji appeared, the "View Parcels" info blurb's reference to "the 🛠️ Tools panel," was updated to match.

**2) Search bar emoji removed.** The 🔎 magnifying glass sitting in `.tb-search` (`.tb-search-icon`) was dropped along with its now-unused CSS rule, rather than left as an empty, functionless span — the search box's own left padding already gives the input room, so nothing else needed to change to keep it looking right.

**3) "Reveal Road" — a new button that highlights every road in the current view, independent of Shooting Spots.** BC's Digital Road Atlas (DRA) roads were already being drawn on the map, but only as a side effect of running Shooting Spots (v58) — there was no way to see them on their own. Added a genuinely new function, `revealAllRoads()`, following the exact same on-demand, view-scoped shape `revealAllParcels()` ("View Parcels") already established: zoom-gated (`ROAD_VIEW_MIN_ZOOM = 11`, one notch tighter than View Parcels' 8 since BC's public road network is far denser than parcels at a wide zoom), an `INTERSECTS` query against the current viewport (`viewportBboxCql`, the same helper View Parcels uses), disable-while-loading, and a status note reporting how many roads were drawn once done. Rather than duplicate the road classification logic already living inside `runPotentialSpotsSearch()` (which buffer distance applies to a given road, `routeSpecialInfo()` against the named-highway list), that logic was pulled out into its own reusable `classifyDraRoads()` function and both callers now share it — a real de-duplication, not just new code bolted alongside old. Drawing itself reuses `renderNearbyRoads()`/`draRoadStyle()`/`draRoadTooltip()` unchanged, so a road revealed this way looks and behaves identically to one Shooting Spots would have drawn — same rust/orange-vs-slate colour coding, same hover tooltip. Lives in its own `roadViewLayer` layer group so it can be shown or cleared independently of View Parcels and Shooting Spots.

**4) "Clear All" — one button that resets every temporary map element, without touching personal markup.** Previously, fully decluttering the map after using several of these features meant separately pressing "Clear Potential Spots," then picking a location again (or reloading) just to also drop the current pin/highlight/report — and there was no way at all to clear View Parcels or the new Reveal Road layer short of panning away. Added `clearAllTemporary()`, wired to a new always-visible "🧹 Clear All" button in the same Mapping Functions group: clears `parcelViewLayer` (View Parcels) and its `parcelViewFeatures` cache, clears `roadViewLayer` (Reveal Road), calls the existing `clearPotentialSpots()` for Shooting Spots (reusing its own detail-array reset and progress-bar/button cleanup rather than re-implementing it), then clears the currently selected location itself — removes the pin (`marker`), clears its click-report highlight (`overlayLayer`), resets the report drawer back to its original "Pick a location…" placeholder and collapses it, resets the header's status line back to "No location selected yet.," and clears the now-stale `currentClickableFeatures`/`currentNearbyMatches`/`currentCutOpenings` chooser/detail state — essentially running `runLookup()`'s own setup in reverse. Karim was explicit that this should not touch his own drawn markup (pins/lines/areas from the draw toolbar), which lives in a completely separate layer and its own `localStorage`-backed store (see "custom map markup" further down) — `clearAllTemporary()` never references `drawnItems`/`markupItems` at all, so there was nothing to accidentally include even by omission.

**Verification.** `node --check` on the extracted script (clean) and a div-tag balance count (301/301, consistent with the new markup added). Network-stubbed Playwright runs confirmed: both emoji genuinely gone from the DOM (`#toolsDrawerTab`/`.td-head h2` text, `.tb-search` innerHTML no longer containing 🔎); `classifyDraRoads()` correctly assigning the special highway buffer only to a route/coordinate actually inside its named clip window (Hwy 99 → 600m) and the plain 25m default otherwise, with `renderNearbyRoads()` drawing the result into `roadViewLayer` without error; and a full `clearAllTemporary()` run against a faked "selected location + drawn reveal layers + existing markup" state confirming the marker, report, bottom-drawer state, search status, and chooser/detail arrays all reset correctly, the `clearSpotsBtn`/progress bar hide again, the unified note text is set, and — the one Karim was explicit about — the markup store (`markupItems`) is byte-for-byte unchanged before and after.

## v81: the real fix for clicks under parcels (v80's diagnosis was wrong), a polygon boundary-line click fix, forestry flagged inside the active-cutblock buffer, and Crown tenure seasonal closures researched
Karim's requests, verbatim (four separate messages, the last two arriving mid-turn while earlier ones were still being worked):

*"I still am unable to click anything under a parcel. It only brings up the advisory pop up. Let's just merge the advisory pop up into the item list feature when a small area is clicked."*

*"When clicking on crown land tenure lines o the map it shows "No details available for this point". It should open the details for the crown land tenure"*

*"Forestry should be flagged when the pin is within the red radius"*

*"Also with land tenures let's pull up any closure periods that would apply to them. For example in squamish a heli ski tenure is pulled and that one is closed dec 1st to april 1st but doesn't show"*

**1) v80's "fix" for parcels blocking clicks didn't work because it was treating the wrong mechanism — the real cause was a genuine SVG hit-testing gap, not chooser membership.** v80 had assumed a parcel's geometry, since it almost always underlies whatever smaller feature sits on it, was winning the shared click chooser simply by being registered as a co-equal candidate — and "fixed" that by removing parcel/municipality/park from `currentClickableFeatures` entirely. Karim's report proved that diagnosis wrong: the click still only ever brought up the parcel's own advisory popup, tenure/cutblock/etc. underneath never came up at all. Re-investigated from scratch and found the actual cause: tenure, woodlot, WMA, and municipality polygons are drawn with `style:{..., fill:false}` (outline only, no interior fill at all). Per the SVG spec's `pointer-events:visiblePainted` behaviour (Leaflet's default), a shape with no fill — `fill:none` or `fill-opacity:0` — never registers a click on its interior; only a click that lands exactly on the painted stroke line does. So a click anywhere inside one of these shapes was *always* being delivered to whatever real-filled shape sat underneath (almost always the parcel), completely independent of chooser logic, both before v79 and after v80's change. Fixed at the actual root: gave all four layers a small non-zero `fillOpacity:0.03` — visually still near-invisible, but per the same spec now genuinely "painted" and therefore hit-testable across their whole interior — and restored parcel/municipality/park to the shared `currentClickableFeatures` chooser exactly as they were in v79, per Karim's explicit instruction to merge the advisory popup into the shared item-list feature rather than have it stand apart. This is not a reversion to v79's design being blamed unfairly in v80 — v79's design was never actually the problem; the missing fill was. Verified via Playwright: a deliberately-overlapping parcel + municipality + tenure + cutblock square now correctly produces a 4-way chooser, and a spot with only a parcel still resolves cleanly to its own popup.

**2) Clicking a Crown tenure's own visible dashed boundary line showed "No details available for this point" instead of the tenure — a related but distinct hit-testing gap from (1), found from Karim's very next report.** The shared click handler's polygon test (`featureNearPoint`) used a strict `pointInGeometry` contains-check with no tolerance at all — a real, deliberate design choice when it was originally written (a polygon interior doesn't need pixel tolerance the way a thin line does). But (1) above means these shapes are now genuinely near-invisible except for their dashed outline, so a user naturally clicks *on that visible line* rather than blindly into the interior — and a click on a curve rendered a couple of screen pixels wide will often land a hair outside the mathematically exact polygon boundary, which the strict test rejected outright. Fixed by reusing `pointToGeomMeters()` (already built for the cutblock-buffer distance math below — returns 0 if the point is inside, otherwise the real distance to the boundary) in place of the bare contains-test, checked against the same screen-pixel click tolerance the line/point cases already use. A click dead-center still matches (distance 0), a click a few meters outside the line now also matches (within tolerance), and a click genuinely far away still correctly misses — verified directly against `featureNearPoint()` with points at 0m, ~4m, and ~50m from a test polygon's edge.

**3) The single-click report's Forestry activity card now flags specifically when the pin falls inside the mapped 400m active-cutblock buffer zone, not just the generic "nearby" count.** Previously this card's only severity logic was a flat amber "N forestry opening(s)/block(s) recorded within 2 km" regardless of how close or what state any of them were in — the much more specific "you're standing inside the shaded active-logging buffer zone" fact that Potential Spots already computes and draws on the map (`CUTBLOCK_WARN_DISTANCE_M` = 400m, active meaning FTEN `LIFE_CYCLE_STATUS_CODE = ACTIVE`, oversized/implausible records excluded — the exact same definition and constant that feature has used since v50) was never checked for the single picked point. Added that check to the Cutblocks report section using `pointToGeomMeters()` against each active, non-oversized cutblock's real geometry: a hit adds a distinct pill (`within 400m of active logging`) and its own amber reason, and each matching row in the card's own list gets a `⚠ within 400m buffer` marker so it's clear which specific record triggered it. Kept as amber, not red — same "non-exclusionary, check for logging traffic" framing as the map's own buffer-zone legend entry and every other cutblock flag on this card; it isn't a legal access restriction, just a stronger, more specific version of the same advisory. Verified: a pin inside an active cutblock's own geometry flags; the same cutblock 2km+ away doesn't; a retired (non-active) cutblock at the same spot doesn't; an oversized active cutblock is correctly excluded, matching Potential Spots' own exclusion.

**4) Researched whether BC publishes structured seasonal closure-period data for Crown tenures (Karim's example: a Squamish heli-ski tenure closed Dec 1–Apr 1) — it doesn't, so this stays a caveat rather than an automated check.** Fetched the live published schema for `WHSE_TANTALIS.TA_CROWN_TENURES_SVW` directly (its `DescribeFeatureType`, 24 fields) rather than relying on training-data recall: no season, closure-period, or access-window field exists at all — only `TENURE_EXPIRY`, a one-time hard end date, nothing recurring. Checked BC's own Crown land closures guidance next: it confirms these conditions live in each tenure's own licence/permit document (Ministerial Orders, PDFs), reachable only by contacting FrontCounter BC about a specific file — not published anywhere as open geospatial or tabular data. Same "researched, no data source available" outcome as the v78 Wildlife Act no-shooting-area finding. Rather than silently drop the request, added an honest, gated caveat in three places: the tenure popup, the "Crown land tenures" checklist info blurb, and the report card itself — each explains the data gap plainly, and for a tenure whose purpose is the kind that often does carry a season (recreation, ski/cat-ski, guide-outfitting, grazing, trapline, apiary) it specifically flags that this one is worth confirming directly with FrontCounter BC or the tenure holder, instead of a generic note attached to every tenure regardless of purpose. A standing backlog item was added so this gets revisited if BC ever publishes structured tenure-condition data.

**Verification:** `node --check` on the extracted script (clean), a div-tag balance count (294 open / 294 close, consistent with the new markup added), and network-stubbed Playwright runs covering all four changes: the 4-way overlap chooser and single-parcel-only case for (1); direct `featureNearPoint()` distance tests at three tolerances for (2); the four active-cutblock-buffer scenarios (inside/far/retired/oversized) for (3); and the tenure-popup and report-card caveat wording, gated correctly on purpose, for (4). No console or page errors in any run.

## v80: parcels opted back out of the shared click chooser, the verdict's flag chips reorganized into 3 columns, and a real latent CSS bug found and fixed along the way
Karim's requests, verbatim (two separate messages):

*"It looks like the parcels are blocking the ability to click on elements under that let's fix that"*

*"Also for the items list at the top of the report lets organize them so it's 3 collumns, first one will be greens second yellows third reds"*

**1) Parcels (and municipality/park) opted back out of the v79 shared click chooser.** Root cause: v79 registered *every* clickable map layer -- parcels included -- into one shared list a click is tested against, so that overlapping features of different types could surface together in one chooser. But a parcel polygon almost always geometrically contains whatever smaller feature sits on top of it -- a tenure, a cutblock, a road normally sit *inside* some parcel, that's just how land parcels work -- so testing the parcel as a co-equal candidate meant it matched practically every single click, turning what should have been one clean popup for the actual thing clicked into a 2-item "which one?" chooser every time. That's the "blocking" Karim reported: not a z-order/stacking bug, but the parcel showing up as unwanted competition in nearly every chooser. Fixed by taking parcels -- and, for the identical reason, municipality and park, both of which are similarly large background boundary polygons that would swallow nearly every click the same way -- back out of `currentClickableFeatures` entirely, back to a plain `bindPopup()` each. Leaflet still routes a click to whichever *one* shape is drawn on top at that exact pixel: a smaller feature drawn on top of a parcel still wins the click outright (and still gets the full shared-chooser treatment against *other* small-scale features, if any also overlap there); only a click that hits just the parcel, with nothing more specific there, falls through to the parcel's own plain popup -- the exact pre-v79 behaviour. Tenures, woodlot licences, WMA, cutblocks, recreation, MVPR, and roads are unaffected and still fully participate in the shared chooser, since none of them are the kind of near-universal background layer parcels/municipality/park are.

**2) The verdict's flag-chip list is now three columns by severity instead of one long wrapped row.** Straightforward layout change (`.verdict-flags` switched from `display:flex;flex-wrap:wrap` to a 3-column CSS grid), grouping `sectionFlags` into green/amber/red buckets and rendering one column each, in that left-to-right order, per Karim's literal spec. An empty column shows a small muted "None" placeholder rather than a blank gap, so the grid doesn't look broken when, say, nothing is flagged red. Gray (data-unavailable) sections are real information but aren't one of the three colours Karim named, so they get their own small "Data unavailable" row underneath the three columns instead of being forced into a column they don't belong in. Collapses to one column under 600px width, matching the app's existing narrow-width breakpoint. This list is far more populated than it used to be: since v78 gave every clean "no overlap"/"none found" section a green flag too (not just the exceptional red/amber ones), `sectionFlags` now normally holds nearly every report section on every lookup, which is exactly why a flat wrapped row had started to feel like clutter worth organizing -- three columns read at a glance in a way a single long wrapped line of a dozen chips doesn't.

**3) A real, pre-existing latent bug was found (and fixed) while building #2.** The very first version of the new 3-column CSS rule silently failed to apply at all -- `getComputedStyle` kept reporting `display:block` instead of `display:grid` no matter what the rule said. Traced to the multi-line comment sitting directly above it (written back in v76): it references two CSS class names as `.card-flag-*/.flag-explain`, and that `*/` in the middle -- meant as a wildcard-selector-prefix followed by a slash, not a comment terminator -- is *also* the literal token that closes a CSS comment. The comment therefore silently ended several sentences early, and everything after it (several more sentences of real explanatory text) was parsed as garbled CSS until the parser's error recovery consumed the next `{...}` block it found as part of resyncing -- which happened to be the brand new `.verdict-flags{}` rule this session added right after it, discarding that entire rule while everything past it recovered and parsed normally. This bug predates this session (the comment has read this way since v76) and had apparently never been caught, since nothing anyone added directly after that comment block needed its own CSS rule to actually take effect until now. Fixed by rewording the comment to "`.card-flag-* and .flag-explain`", removing the accidental `*/`; swept the rest of the stylesheet for the same `<letter>-*/` shape and found no other occurrences. Worth being upfront that this was a lucky catch, not something looked for on purpose -- it surfaced only because this session happened to check `getComputedStyle` on the new rule rather than just checking the DOM structure it produced, which is a checking habit worth keeping for future CSS-only changes.

**Verification.** `node --check` on the extracted script: clean. Div balance: 292/292. Stylesheet rule count confirmed 253 (up from 252) after the comment fix, with `.verdict-flags`'s own parsed rule now showing up in the DOM's live stylesheet and `getComputedStyle` correctly reporting `display: grid`. A synthetic map render with a tenure and a cutblock deliberately made to overlap at the same point, alongside a parcel/municipality/park that also technically cover that point, confirmed the shared chooser now finds only the tenure + cutblock (2 matches) -- parcel/municipality/park are absent from `currentClickableFeatures` entirely. A synthetic report render with a red (inside municipality), one amber (Crown tenure), six green (clean sections), and one gray (a failed woodlot query) flag confirmed the DOM renders exactly three `.vf-col` columns labelled Clear/Caution/Flagged with the right chip counts and content in each, plus a separate gray "Data unavailable" row with the one gray chip. A screenshot of the rendered verdict box visually confirmed the three-column layout reads correctly.

## v79: one shared "what's right here" click popup for every map layer, generalizing v30's cutblock-only overlap chooser
Karim's request, verbatim:

*"Let's make it so on a click if there are multiple elements (ex. roads, tenures, cut block) have a pop up with a list of elements right near the click"*

**Root cause and scope.** Since v30, clicking a spot where two or more *cutblock* polygons genuinely overlapped in BC's own data already showed a small chooser popup listing every one of them — because Leaflet only ever routes a click to whichever single shape is drawn on top at that pixel, so a plain per-layer popup silently hides everything underneath. But that fix was built specifically for cutblocks: every other layer (parcels, municipality, park, WMA, tenures, woodlot, recreation, MVPR, roads) still used a plain one-shape-only popup, so a click where, say, a tenure polygon and a cutblock and a forest service road all pass through the same spot — Karim's own examples — would only ever show whichever one of those happened to be on top, with the others reachable only by clicking a slightly different pixel that isn't also covered by the winner. This wasn't a new bug so much as v30's fix never having been generalized past the one layer type it was built for. Root-caused and fixed by turning it into a single mechanism every layer shares, rather than adding a second parallel "which layers am I checking" system alongside the first.

**1) Every clickable map feature — across every layer type — now registers into one shared list, rebuilt on every lookup.** New `currentClickableFeatures`, populated by a small `pushClickable(geometry, icon, rowLabel, title, html)` call added next to where each layer already gets drawn in `renderMapOverlays()`: parcels (both the clicked one and the faded "nearby, for context" ones), municipal boundary, park/protected area, Wildlife Management Area, Crown land tenures, forest tenure licences (woodlot), cutblocks, recreation sites/trails/areas, motor vehicle prohibitions (routes and areas), and — new as of this version — forest service roads. Each entry carries the feature's real bare geometry (Polygon/MultiPolygon, LineString/MultiLineString, or Point — never a wrapped Feature), a short escaped label for a chooser-list row, and the same popup HTML that layer already showed before this existed.

**2) A single shared click handler, `openNearbyFeaturesPopupAt(latlng)`, replaced every layer's individual `bindPopup()`.** It re-tests the real click point against every registered feature's real geometry — a polygon via an exact contains-test (`pointInGeometry`, already used for the old cutblock-only version), a line or point via a screen-pixel-tolerant distance test (`pointToLineGeomMeters`/`distanceMeters`, both pre-existing meters-math helpers already used elsewhere in the app for the Potential Spots road/building buffer, converted from a fixed number of screen pixels to real-world meters at the click's own zoom level via a new `pixelToleranceMeters()` helper, so the click target feels equally forgiving whether zoomed in or out). Exactly one match behaves exactly as it always did — the same single-record popup that layer already showed. Two or more matches — of any mix of types, not just same-type records — opens the same style of "N records here" chooser v30 introduced for cutblocks, generalized: each row shows an icon and a short label (e.g. "📜 Crown tenure — Grazing", "🪵 Opening OP123", "🛣️ Test FSR — active"), and clicking a row opens that one feature's full detail in the app's existing info-modal pattern.

**3) Roads gained a click popup for the first time, alongside their existing hover tooltip.** Roads were previously the one layer with no click behaviour at all — info only appeared on hover, and a click on a lone road did nothing (its `bubblingMouseEvents:false` deliberately swallowed the click so it wouldn't also re-select that point as a new location, but nothing filled the gap). Since roads were one of Karim's own named examples, this needed to change: a road now also participates in the shared click handler, so clicking a spot where a road and something else — a tenure, a cutblock — meet now surfaces both, while the hover tooltip keeps working exactly as before for a quick glance without clicking.

**4) The old cutblock-only overlap mechanism was removed outright, not left alongside the new one.** `openCutblockMapPopupAt()`, `cutblockOverlapPopupHtml()`, `showMapCutblockDetail()`, and the now-redundant `currentMapCutOpenings`/`currentDrawableCutEntries` globals are gone; `openCutblockFromList()` (clicking a cutblock in the report list) now calls the same `openNearbyFeaturesPopupAt()` everything else uses, so it picks up any cross-type overlap too, not just cutblock-vs-cutblock — a genuine improvement over the old behaviour, not just a refactor, since a cutblock in the report list can now surface an overlapping tenure or road from the exact same click-from-list action.

**Verification.** `node --check` on the extracted script: clean. Div balance: 288/288. Same network-stubbed Playwright approach as prior rounds (cdnjs still unreachable this session) — with one added caveat worth being upfront about: the real Leaflet `map.latLngToContainerPoint()`/`distance()` calls behind `pixelToleranceMeters()` can't be exercised through the generic stub used all session (it has no real pixel-to-meters math, only a chainable no-op), so that one piece is standard, well-established Leaflet library behaviour taken on faith, same as every other real-map interaction this whole session's stub testing has never been able to reach. Everything that *is* custom app logic was verified directly: `featureNearPoint()` correctly dispatches and hit-tests all three geometry types (polygon contains-test, line and point distance-tests, each confirmed both inside and outside an explicit tolerance); a synthetic `renderMapOverlays()` call covering all ten layer types registered exactly the right `currentClickableFeatures` entries with the right icon/label for each; a click at a point where a parcel, a tenure, and a cutblock were deliberately made to overlap correctly produced a 3-way match with the right icons/labels/titles, and clicking a chooser row opened the info modal with that exact feature's own content; a single-feature click and a no-match click both ran without error, preserving the original one-shape popup behaviour for the common case.

## v78: universal green card tinting, click-anywhere card expand, right-aligned checklist buttons, 2 km cutblock radius, and a negative research finding on Wildlife Act no-shooting areas
Karim's requests, verbatim (sent together, with an attached screenshot of a red/pink-tinted "Municipal boundary" card showing an "inside" pill and a "Details ▾" button):

*"Municipal boundary should be green if the pin is not in one."*

*"A click anywhere on the category in the report should expand/close it (see attached photo)"*

*"All the 'I' icons on the checklist should be pushed to the right side then the 'See report' buttons"*

*"All the categories showing no overlap or none found should be highlighted green"*

*"Forestry activity category list should be expanded to 2 km"*

*"Let's build in the rules from the Wild life act no shooting areas"*

**1) "Municipal boundary should be green if not in one" turned out to be one symptom of a much broader gap — every "clean" report section had the same untinted-white bug, not just municipality.** Root cause: v76's card-tinting mechanism only colours a card when `addReason(level, text)` was actually called during that section — `pushCard()` reads whatever's in the `currentFlags` buffer, picks the worst level present, and tints the card accordingly; call it never, and the card renders with no tint at all, regardless of what its pill/body actually says. Nine sections' "clean" branches (municipality "outside", park "no overlap", WMA "no overlap", recreation "none nearby", MVPR "none on record", cutblocks "none nearby", tenures "none found", woodlot "none found", and road "no FSR sections + no DriveBC alerts") never called `addReason('green', ...)` on their clean path — only their flagged paths did — so a genuinely clean result rendered as a plain white card instead of the reassuring green one. Fixed the same way in all nine: an explicit `addReason('green', "...")` call on the clean branch, plus (new, not asked for directly but the same gap) an `addReason('gray', "...")` call on each section's own data-retrieval-failure branch, so "we don't know" now reads as gray/uncertain instead of also defaulting to untinted white. Treated as one generalized fix across every section per the literal ask ("**all** the categories showing no overlap or none found"), not a one-off patch to municipality alone.

**2) A card's whole title bar is now a click target for expand/collapse, not just its "Details ▾" button.** The existing delegated `#report` click listener already special-cased `.details-toggle` clicks; the logic behind that (`toggleCardDetails(btn)`) was extracted into its own function so a second branch — `.card-title` clicks that *aren't* the toggle button itself — can call the identical code without duplicating it. Checked `.details-toggle` first and returned immediately so a literal button click can't double-fire through both branches. Deliberately scoped to `.card-title` only, not the whole `.card` — a card's expanded body can contain real interactive content (a cutblock's own "details ›" row, an external link), and those need to keep working as their own click targets rather than being swallowed by a card-wide collapse handler; verified directly (see below) that clicking inside an open card's body does not collapse it. New CSS, `.card-title:has(.details-toggle){cursor:pointer;}`, shows the pointer cursor only on cards that actually have something to expand (the same `:has()` pattern the checklist's own manual-item checkbox styling already uses).

**3) Checklist "i" and "See report ›" buttons are now a right-aligned group instead of flowing inline right after the title.** Both `autoChecklistItemHtml()` and `manualChecklistItemHtml()` now emit a separate `.ci-actions` wrapper (`display:flex;gap:6px;flex:0 0 auto;margin-left:auto`) as its own sibling of the title text, rather than appending the buttons directly after it; the title's own `.txt` wrapper got `flex:1 1 auto;min-width:120px` so it claims the row's slack space and pushes `.ci-actions` flush to the row's right padding regardless of how long the title or pill text is. On a narrow row that wraps, the button group still lands flush-right on whichever line it wraps to (`flex-wrap:wrap` on `.checklist-item`), rather than being cut off or left floating mid-row.

**4) Forestry activity's search radius widened from 1 km to 2 km.** New dedicated `CUTBLOCK_SEARCH_RADIUS_M = 2000` constant, deliberately kept separate from the existing `SEARCH_RADIUS_M = 1000` that roads, recreation, tenures, and the surrounding-parcels context draw all still use — widening only cutblocks/forestry was the ask, and a shared constant would have silently widened all of those other, unrelated queries too. All three layers that build the merged cutblocks list (`cutblock` / RESULTS openings, `harvest` / Consolidated Cutblocks harvest-completion join, `cutblockPlan` / FTEN planned blocks) now read `radius:CUTBLOCK_SEARCH_RADIUS_M`. Every display string that named the old 1 km figure was found and updated to 2 km: the report card's title ("Forestry activity within 2 km") and its "none nearby"/count-found body text, and the checklist's own "i" button explanation (`CHECKLIST_ITEM_INFO.cutblocks`). Confirmed no other text (the `active-logging` manual item's own explanation, the map legend) named a specific radius that needed updating.

**5) Researched whether BC publishes a spatial dataset for Wildlife Act "no shooting" areas — it doesn't, so this stays a manual item.** Went beyond a training-data recollection and fetched BC's live ArcGIS REST MapServer catalogue directly (`delivery.maps.gov.bc.ca/arcgis/rest/services/whse/bcgw_pub_whse_wildlife_management/MapServer`, ~78 layers) looking for a "Closed Areas"/"Public Access Prohibition Regulation"/no-shooting-areas layer. Found none — the only spatially-mapped Wildlife Act closures in BC's open data are the two Motor Vehicle Prohibition layers (routes/areas) this app already auto-checks (since v39). Rather than fake a partial automation or silently drop the request, the "Wildlife Act closed areas" manual checklist item (added in v68) stays exactly as it is — its own "i" button already explains the Public Access Prohibition Regulation and why it's manual — and a standing to-do item was added to the task list so this gets picked up automatically if BC ever does publish one.

**Verification.** `node --check` on the extracted script: clean. Div balance: 288/288. Same network-stubbed Playwright approach as v76/v77 (cdnjs still unreachable this session). Confirmed directly: with every layer returning a clean/empty result, all nine report cards (municipality, park, WMA, recreation, MVPR, cutblocks, tenures, woodlot, road) render `card-flag-green`; with every layer returning a failed retrieval, all nine render `card-flag-gray` (not green — "unknown" correctly stays visually distinct from "checked and clean"); a municipality "inside" result still renders `card-flag-red` (the original flagged case is untouched by the new clean-case tinting). Clicking a card's title text (not the Details button) toggles its body open, then closed again, on repeated clicks; clicking the Details button directly still works and doesn't double-toggle; clicking an interactive field row inside an already-open card's body does not collapse the card. The checklist's `.ci-actions` group renders as the row's actual last child with both an `.ci-i` and `.ci-jump` button inside it, and a bounding-box check confirmed it sits flush against the row's right padding (16px from the row's own right edge) regardless of title length. `CUTBLOCK_SEARCH_RADIUS_M` reads 2000 and all three cutblock-related `LAYERS` entries use it, while the unrelated road layer stays on the original 1000; a synthetic cutblock hit renders both the card title and its amber body text as "2 km", and the checklist's cutblocks "i" button text also reads "2 km". Screenshots of the populated checklist and an open report card confirmed the visual result matches what was asked.

## v77: checklist redesign — Done/Incomplete pills instead of checkboxes+results, simplified titles, jump-to-report buttons, and three content changes (lawful access, hunting scope, PAL transport/storage)
Karim's requests, verbatim:

*"The only thing in the checklist that you should be able to manually check is the manual items, auto items should be checkmarked after a pin drop returns a report."*

*"Remove the results from the checklist. It should say 'Done' or 'Incomplete' in Green or Red respectively (like how 'Auto' is designed). Extra information should be in an 'i' icon to the side of the checklist items."*

*"Simplify the title of each item on the checklist."*

*"Make a button next to each completed item on the checklist that takes you to it's corresponding spot on the report and expands the results for that category."*

*"Remove 'Lawful access' from the checklist and just put a blurb in disclaimers about how you must gain access to these spots lawfully and it is not endorsing trespassing."*

*"In the 'Valid Pal' item put a button triggers brings a pop up with a list of how to properly transport and store non-restricted rifles."*

*"Lets remove the hunting item from the checklist"*

**1) Auto items are no longer checkboxes — they're a Done/Incomplete pill, and nothing else in the checklist repeats their result.** Root cause of the whole ask: the old auto items carried a (pointless — nothing ever checked them) checkbox *and* an inline result sentence ("yes — City of X", "3 record(s) — see report above") that just restated, in miniature, what that item's own report card already showed in full. Both are gone. `AUTO_ITEMS` (an id/report-card-key/title per item, replacing the old hand-written HTML template) now renders through `autoChecklistItemHtml(item, ok)`: a `<span class="tag done">`/`<span class="tag incomplete">` pill (new CSS, same pill shape as the old blue "AUTO" tag, coloured with the app's existing green/red tokens) replacing the checkbox entirely, `ok` meaning "this specific check actually ran against live data for the picked location" — not whether it found something, just whether it *ran*. Before a location is picked, every auto item renders `ok:false` (Incomplete) since there's nothing to check yet; `renderReport()` now builds a small `autoOk` map (one boolean per item, reusing each layer's own existing `.ok`/`cutListOk`/`recOk`/etc.) and passes it to `renderAutoChecklist()` once real data comes back, flipping each item to Done or leaving it Incomplete (a failed retrieval) independently.

**2) Manual items dropped their "More ▾" inline-expand text too — everything now goes through the same "i" button pattern as the rest of the app.** The old `.mi-more`/`.mi-full` toggle (and the CSS/JS behind it) is deleted outright rather than left as unused cruft; manual items keep their checkbox (unchanged — that's the one thing this checklist should still let you tick yourself) and a `MANUAL` tag, but their explanation now lives behind an "i" button exactly like the map key's own row-level "i" buttons already work. Both auto and manual items' "i" buttons read from one new registry, `CHECKLIST_ITEM_INFO` (id → `{title, html}`), through a single delegated click listener on `#checklist` — the same `data-info` lookup-table pattern `LEGEND_INFO`/`.lg-i` already established, reused rather than inventing a second mechanism.

**3) Every item's title is a short label now; the old full sentence moved into its "i" button.** E.g. "Inside an incorporated municipality?" → "Municipality"; "Crown land tenures (lease/licence/permit) here?" → "Crown land tenures"; "Tenures not covered by the two auto-checks above" → "Other Crown tenures". Nothing about *what* each item checks changed — only where the full sentence explaining it lives.

**4) A "See report ›" button on every Done auto item jumps to, and opens, its exact report card.** New shared `jumpToReportCard(key)` — factored out of the verdict's existing flag-chip click handler (v76), which did the identical three steps (make sure the report is visible, scroll to `#card-<key>`, open its Details if it wasn't already) — is now called from both the flag chips *and* a new delegated `.ci-jump` handler on `#checklist`. Each `AUTO_ITEMS` entry carries a `cardKey` matching the report card it mirrors 1:1 (parcel, municipality, bylaw, park, wma, recreation, cutblocks, tenures, woodlot, road), so the button always lands on the right card. `jumpToReportCard()` also calls `openBottomPanel()` first, since the bottom report drawer can be collapsed independently of the checklist being open.

**5) The bylaw item moved from the manual list to the auto list — because it already behaves like an auto item.** This one wasn't asked for directly, but fell straight out of applying #1's "don't repeat a result the report already shows" rule honestly: the old manual "bylaw" item had its own `summaryOverride` logic duplicating, word for word, what `pushCard('bylaw', ...)` already puts in the report's own "Bylaw lookup for this spot" card (a live-fetched excerpt for verified jurisdictions, or a generated search link otherwise) — that card fires automatically the instant a jurisdiction is determined, with no checkbox-worthy manual step involved. Leaving it as a manual item with a duplicated dynamic summary would have meant fixing #1's stated problem everywhere except the one item that most exemplified it. Reclassified: `bylaw` is now `AUTO_ITEMS[0]`, Done means `bylawJurisdiction` was actually determined for this point (there's no separate WFS query to check `.ok` against), with a `cardKey:"bylaw"` jump button like every other auto item.

**6) "Lawful access to the site itself" is gone from the checklist — its content is now a Disclaimers paragraph.** Root reason it never fit well as a per-location checklist item: it isn't actually about the clicked location at all ("even where the parcel is Crown land, the route to it may cross private property... this isn't auto-checked... confirm the road/trail in is public the whole way") — it's a standing caveat that applies identically everywhere, which is exactly what Disclaimers is for. Added as a new `.mrow` in `DISCLAIMERS_HTML`, right after the existing sourcing note: gaining access lawfully is the reader's responsibility, and the tool doesn't endorse or condone trespassing to reach anywhere it shows, with a link to BC's Trespass Act.

**7) "If this trip might turn into hunting" is deleted, no replacement.** Straightforward removal per Karim's ask — this tool has been explicitly scoped to recreational target shooting since early on (see "Scope decided with Karim" above), and the item had nothing left to check once removed.

**8) The Valid PAL item gets a "Transport & storage guide" button.** Opens a new modal (`CHECKLIST_EXTRA_MODALS.palTransport`, via a small second registry keyed by `data-open-modal` for longer/list-style content that doesn't fit the one-blurb-per-item `CHECKLIST_ITEM_INFO` pattern) summarizing the baseline federal storage (s.5) and transportation (s.10) requirements for an ordinary **non-restricted** rifle/shotgun, sourced directly from the actual regulation text (Storage, Display, Transportation and Handling of Firearms by Individuals Regulations, SOR/98-209 — fetched and read directly, not recalled from memory, given how much it matters to get this right) rather than a general summary: unloaded plus either a secure locking device/bolt removal or a securely-locked container for storage, ammunition stored separately and securely locked; unloaded at all times in transport, with a locked trunk (or out-of-sight-in-a-locked-vehicle) requirement only when left unattended. Explicitly scoped to non-restricted firearms only, with a note that restricted/prohibited firearms carry additional requirements this tool doesn't cover, and links to both the regulation itself and the RCMP's Canadian Firearms Program for anything the summary doesn't answer.

**Verification.** `node --check` on the extracted script: clean. Div balance: 286/286. Same network-stubbed Playwright approach as v76 (cdnjs still unreachable this session). Confirmed directly: the checklist renders 10 auto + 8 manual items in the right order with the right simplified titles, and "access"/"hunting-scope" are genuinely gone from the DOM; every auto item shows Incomplete with no jump button before any location is picked; after a synthetic `renderReport()` call with mixed success/failure data, the bylaw and parcel items correctly flip to Done with working "See report ›" buttons while a deliberately-failed tenure query stays Incomplete with no jump button; clicking a jump button opens the bottom panel, scrolls to, and expands the right card; an item's "i" button opens the right modal with the right content; the PAL item's "Transport & storage guide" button opens a modal containing both the "Storage (s.5)" and "Transport (s.10)" sections; and the Disclaimers modal now contains the lawful-access/trespass paragraph. Screenshots at both a populated (post-lookup) and empty (pre-lookup) state confirmed the visual result — colour-coded pills, checkboxes only on manual rows, no inline result text anywhere — matches what was asked.

## v76: condensed flag-driven verdict, disclaimers consolidated, tools drawer moved to the right, and a run of smaller cutblock/layout fixes
Karim's requests, verbatim, sent across several messages in the same round:

*"For the report on the bottom instead of having the explanation of why you need to have caution at the top just show the state (ex. caution or likely not allowed) followed by a list of sections that have a flag. Under that where the categories are, highlight the ones in the corresponding colour that have flagged something and explain in those categories expanded states what is being flagged. Also clean up the app by putting all the disclaimers in one section. Put it all in the Not Legal advice pop up but change the name to 'Disclaimers'. Also switch the tools tab to the right side and put it under checklist. Remove the check mark emoji from the tab."*

*"My mark ups is being burried and you can not scroll to it. Highlight the cut block on the map corresponding to the item on the list that the mouse is hovering over. And when clicking on the cutblock item in the list it shouldn't create it's own pop up it shoul'd just open the same pop up that would pop up if you we're to click on the cutblock on the map."*

*"When expanded, the cutblock list should show all cut blocks instead of saying '+14 more'"*

*"Put the 'i' icon in the search bar to the right of the search button outside that box"*

*"The map key is layered behind the buttons on the left side, they should not overlap"*

*"Remove the potential spots section and put the loading bar and clear button under map layers. Change 'Map layers' to 'Mapping Functions'. Change 'Map tools' to 'Tools'"*

*"Make the report at the bottom move with the tools tab. Both of them should be able to be open and read both. They should not overlap eachother"*

*"The crown land button should be switched to 'View Parcels' and instead of just showing the nearby crown land it should highlight every single parcel in the view. The parcel colour scheme should be the same as what shows up in he shooting spot button"* — the last sentence was ambiguous between two real palettes already in the app (the ownership-type `OWNER_COLORS` rainbow used for the clicked parcel/legend, versus Shooting Spots' own teal/dashed legal-status styling), so this was checked directly rather than guessed; Karim picked the ownership-type palette.

Thirteen changes, grouped by what they touch:

**1) The verdict box no longer repeats every reason inline — it shows the state, then a chip per flagged section.** `renderReport()` used to build one flat `reasons.{red,amber,green,gray}` list and print the whole thing (every reason from every card) directly under the verdict headline — duplicating text that a card below it would also show once you opened that card's Details. Root cause of the redesign brief: that list was doing two jobs at once (driving the overall verdict level, *and* being the explanation) when only the first one actually needs to happen at the top. Fixed by splitting them: a new `addReason(level, text)` still pushes into the existing `reasons` object exactly as before (the overall verdict level/label computation is completely unchanged, so no separate logic to keep in sync), but also buffers the same `{level, text}` into a small `currentFlags` array. A new `pushCard(key, icon, title, pill, body, collapsible)` wrapper reads that buffer when it builds each card: if the card pushed anything, it picks the worst level present (`FLAG_PRIORITY`, red > amber > green > gray), passes `{level, texts}` into `cardHTML()` as a new `flag` argument, records `{key, icon, title, level}` into a `sectionFlags` list, then resets the buffer for the next card. `cardHTML()` (now `cardHTML(key, icon, title, pillHtml, bodyHtml, collapsible, flag)`) uses `flag` to tint the card with a `card-flag-{level}` class (reusing the existing `--red-bg`/`--amber-bg`/etc. tokens) and append a `.flag-explain` block — a small label plus a `<ul>` of exactly what that section flagged — into the card's own (still-collapsible) body. The verdict box itself now renders the state headline followed by a `.verdict-flags` row of clickable `.flag-chip` buttons, one per entry in `sectionFlags`, colour-coded the same way (or a "No flagged sections below" message on a genuinely clean result). Clicking a chip (`data-jump="<key>"`, matched by a new branch in `#report`'s existing delegated click listener, checked before the `.details-toggle` branch so the two never conflict) scrolls to that card (`id="card-<key>"`, new — `cardHTML()` sets it) and auto-opens its Details if it wasn't already open, so "what's flagged" and "why" are one click apart instead of both crammed at the top. Verified with synthetic report data covering a red case (private parcel → single chip, verdict "🛑 Significant red flags"), a mixed amber+green case (Crown-provincial parcel + a Crown tenure → two chips, correct per-card `card-flag-green`/`card-flag-amber` classes, verdict "⚠️ Caution"), and a chip click (scrolled to and expanded the right card).

**2) Every disclaimer now lives in one place, renamed "Disclaimers."** The header's "⚠️ Not legal advice" button/modal is renamed "Disclaimers" (button label, and the modal's title), and `DISCLAIMER_HTML` → `DISCLAIMERS_HTML` now also contains the sentence that used to live in the separate "Data sources & attribution" modal about BC's Province not being affiliated with this tool and the backroadstatus.com link for community-reported conditions — that sentence (and its `.mnote` div) is removed from `SOURCES_HTML`, which is now purely the factual list of DataBC/Open511 sources it was always meant to be. Nothing about disclaimers is left split across two modals anymore.

**3) The tools drawer is on the right edge now, stacked under the checklist tab, and the checklist tab's checkmark is gone.** The entire former `.left-toolbar` (Map type, the action buttons, My Markup) is removed from its old position on the left and rebuilt as `.tools-drawer` after `.side-checklist` in the DOM, mirroring its CSS structure but on the right (`right:0`, border/shadow direction flipped) instead of the left. A new `--sc-tab-h` CSS var, kept current by a `syncChecklistTabHeight()` ResizeObserver (replacing v73's `--leaflet-left-h`, which no longer has anything to measure now that nothing sits on the left), lets `.td-tab{margin-top:calc(var(--sc-tab-h) + 10px)}` position the Tools tab exactly below the Checklist tab regardless of the checklist tab's real rendered height, rather than a guessed constant. Because both tabs now live in their own independent `right:0`-anchored fixed containers, opening either one's panel would otherwise visually collide with the *other* drawer's tab (both sit flush against the same edge) — so a new `body.right-drawer-open` class (set by a single `updateRightDrawerState()`, called from every open/close handler) hides *both* tabs whenever *either* drawer is open, and each drawer's open handler closes the other one first (`closeToolsDrawer()`/`closeChecklist()`). Switching drawers goes through each one's own close (✕) button, not by clicking the other tab mid-open — verified directly: opening Tools hides both tabs and sets `--right-drawer-w:260px`; closing it via its ✕ restores both tabs and `--right-drawer-w:0px`; opening Checklist afterward sets `--right-drawer-w:360px` with Tools confirmed still closed. The dead `body.tools-open` class, its `margin-left:276px` rule for Leaflet's top-left controls, and the whole `syncLeafletLeftHeight()` IIFE were removed rather than left as unused cruft, since nothing sits on the left edge to shift out of the way anymore. Separately, `#sideChecklistTab`'s label dropped its "✅ " prefix per Karim's ask — plain "Checklist" now, same as "Tools" already was unadorned by an emoji.

**4) The markup panel's "buried, can't scroll to it" report was a real min-height:0 flexbox bug, not a fluke.** `.td-scroll` (the tools drawer's scrollable body, holding all its groups including My Markup) is `flex:1 1 auto; overflow-y:auto; display:flex; flex-direction:column;` — a flex child that's *itself* a flex container. Every browser's flexbox default gives a flex item `min-height:auto`, which resolves to its own content's intrinsic height, not `0` — so a flex child with `overflow-y:auto` still won't shrink below that intrinsic height on its own, meaning when the drawer's actual available height is smaller than the combined height of every group inside it, the *ancestor* `.td-panel` (which does have `overflow:hidden`) silently clips the excess instead of `.td-scroll`'s own scrollbar ever engaging. This is the exact bug pattern `.bp-body` (the bottom report drawer) already hit and fixed, with its own explanatory comment, earlier in this file's history — same fix applied here: `min-height:0` added to `.td-scroll`, and proactively to `.side-checklist .checklist-scroll` too (same container shape, same latent risk, no report against it yet but no reason to leave it). Verified by forcing genuinely-unshrinkable content (a flex-shrink:0 spacer, since a plain shrinkable div doesn't reproduce the bug) past the drawer's available height: before the fix behavior, `scrollHeight === clientHeight` (no scroll possible); after, the container correctly overflows and `scrollTop` can be set past 0 to reach the bottom.

**5) Hovering a cutblock in the report list highlights that same shape on the map.** New delegated `mouseover`/`mouseout` listeners on `#report`, matching `[data-cutblock-idx]` rows (already present on each cutblock line for the click-to-open feature below), look up `currentCutOpenings[idx].mapLayer` — a reference to the entry's actual drawn Leaflet layer, newly stored by `renderMapOverlays()` the moment it draws each cutblock — and call `.setStyle()` with a new `cutblockHighlightStyle()` (same colour, heavier weight, higher opacity) on hover, reverting to the normal `cutblockStyle()` on mouseout, plus `bringToFront()` so the highlighted shape isn't hidden under a neighbour. `mouseover`/`mouseout` (not `mouseenter`/`mouseleave`, which don't bubble) with a `relatedTarget` guard keeps this from re-firing while the pointer moves between child elements of the same row. Verified directly: dispatching a synthetic hover on a list row with a mock map layer attached calls `setStyle` with the heavier/highlighted style and `bringToFront`; the following mouseout call reverts it to the normal style.

**6) Clicking a cutblock in the report list now opens the literal same on-map popup a real map click would — not its own modal.** The gap here was never the popup's *content* (`cutblockPopup()`/`cutblockOverlapPopupHtml()` were already shared between both paths) — it was presentation: the list click used to open a centred modal dialog, while a map click opened a real Leaflet popup anchored to the shape. Closed by reusing the entry's stored `.mapLayer` (see #5): `openCutblockFromList()` takes its bounds' centre, verifies that point actually falls inside the entry's real geometry via the existing `pointInGeometry()` (falling back to a real vertex-centroid, `ringCentroid()`, for concave shapes where a bounding-box centre can land outside the polygon), pans the map to it if it's off-screen, then calls a newly-factored-out `openCutblockMapPopupAt(latlng, drawableEntries)` — the exact same function a real map click on a drawn cutblock now calls too — so if other cutblocks genuinely overlap at that point, a list click shows the same "N overlapping records" chooser a map click would, not a single-record view. `cutblockColor()`/`cutblockStyle()`/`cutblockHighlightStyle()` were factored out the same way, shared by both the real map-click drawing code and this list-click path, so the two can never drift out of sync. An entry with no `.mapLayer` (an oversized cutblock, deliberately excluded from the map draw — see the v26 section below) still falls back to the old modal, since there's genuinely no on-map popup for it to mirror. Verified: a synthetic entry with a mock `.mapLayer` routed through `showCutblockDetail()` without throwing and without ever calling the modal-opening function (spied on directly), confirming the map-popup path — not the fallback — is what actually ran.

**7) The cutblock list shows every record when expanded — no more "+N more."** `currentCutOpenings` (the array the list renders from, and that `showCutblockDetail(i)` looks entries up in) used to be capped to `cutList.slice(0,5)` with a "+N more" line for the rest; now it's the full `cutList`, unsliced, and the body maps over all of it. Each entry is still condensed to one line (ID, life-cycle status, one headline date) — the full planned/start/completion breakdown, area, and licensee are still one click away via the same "Details" popup as before, per record; this only removes the arbitrary display cap on how many *records* show up in the list, not how much detail each one carries. Verified with a synthetic 20-entry `cutList`: the report renders exactly 20 `[data-cutblock-idx]` rows and no "+N more" text appears anywhere in the card.

**8) The search bar's info icon moved outside the search box, to the right of the Search button.** `#coordInfoBtn` used to live inside `.tb-search` itself; it's now a sibling of `.tb-search` inside a new `.tb-search-row` flex wrapper, after it — so visually it's Search input, then the "Search" button (both still inside the pill-shaped `.tb-search` box), then the circular "i" icon sitting outside that box entirely, to its right. `.tb-search` switched from `width:100%` to `flex:1 1 auto; min-width:0` so it still fills the available space alongside the icon rather than the icon being squeezed off or overlapping.

**9) The map key/left-edge overlap is gone — a side effect of #3, not a separate patch.** The map key (bottom-left) used to sit layered behind the old left-edge toolbar's tab/panel. Moving that toolbar to the right edge entirely (#3) removes the only thing that was ever positioned over there to begin with; there's nothing left on the left edge for the legend to collide with. No dedicated CSS change was needed beyond the move itself.

**10) The bottom report drawer now pulls its right edge in when either right-side drawer is open, so both can be read together.** `.bottom-panel` was hard-pinned `right:0`, which would run it directly underneath whichever right-side drawer (Checklist or Tools) happened to be open. A new `--right-drawer-w` CSS var — set by the same `updateRightDrawerState()` from #3 to `360px` (checklist open), `260px` (tools open), or `0px` (neither) — drives `.bottom-panel{right:var(--right-drawer-w); transition:right .18s ease;}`, so the report drawer's own right edge slides in to clear whichever drawer is open instead of running behind it. Verified: opening Tools sets `--right-drawer-w:260px`; opening Checklist instead sets it to `360px`; closing either brings it back to `0px` — all read directly off `document.documentElement`'s computed style after each drawer transition.

**11) "Map layers" and "Potential spots" merged into one "Mapping Functions" group; "Map tools" → "Tools."** The tools drawer used to have two separate groups — one holding the View Parcels/Shooting Spots buttons, a second below it holding the progress bar, status note, and "Clear Potential Spots" button. Karim asked for the second to fold into the first (now labelled "Mapping Functions" instead of "Map layers") rather than sit as its own section, and for the drawer's own header ("Map tools") to read simply "Tools." Both are just markup/label changes — no behavioural change to any of the controls themselves. Verified: the drawer now has exactly two `.td-group-label`s ("Map type", "Mapping Functions") instead of three, and the merged group contains the View Parcels button, the status/progress elements, all under the one heading.

**12) "Crown Land" is now "View Parcels" and draws every parcel in view, colour-coded by ownership.** The button used to query only Crown provincial/untitled provincial parcels (`CROWN_OWNER_CQL`, a server-side `OWNER_TYPE` filter) and draw just those two categories. Renamed to "View Parcels," it now drops that filter entirely — every parcel returned for the current viewport is drawn — coloured by `OWNER_COLORS[ownerCategory(...)]`, the same palette already used for the single clicked parcel in the report and the map legend (red private, green Crown provincial, gold untitled provincial, purple municipal, blue First Nation, brown Crown federal), rather than the narrower teal/dashed legal-status styling Shooting Spots uses — confirmed with Karim directly since the request's wording could have meant either. `CROWN_OWNER_CQL` itself is untouched and still used by the unrelated automatic Shooting Spots sweep. Renamed throughout: `revealCrownBtn`→`revealParcelsBtn`, `crownLandInfoBtn`→`parcelsInfoBtn`, `crownParcelLayer`/`Style`/`Features`→`parcelView*`, `drawCrownParcelLayer`→`drawParcelViewLayer` (guard removed — draws every feature instead of just crown/untitled), `revealCrownLand`→`revealAllParcels`, `CROWN_PARCEL_MIN_ZOOM`/`MAX_FEATURES`→`PARCEL_VIEW_*`, `CROWN_LAND_BLURB_HTML`→`PARCELS_VIEW_BLURB_HTML`; the legend's still-valid "Crown provincial parcel" swatch row (unrelated to the button rename — it's describing one specific colour, not the button) keeps its own `data-info="crownParcel"` info entry, with its blurb text updated to describe the new button/behaviour. Verified directly against the source: `drawParcelViewLayer()` colours every feature via `parcelViewStyle(ownerCategory(...))` with no category filter, and `parcelViewBboxCql()` issues no `CROWN_OWNER_CQL`-style restriction — confirming the query and the draw are both genuinely unfiltered now.

**Verification.** `node --check` on the extracted script: clean. Div balance: 277/277. cdnjs.cloudflare.com (where this app loads Leaflet/Leaflet.draw live) was unreachable to this session's Playwright — a different, more restrictive network policy than earlier sessions in this same project had — so verification used a from-scratch, fully generic Proxy-based `L` stub (real coordinate math for `L.latLng`/`L.latLngBounds`/`L.point`, since app code genuinely uses those; an infinitely-chainable, construct/apply/get-trapped proxy for every other Leaflet class/method) served via Playwright's `page.route()` in place of the real CDN files, so the app's own code runs completely unmodified against it. Confirmed end to end, with zero console/page errors throughout: the redesigned verdict (state + flag chips, correct colour-coded chips and card highlighting across a red-only case, a mixed amber+green case, and chip-click scroll+expand); the consolidated "Disclaimers" modal (renamed button/title, backroadstatus.com sentence merged in, no longer duplicated in Data Sources); the tools drawer on the right under Checklist (correct stacking, mutual exclusion via each drawer's own ✕, `--right-drawer-w` tracking which drawer if any is open); the Checklist tab's emoji removed; the My Markup scroll fix (genuinely scrollable past the drawer's visible height, where it wasn't before); cutblock hover-highlight (`setStyle`/`bringToFront` on the real map layer, confirmed via a mock layer) and list-click (routes through the real map-popup path, not the modal, confirmed by spying on the modal opener); all 20 rows of a synthetic 20-entry cutblock list rendering with no "+N more" text; the search bar's "i" icon sitting outside `.tb-search`, after it, in the DOM/visual order; the merged "Mapping Functions" group and "Tools" header label; and View Parcels' unfiltered, ownership-coloured query/draw logic read directly from source. A 1400×900 screenshot of the idle state confirmed the visual layout (both tabs stacked on the right, renamed Disclaimers button, repositioned search icon, unobstructed map key) matches.
