## v75: header/toolbar redesign — a real hidden-attribute bug, a consolidated search bar, and everything that used to float over the map moved into one place
Karim's ask, verbatim: *"The loading bar is still there when a pin has not been dropped or the shooting spot button has been pressed. It only needs to be active and show progress of query when one of the two functions are being used. As well show under the loading bar what is currently being fetched in simple terms, just a few words. Remove "Check this location" and simply put "Search" beside the coordinates. Also consolidate the address search and coordinates into one search bar in the header so that both can be used in the same one. Remove the explanation currently under the loading bar. Put the type of map selection in the tool bar and make it a 3 way slider to select the map. The tool bar and checklist opens behind the header, make it so all elements that would go behind the header stop below it except the map. Put my markups in the tool bar. When the tool bar expands the elements on the left side should not get buried under it they should come out with it and be to the left of it. Make sure the new search bar is centred."*

Seven changes in one pass, each addressed on its own:

**1) The progress bar's `hidden` attribute was cosmetic only — a real bug, not a visibility oversight.** `#spotsProgress` carries both `hidden` and the `.spots-progress` class, and `.spots-progress{display:flex;...}` is an author-stylesheet rule. Author rules always beat the browser's own `[hidden]{display:none}` user-agent rule at equal specificity, regardless of which one is "more specific" in the usual sense — origin order in the cascade settles it before specificity even gets compared. So `hidden` was never actually hiding the bar; it just sat there, visibly, at all times, including a stale 0%/empty state before any search had ever run. Fixed with an explicit `.spots-progress[hidden]{display:none;}` override — the actual root cause, not a workaround (e.g. switching to `.classList.toggle` would have "worked" without explaining why `hidden` alone didn't).

**2) A live, few-word "what's fetching" line replaces the old static paragraph.** `trackSpotsProgress()` now takes a `labels` array — one plain name per query (`parcels`, `parks`, `municipalities`, `Crown tenures`, `woodlots`, `cutblocks`, `active cutting permits`, `parcel boundaries`, `roads`, `buildings`), same order as the ~10 promises fired in `runPotentialSpotsSearch()` — and keeps a small `#spotsProgressLabel` line under the percent readout updated with whichever of those is still outstanding as each one settles. Since the queries genuinely run concurrently (not sequentially), this is a real reflection of what's still in flight, not a fake staged animation — the label jumps around according to actual network timing, same as the percent bar does. The old permanent explanation paragraph that used to sit under the buttons by default (`#mapActionsNote`'s HTML default text, "Picking a location — click the map...") is gone entirely; that element is now only ever populated with a real result, error, or status message once there's something to say — never a standing how-to blurb.

**3+4) One header search bar, coordinates or address, "Search" beside it.** The old three-piece header — separate Lat/Lng inputs, a magnifying-glass button that opened its own address-search modal, and a "📍 Check this location" button — is replaced by a single `#searchInput` + `#searchGoBtn` ("Search"). A typed value shaped like `lat, lng` (or `lat lng`) is recognized by a strict, anchored regex and used directly with no network round-trip; anything else is geocoded via the same Nominatim call the old modal used, showing a small dropdown under the bar when there's more than one match (auto-selecting straight through to the report when there's exactly one). The address-search modal (`#placeSearchModalOverlay` and its dedicated JS) is deleted outright, not just hidden — there's nothing left for it to do.

**5) The "explanation under the loading bar" is gone** — covered by #2 above; called out separately here because it was its own line item in the ask, distinct from the live label.

**6) Basemap picker → a 3-way segmented slider, in the toolbar.** The old three independent `.bm-btn` buttons floating top-right on the map are gone; `.seg3`/`.seg3-btn`/`.seg3-thumb` in the left toolbar's new "Map type" group render the same three choices as one sliding control instead — a single navy pill (`.seg3-thumb`) translates under whichever button is active, sized and positioned to match a button's own rendered width exactly (`calc((100% - 10px)/3)`, stepped by `calc(idx * 100% + idx * 2px)`) so it lands precisely instead of drifting off by the third segment. Click wiring is unchanged apart from the selector rename (`.bm-btn` → `.seg3-btn`) and the added `moveBasemapThumb()` call.

**7) Drawers stop *at* the header instead of running behind it.** Root cause of "opens behind the header": `.side-checklist` and `.left-toolbar` were `position:fixed; top:0`, i.e. their whole translucent box ran the full viewport height starting at y:0 — `.sc-head`/`.lt-head`'s own `padding-top:var(--tb-h)` only pushed their *content* down, the box itself (background, border, shadow) still extended up underneath the header's own translucency in the same stacking context, doubling up the blur/tint right along the top edge the instant either drawer opened. Fixed by starting both containers at `top:var(--tb-h)` instead of `top:0` — there's no overlapping region left for anything to render "behind." Only `#mapWrap` is meant to run the full height behind the header (that's the deliberate "floating over an edge-to-edge map" design); every other drawer now stops there. `.lt-tab`/`.sc-tab` margin-top and `.lt-head`/`.sc-head` padding-top were adjusted to match (they no longer need to add `var(--tb-h)` a second time now that the container itself already starts there).

**8) My Markup moved into the toolbar.** Same `#markupPanel` id (kept — `markupFlashStatus()` still queries `#markupPanel .mp-hint`) and the same internal `mp-toggle`/`mp-body` collapse/list/Export/Import, just as one more `.lt-group` in `.lt-scroll` instead of its own floating bottom-right card. No JS changed beyond the DOM move; only the outer positioning CSS was dropped in favour of a plain card background matching the other toolbar groups.

**9) Leaflet's own controls and the map key now slide out with the toolbar instead of being buried under it.** The open `.lt-panel` is 260px wide, sitting at `left:0` — exactly where Leaflet's own zoom/draw-toolbar stack and `.map-legend` also live. Previously the panel's higher z-index just covered them the moment it opened. Fixed with a `body.tools-open` class (toggled by `leftToolbarTab`/`leftToolbarClose`/`openLeftToolbar()` in lockstep with `.left-toolbar`'s own `.open` class) that shifts both `margin-left:276px`, on the same `.18s ease` transition as the panel's own width animation, so they visibly travel with the panel's trailing edge instead of popping into a new position after the fact or disappearing outright.

**10) The search bar is genuinely centred, not approximately.** `.tb-search-wrap` is `position:absolute; left:50%; transform:translateX(-50%)` on `.top-bar` itself (already a containing block, being `position:fixed` — no extra wrapper needed), centring it on the *whole header width* regardless of how wide `.tb-left` (title) and `.tb-right` (pin + disclaimer) happen to be — a flex-grow/`margin:auto` approach only centres correctly when the two side groups are equal width, which these never are. Being out of normal flow, it doesn't compete with `.tb-left`/`.tb-right` for space either. `#searchStatus` and the results dropdown are pinned to the wrap's bottom edge as their own absolutely-positioned (and mutually exclusive — never both shown at once) children, so a status line appearing/disappearing never grows the wrap's own box height and nudges the centred search bar itself up or down. Below 760px, the whole header folds to a plain stacked column (title, then the search bar at full width, then pin/disclaimer) instead of letting three independently-positioned regions collide.

**Verification.** `node --check` on the extracted script: clean. Div balance: 275/275. A Playwright pass with a fully generic Proxy-based `L`/map stub (construct + apply + get traps, so no more enumerating every Leaflet method/class by hand) confirmed, end to end: the progress bar is genuinely `hidden`/`display:none` at idle and becomes visible with a live "Fetching parcels…"-style label the moment a search runs; the old `#goBtn`, `.coord-group`, and address-search modal are gone from the DOM; the search wrap sits pixel-centred in a 1400px-wide header; the basemap segmented control and My Markup panel render inside the toolbar and are absent from `#mapWrap`; opening the toolbar adds `body.tools-open`, shifts `.map-legend`'s `margin-left` to 276px, and the toolbar panel's top edge lands exactly at the header's own height (54px = 54px) rather than above it; and a full coordinate-search run through `runLookup()` → `revealPotentialSpotsAroundPin()` → `runPotentialSpotsSearch()` completes with no console/page errors. Screenshots at 1400×900 (idle, toolbar open) and 500×850 (mobile-width stacked header) confirmed the visual result matches.

## v74: the tab's rounding was backwards -- a rotation-math bug, not a leftover
Karim's ask, verbatim: *"Flip the tools tab so its flush with the left side."*

**Root cause.** `.lt-tab` carries `transform:rotate(180deg)` (needed to make the vertical `writing-mode:vertical-rl` text read top-to-bottom instead of bottom-to-top) on top of a `border-radius`. `border-radius` is evaluated in the element's own *pre-rotation* box -- the transform spins the finished, already-rounded box 180° around its center afterward, which swaps top-left with bottom-right and top-right with bottom-left. v73's value, `0 10px 10px 0` (square left / round right -- correct-looking for a plain, unrotated box), ends up rendering *rounded-left / square-right* once the rotation is applied: the flush edge landed against the map instead of against the viewport, and the rounded curve landed against the viewport edge instead of sitting flush -- read as "there's a gap/line where the tab should be flush."

**Fix.** Swapped the value to `10px 0 0 10px` (round left / square right, in the box's own pre-rotation terms) so that after the 180° flip it renders square-left/round-right -- flush against the viewport edge, rounded corner facing the map, matching every other collapsed tab on this page (the side checklist's `.sc-tab` isn't rotated, so its own `border-radius` never had this problem -- this bug was specific to `.lt-tab` being the one tab on a rotated 180°).

**Verification.** `node --check` passes clean; div balance holds (273/273). A cropped screenshot of the tab confirms the fix: flush square edge against the viewport's left side, rounded corner facing the map.

## v73: left-side redesign — the hairline-sliver bug, root-caused, and a real layout instead of a collision patch
Karim's ask, verbatim: *"There's a very thin line in front of the tool tab on the left side. Let's get rid of that and re-arrange the left side to be clean, professional and easy to use. I want you to design this with user heuristics in mind as if you're a senior developer. No shortcuts."*

**The bug, root-caused.** The sliver was `.lt-panel`'s own `border-right:1px` (plus a `box-shadow`), sitting on a permanent, unconditional CSS rule that applied whether the panel was open or collapsed. `box-sizing:border-box` (set globally on this page) doesn't make a border disappear when an element's `width` is `0` — border-box only changes how the width budget is split between content/padding/border; a border always paints at its own declared width regardless. So the collapsed panel, despite being logically "0 width," was still rendering a real 1px vertical line (plus a soft shadow blur past even that) directly beside the tab. Fixed by moving both the border and the box-shadow onto the `.left-toolbar.open .lt-panel` rule instead of the base one, so a collapsed panel is genuinely invisible, not just visually thin. **The same bug existed on the right-side checklist drawer** (`.sc-panel`'s `border-left`/`box-shadow`, identical unconditional rule) — Karim only noticed it on the left, but it's the same defect, so it got the same fix on both rather than leaving a known-bad pattern sitting on the other drawer for someone to notice later.

**The tab's position, fixed at the root instead of patched.** v71 put the tab at the very top of the screen, directly over Leaflet's own top-left corner (the zoom control and the markup draw toolbar), and "fixed" the resulting collision by shoving *Leaflet's* controls sideways with a guessed `margin-left:48px` — moving a control the user already knows the location of, by a number that was never measured against anything. v73 reverses that: Leaflet's zoom/draw controls stay exactly where every Leaflet user expects them, untouched; the tool tab instead sits *below* them, cleared by their real rendered height. A new `syncLeafletLeftHeight()` function (mirroring `syncHeaderHeight()`'s existing `--tb-h` pattern exactly) keeps a `--leaflet-left-h` CSS variable in sync with `.leaflet-top.leaflet-left`'s actual `offsetHeight` via a `ResizeObserver`, so the tab's `margin-top` — `calc(var(--tb-h) + var(--leaflet-left-h) + 14px)` — tracks reality: the draw toolbar loading (or failing to, and leaving just the zoom control) both correct the gap automatically instead of needing their own separate fix later.

**The toolbar's contents, given real hierarchy.** The panel used to be one flat stack of buttons and text with no grouping. It's now two labeled sections — "Map layers" (Crown Land, Shooting Spots) and "Potential spots" (Clear Potential Spots, the progress bar, and the status note) — separated by a divider, with the status note moved into its own bordered card instead of sitting as bare text under the buttons (matching how every other result/status area on this page — report cards, the checklist — already gets a visible container, so a run's outcome reads as a distinct piece of information rather than an afterthought).

**Verification.** `node --check` passes clean; div/button balance holds (273/273, 55/55). A headless-browser Playwright run confirmed: the collapsed `.lt-panel` and `.sc-panel` both measure `0px` border width, `none` box-shadow, and `0` rendered width (the sliver is genuinely gone on both, not just thinner); the open panel correctly gains its `1px` border back; `--leaflet-left-h` and the tab's real `margin-top` are being read from computed styles rather than assumed; the two group labels, the divider, and the status card all render; and the full functional regression suite still passes — Crown Land, Shooting Spots (with its zoom gate), Clear Potential Spots, the progress bar, and its auto-open-the-toolbar behavior from v72 all still work exactly as before. Screenshots of both the collapsed and open states confirm the visual result: no line next to the tab, and a toolbar that reads as one deliberately organized panel instead of a stack of controls.

## v72: concise map key with per-row "i" icons, and a progress bar that's actually visible
Karim's ask, verbatim: *"Map key descriptions are too long, they need to be concise and easy to quickly read any explanation should be hidden in an 'I' icon beside it. Make it so the loading bar only shows up when you click the shoting button place aa pin. Make sure it's actually functional for both."*

**Map key.** Every row in the 28-row key is now just a swatch plus a few words (e.g. "Wildlife Management Area (advisory — not a discharge ban by itself)" → "Wildlife Management Area"; "Restricted — park, municipality, or road/building/private-land buffer (reason shown on click); only the affected part of a parcel is shaded if the rest is clear" → "Restricted (reason on click)"). Every row that used to carry inline parenthetical detail, plus the six long `.lg-note` paragraphs that used to sit permanently under the rows (cutblock-colouring methodology, the logging-phase status-code glossary, recreation-data caveats, motor-vehicle-closure caveats, the "clicking highlights neighbours" behaviour note, the Crown-parcel/Potential-Spots note, the full Wildlife Act buffer-distance writeup, and the personal-markup note) moved into a `LEGEND_INFO` lookup table and now live behind a small "i" button — reused, shrunk down (`.lg-i`, 14px vs. the usual 19px `.info-btn`), pinned to each row's right edge. Two notes that aren't tied to any single swatch (the neighbour-highlighting behaviour, personal markup) sit behind one general "i" next to the "Map key" header itself instead of being force-fit onto an unrelated row. The status-code glossary is still generated from `CUTBLOCK_STATUS_CODES` itself (so it can't drift out of sync with the report's own hover text) — it just builds into a variable now instead of writing straight into an always-visible div. The Crown-parcel note was also corrected while it was being moved: it referenced "the buttons above," which stopped being true once those buttons moved into the v71 left toolbar.

**Progress bar, actually visible.** The v71 progress bar was already technically firing correctly for both triggers — Playwright confirmed that at the time — but it lived inside the left toolbar's panel, which only shows content once `.open` is set. Pressing the Shooting Spots button "worked" by circumstance (you have to open the toolbar to reach the button in the first place); every other way of picking a location — a plain click, dragging the pin, search, typed coordinates — starts the automatic sweep without ever touching the toolbar, so the bar was updating correctly behind a collapsed, zero-width panel nobody could see. Fixed with one call, `openLeftToolbar()`, at the top of `runPotentialSpotsSearch()` itself (the shared core both triggers already run through), mirroring the app's own existing pattern for this exact problem — `openBottomPanel()` already does the same thing for the report drawer on every lookup, "since that's the whole point of clicking a spot." The toolbar stays open afterward rather than auto-closing, same as the bottom drawer and side checklist never auto-close either.

**Verification.** `node --check` passes clean; div/button balance holds (268/268, 55/55). A headless-browser Playwright run confirmed: every legend row's text is short (longest row measured 30 characters); all 13 "i" icons (12 per-row + the header one) are wired to `LEGEND_INFO` and open the right modal with the right content (spot-checked "Restricted" for the buffer numbers and "Cutblock colours" for the status-code glossary); and, with the toolbar explicitly closed beforehand, both `window.runLookup()` (the automatic-sweep path) and the Shooting Spots button genuinely open the toolbar and show the progress bar mid-run, not just update hidden DOM state. Zero JS errors.

## v71: Shooting Spots button is back, a left toolbar, a progress bar, and 50% transparency everywhere
Karim's ask, verbatim: *"Let's add a loading animation with percent finished and loading bar under clear potential spots. Let's also bring back the shooting spots button and put it in a collapsable tool bar on the left with the rest of the functions on the left. Add the crown spot button there too. Make the header and all collapsable elements 50% transparent."*

**The one real ambiguity, resolved before writing code.** "Bring back the shooting spots button" could have meant two different things: replace the v69/v70 automatic 2 km sweep with a fully manual button again, or add the button back *alongside* the automatic sweep as a second way to trigger the same underlying check. Getting this wrong meant real rework (the view-based CQL/zoom-gate code either needed to exist or not), so this was asked directly rather than guessed: Karim confirmed **both** — keep the automatic sweep, and have the button do what it did pre-v70 specifically (whatever's currently in view, not another 2 km circle).

**Shooting Spots button, restored.** Since there are genuinely two callers of the core search again (the automatic radius sweep and the view-based button), the `area`-parameterized shared-core design v68 first introduced and v70 collapsed away is back — `runPotentialSpotsSearch(area)`, called by `revealPotentialSpotsAroundPin(lat,lng)` (unchanged: `DWITHIN`, always runs, re-fits the map after) and the new `revealPotentialSpots()` (`INTERSECTS` against `map.getBounds()`, zoom-gated, doesn't move the map). Two zoom constants came back with it, at their pre-v70 values: `SPOT_MIN_ZOOM = 9` bails out entirely below that zoom (same idea as `CROWN_PARCEL_MIN_ZOOM`, one notch tighter since this feature does more per-parcel work); `INFRA_MIN_ZOOM = 12` gates only the road/building buffer sub-check — parcel/park/municipality checks still run below it, just unfiltered by the buffer, same "fail open but say so" note pattern the rest of this feature already uses for a failed query. The radius sweep passes `infraZoomOk: true` unconditionally (a fixed 2 km circle is never "too wide"). The gap-detection sampling loop's circular clipping (added in v68 so the purple gap layer reads as a true 2 km circle, not the ~4 km square its sampling grid is built from) is now conditional on a `circleClip` field in `area` — set for the radius sweep, `null` for the view-based button, whose "area" genuinely is the viewport rectangle it was asked to check.

**Left toolbar.** Crown Land, Shooting Spots, and Clear Potential Spots moved off the map canvas (where they sat as a small floating box, top-right, competing with the basemap toggle for space) into a new collapsible drawer docked to the left edge — a near-exact mirror of the existing right-side checklist drawer: a slim vertical "🛠️ Tools" tab that expands into a 250px panel on click, closable with its own "✕". Leaflet's own top-left corner (the zoom control and the personal-markup draw toolbar) got a matching `margin-left` clearance so the collapsed tab doesn't sit on top of them, the same fix v62 already applied on the right side for the checklist tab vs. the basemap/action buttons.

**Progress bar.** The Potential Spots search fires roughly 10 queries in parallel (parcel ×2, park, municipality, tenure, woodlot, cutblock ×2, road, Overpass buildings). Each one now updates a shared counter as it settles (success or failure — `queryLayer()`/`queryOsmBuildings()` don't actually reject, but the tracker handles both anyway), driving a bar + percent readout directly under "Clear Potential Spots" for the duration of the run, then hiding again once results are drawn.

**50% transparency.** The header and every collapsible panel — both drawers, the new left toolbar, the map key, and "My Markup" — now share one `rgba(255,255,255,.5)` background (the header's own dark navy gradient dropped from `.92` to `.5` alpha to match). The always-visible basemap toggle was left at its existing near-opaque look, since it isn't a collapsible panel.

**Verification.** `node --check` on the extracted script passes clean; div-balance holds at 252/252. A headless-browser Playwright run (the map/Leaflet stub extended with a Proxy-based map object this round, since a hand-rolled method list kept needing "just one more" call the app actually makes — `addControl`, `invalidateSize`, etc. — a catch-all `get` trap that returns a chainable no-op for anything unmodeled sidesteps that permanently) confirmed: the toolbar opens/closes; below zoom 9 the button fires zero queries and shows a "zoom in" note; at zoom 10 it runs but skips the buffer with the right note and fires no road/Overpass queries; at zoom 14 it fires a real `INTERSECTS` query, the progress bar shows during and hides after, and the note/Clear-button state update correctly; the automatic radius sweep (via `runLookup()`, simulating a plain map click) still fires a `DWITHIN` query and still re-fits the map; and the header/bottom drawer/left toolbar/side checklist all measured back at `rgba(255,255,255,.5)` (header via its gradient) in the browser's own computed styles. Zero JS errors in any of it. A screenshot of the open toolbar (buttons, progress bar mid-run, note text, translucent panel) matched the intended layout.

## v70: the actual bug behind v69, fixed -- and the "Shooting Spots" button is gone
Karim's report: *"It doesn't look like the pin click is doing the same thing as the shooting function. The idea is to get rid of the shooting spots button and just have both of them together in the pin click."*

**The bug.** v69 (below) wired the 2 km sweep into only the drag-and-drop 📌 header button's own `'drop'` event handler, on the theory that "the pin location feature" meant that one specific button. It didn't: a plain map click, dragging the location marker itself (v55), the address search result, and typing coordinates into Lat/Lng all call `runLookup()` directly and never touch that handler at all — so for every one of those (almost certainly what Karim was actually testing with, since the drag-header-button is the least-used of the five ways to pick a location) the sweep silently never ran. Confirmed with a headless-browser test that called `runLookup()` directly, the same way `map.on('click', ...)` does: before this fix, no sweep; after, one fires every time.

**The fix, and the simplification Karim asked for.** The sweep now lives inside `runLookup()` itself — the one function every location-picking path already converges on — so it fires automatically no matter how a location was picked, "on top of" the point check the way the original ask described. That made the "Shooting Spots" button and its whole v68 view-based code path genuinely redundant, so it's gone: the button, its info-modal blurb (rewritten instead of deleted, since the explanation is still useful — now describes the automatic behavior), the now-always-true zoom gate, and the `SPOT_MIN_ZOOM`/`INFRA_MIN_ZOOM` constants that gate no longer needed. With only one caller left, v68's generic `runPotentialSpotsSearch(area)` abstraction (built to share code between the button and the pin) was collapsed back into a single direct function — the abstraction's only reason to exist was supporting two call patterns that don't both exist anymore, and keeping it would have meant carrying dead flexibility (an always-null `btn`, an always-true `infraZoomOk`, an always-set `circleClip`) instead of just deleting it. "Clear Potential Spots" stays, since it's still useful for removing the drawn shapes without picking a new location, and the "Crown Land" button is untouched — Karim asked to remove Shooting Spots specifically, not Crown Land, which is a genuinely different, ownership-only, current-view-only query.

**UI.** The map-actions panel's "Shooting Spots" button row is now a plain label ("🎯 Potential spots — automatic") with the same "i" info icon beside it, and the panel's default note explains what happens automatically and how to trigger it (any of the five ways to pick a location) rather than describing a button to press.

**Verification.** `node --check` passes clean; div-balance check passes (245/245). A headless-browser test confirmed: `#revealSpotsBtn` no longer exists in the DOM, `window.revealPotentialSpots` (the old wrapper) is gone, and calling `runLookup()` directly — the exact function a plain map click invokes — now populates both the single-point report *and* the `mapActionsNote` sweep summary, shows the "Clear Potential Spots" button, and draws spots on the map, all with zero JS errors. This is the verification v69 below was still owed at the time it shipped (a tool outage blocked it that round) — it's done now, on the corrected implementation.

## v69: dropping the pin now also sweeps a 2 km radius for potential shooting spots
Karim's ask: *"For the pin location feature lets essentially take what the potential shooting feature does and apply it to a 2 km radius when a pin is placed down on top of what it already does."*

**What changed.** Dropping the header pin (v67) already ran the normal single-point check (`runLookup()`). It now *also* fires the same qualification logic "Shooting Spots" runs from its button — parcel ownership, park/municipality exclusion, tenure/woodlot/forestry advisories, the road/building buffer, and the "no ParcelMap record" gap layer — scoped to a fixed 2 km radius around wherever the pin lands, with no button press and no zoom requirement. Both checks fire at the same time (not one after the other) so dropping a pin isn't slower than it needs to be.

**Root-cause-first refactor, not a copy-paste.** "Shooting Spots" was a single ~330-line function built entirely around `map.getBounds()` — the current viewport rectangle, gated behind a minimum zoom so an accidentally huge query can't run. Duplicating that whole function for a second, pin-scoped copy would have meant two near-identical places to fix every future bug. Instead, the actual qualification logic (parcel classification, gap detection, cutblock buffers, the status-note wording) was pulled into one shared `runPotentialSpotsSearch(area)`, parameterized by an `area` describing only the two things that genuinely differ between "current view" and "2 km around a pin": how each layer's live query is spatially scoped, and whether the road/building buffer needs a zoom gate at all (a fixed-radius sweep is always the same physical size, so it always runs the full check — there's no "too wide, zoom in" case for it the way there is for a viewport the user controls by zooming). The "Shooting Spots" button and the new pin sweep are now two short wrappers calling the same core.

**Genuinely circular, not a square pretending to be one.** The pin sweep queries BC's WFS layers with `DWITHIN(geometry, point, 2000, meters)` — a real circular radius query at the server, the same convention this file already uses for every proximity ("dwithin") layer — rather than approximating "2 km radius" with a same-size bounding square. The one place a literal rectangle is unavoidable is the two client-side pieces that only understand one (the OpenStreetMap building-footprint query, and the gap-detection sampling grid) — both are still built from a circle-containing square, but the gap-detection sample loop now also drops any point farther than 2 km from the pin, so the visible purple "no record" layer reads as a true circle too. The building query being allowed to run slightly generous at the square's corners is harmless — same "expand the bbox pre-filter so nothing real gets missed at the edge" reasoning this file already applies elsewhere (`pBboxExp`).

**Map view.** Unlike the button (which never re-centers, since the viewport already *is* the area it just checked), the pin sweep re-fits the map to the full 2 km search area once results are in, via `map.fitBounds()` — otherwise a sweep could easily draw spots well outside whatever was on screen at drop time. Worth flagging: this fit and `runLookup()`'s own tighter fit-to-parcel both fire concurrently, so whichever of the two finishes last wins the final camera position — in practice that's almost always the wider 2 km fit, since it's doing more queries, and it's a strict superset of the parcel view either way, but it isn't strictly guaranteed. Not worth engineering around (forcing a fixed order would either slow down the point-check or drop its useful auto-zoom-to-parcel behavior for every *other* way of picking a location), so it's called out here rather than left silent.

**Verification.** Manual review of the refactor was thorough (traced every reference to the old `bounds`/`viewportBboxCql`/`infraZoomOk` local variables to its new `area.*` equivalent) given this session's Bash tool was intermittently unavailable (a temporary environment issue, not specific to this file) right when the automated headless-browser test and `node --check` syntax pass would normally run — those are still owed as a follow-up the moment the tool is available again, before this is considered fully verified the way v65-v68 were.

## v68: Wildlife Management Areas auto-check + location-legality manual items, from the discharge-checklist research
Karim's ask: *"Don't worry about the logistics of firearms itself I'm only concerned about location legality. With that let's start implementing that into the app."* — following three rounds of research building the standalone BC discharge-legality checklist (see `bc-discharge-legal-checklist.md`), this scopes that research down to **location-legality items only** (excluding firearm classification/PAL/minors/storage/careless-use, which stay reference-only in the checklist doc) and implements the relevant findings directly into the app's own auto-check and manual-checklist logic.

**New 9th AUTO check: Wildlife Management Areas.** Added `WHSE_TANTALIS.TA_WILDLIFE_MGMT_AREAS_SVW` (B.C. Reg 161/87, confirmed real WFS layer/fields via DescribeFeatureType) as a new `LAYERS.wma` entry, queried alongside every other layer in `runLookup()`. Drawn on the map with the same dashed-outline, non-exclusionary treatment as Crown tenures/woodlots (not the filled hard-exclusion treatment Parks get) — deliberately, since a WMA overlap doesn't ban discharge by itself the way a Park does; some WMAs carry their own gazetted Wildlife Act access/discharge restrictions and some don't touch shooting at all. A hit shows as an **amber** advisory in both the map popup and a new dedicated report card, prompting a manual check of that specific WMA's Order in Council rather than a red verdict-changer. New 9th row added to the AUTO checklist section, matching the existing 8-row pattern.

**Manual checklist items updated/added, sourced from the discharge-checklist research:**
- `wildlife-act` item's text now also covers the **Public Access Prohibition Regulation** (B.C. Reg 187/2003) — confirmed to exist but with no province-wide spatial/WFS dataset found (checked; BC's Motor Vehicle Prohibition Regulation dataset, which the app already auto-checks, is a different, motor-vehicle-only thing), so this stays manual-only. Reworded so it no longer redundantly re-mentions WMAs now that those have their own AUTO check above.
- `closures` item's text now explicitly names **Wildfire Regulation s.2.2 high-risk-activity restrictions** (a fire centre can restrict specific activities like exploding targets without closing the whole area — the Kamloops Fire Centre has done exactly this) as a concrete example, not just generic area closures.
- New `access` item: **lawful access to the site itself** — even a legal Crown-land destination can require crossing private or leased land to reach, which is trespass under BC's Trespass Act regardless of the destination's own legality; flagged as manual-only since the app can only assess the clicked point/area, not the access route to it.

**Deliberately left out of this round** (per Karim's scoping): firearm classification (restricted/prohibited-only-on-ranges), PAL, minors/supervision, safe transport/storage, careless-use conduct rules, and litter/pack-out — these are location-legality-adjacent but not location-legality itself, and remain reference-only in the standalone discharge checklist, not wired into the app's own logic.

**Verification.** Full-file syntax check (`node --check` on the extracted script) and div-balance check (244/244) both pass clean. Headless-browser exercise of `runLookup()` against a stubbed WMA feature confirmed: the new report card renders with the WMA name and advisory language, the 9th AUTO checklist row shows the "yes — <name> (advisory only...)" text, and a separate run against an empty WMA response confirmed the "no overlap" / "no." states render correctly too. Also confirmed the updated `wildlife-act` and `closures` manual items' "More" text contains the new Public Access Prohibition Regulation / Wildfire Regulation s.2.2 / Kamloops Fire Centre language, and the new `access` item's text contains the Trespass Act reference. Zero JS errors from the app itself in either run.

## v67: shorter map-action button labels + their own "i", Clear button appears on demand, drag-to-place pin
Karim's asks, in one round: *"Change 'Reveal Crown Land (This View)' to 'Crown Land' and put an 'I' icon next to it to open a description of what it does. Do the same with the shooting spot button but change the name to 'Shooting spots'. Only have the clear button pop up after shooting spots has been pressed. Create a button on the header where you can click and drag a pin to where you want it to go."*

**Button renames + info icons.** "Reveal Crown Land (this view)" → **"Crown Land"**, "Show Potential Spots (this view)" → **"Shooting Spots"**. Each now has its own small "i" button beside it (the same pattern used everywhere else on this page) that opens a modal explaining what the button actually does — the parenthetical "(this view)" and the viewport-scoped/press-again behavior that used to be baked into the label now lives there instead, along with a fuller explanation of what each button excludes/includes. `.ma-row` is a small new flex wrapper so the button still fills the available width with the icon fixed beside it.

**Clear button, on demand.** "Clear Potential Spots" now starts hidden (`hidden` attribute) and only appears once Shooting Spots has actually run past its zoom gate and started drawing something — not merely on button press, since a press that bails out early (view too wide) draws nothing new and showing a clear button for it would be misleading. Clicking Clear hides the button again along with removing the shapes, so it's only ever visible when there's actually something on the map to clear.

**Drag-to-place pin.** A new 📌 button sits in the header next to the address-search icon — drag it and drop it anywhere on the map to check that exact spot, a third way to pick a location alongside clicking the map and typing/searching coordinates. Built on the plain HTML5 drag-and-drop API (`draggable="true"`, `dragstart`/`dragover`/`drop`) — no extra library, and no manual "ghost pin follows the cursor" tracking, since the browser already renders a drag image for a draggable element for free. The drop handler converts the drop's pixel position to a lat/lng via Leaflet's own `containerPointToLatLng()` and feeds it straight into the same `runLookup()` every other location-picking method already uses — deliberately no `map.setView()` call here, since wherever the pin lands is by definition already on screen.

**Verification.** Full-file syntax and div-balance checks pass clean (236/236, no dangling ids). Exercised end-to-end in a headless browser: confirmed both buttons show their new short labels, both info modals open with the right title/content, `clearSpotsBtn` starts hidden and only appears once the reveal path reaches its "draw" step, disappears again after Clear is pressed, and a synthetic drag-and-drop onto the map correctly invokes `runLookup()`. Zero JS errors throughout. A screenshot confirmed the map-actions panel and header both lay out cleanly with the new elements in place.

## v66: map key no longer extends off-screen -- root-caused, not just patched
Karim's report: *"Map key seems to extend outside the elements."*

**Root cause.** Not a text-wrapping/overflow bug (checked first, since it followed a font swap) -- it's a missing height cap. The map key has grown to 27+ rows plus several long note paragraphs over the life of this project (flagged as a growing risk back in v62: "worth raising if Karim ever finds himself scrolling through it more than glancing"). With no `max-height` anywhere on `.lg-body`, opening the full key rendered it roughly **2,270px tall** -- confirmed by measuring the real markup and CSS in an actual browser (with the real Space Grotesk font loaded, real viewport size, not guessed). Since the panel is anchored to the *bottom* of the screen (`bottom:66px`, so it grows upward as it gets taller), against a typical ~900px-tall window that pushed roughly 1,440px of it above the top of the browser viewport -- everything past the first handful of rows was rendering off-screen and unreachable. This was always going to happen once the key grew past a certain length; the font change didn't cause it, just wasn't the trigger either -- it had simply been growing toward this point for a while.

**Fix.** `.lg-body.open` now has `max-height:calc(100vh - var(--tb-h) - 110px)` (reserving space for the header's live-tracked height plus this element's own bottom offset) with `overflow-y:auto` and `overscroll-behavior:contain`, so the key scrolls *within its own box* once it's taller than the available space, instead of pushing itself off the top of the screen. The panel's position and collapsed-by-default behavior are unchanged -- this only bounds how tall it's allowed to get once opened.

**Verification.** Confirmed in a real browser (not the sandboxed headless stand-in used elsewhere in this project, since that has no network access to load the real Google Font) at two viewport heights -- the full key now fits entirely on-screen (`top >= 0` and `bottom <= viewport height`) at both 900px and a notably short 650px window, and its internal scroll actually moves content (tested via `scrollTop`). Full-file syntax and div-balance checks both pass clean.

## v65: switched to Space Grotesk, no system fallback
Karim's ask: *"Let's do Space Grotesk but I don't want a system fallback."*

Replaced the v64 Mulish Google Fonts link with Space Grotesk (weights 300/400/500/600/700) and set body's `font-family` to `"Space Grotesk"` alone — no Arial/system-font stack behind it, per Karim's explicit instruction. This is a deliberate departure from this app's usual defensive-fallback habit (see the Leaflet CDN handling in v61): if the Google Fonts request is ever slow or blocked, text now falls back to whatever default the browser itself picks, not a chosen stack. One cosmetic note for the record: Space Grotesk's heaviest available weight on Google Fonts is 700, while a handful of existing `font-weight:800` rules on this page (the "Data sources" hover states, etc.) will render at whatever weight the browser substitutes -- almost certainly 700, not a real functional issue. Verified: div-balance and full-script syntax checks both pass clean.

## v64: typeface switched to match backroadstatus.com
Karim's ask: *"Let's switch the font to the font backroadstatus.com has."*

Inspected backroadstatus.com's actual computed styles (its body font-family, not just guessed from appearance) and found it loads **Mulish** from Google Fonts (weights 300/400/700), falling back to Arial. Added the same Google Fonts `<link>` to this app's `<head>` and put `"Mulish"` first in the body's font stack, keeping the existing system-font fallback chain (`-apple-system, Segoe UI, Roboto, Helvetica, Arial, sans-serif`) behind it so the app still reads fine if that Google Fonts request is ever slow or blocked — a missing font is a cosmetic downgrade here, not the functional break a missing Leaflet load would be (see v61), so no fatal-guard handling was needed for it. The `.info-btn`'s deliberate serif "i" glyph (Georgia/Times New Roman) was left untouched — that's an intentional distinct style for the icon buttons, not the general body font. Verified: div-balance and full-script syntax checks both pass clean after the change.

## v63: address / place-name search
Karim's ask: chose "Address/place search" from a menu of candidate next-feature directions, then approved building it ("Yes add it").

**What it does.** A new 🔎 icon button sits in the top bar next to the Lat/Lng inputs. Clicking it opens a modal with a text field and a "Search" button (also triggered by Enter); typing an address, town, or place name and searching geocodes it via **Nominatim**, OpenStreetMap's free search API (no API key needed). Matches come back as a clickable list — picking one runs the exact same `map.setView(...)` + `runLookup(...)` path as clicking the map or pressing "Check this location", so everything downstream (marker, report, checklist) behaves identically regardless of how the location was chosen.

**Design decisions, and why:**
- **Nominatim over a keyed geocoder** (e.g. Google/Mapbox geocoding) — no signup, no key to manage, no cost, consistent with the rest of the app's "free public data, no backend" approach. Trade-off: Nominatim's match quality for sparse rural BC addresses can be rougher than a commercial geocoder, and its usage policy asks for modest request volume — both fine for a personal-use tool.
- **Biased, not restricted, to BC** — the request adds `viewbox=<BC's bounding box>` with `bounded=0`, so results *near* BC rank first but a search for somewhere outside BC still returns something, rather than silently coming back empty. `countrycodes=ca` keeps results within Canada.
- **Explicit "Search" button, not live-as-you-type** — Nominatim's usage policy asks for roughly 1 request/second and no autocomplete-style rapid querying; a deliberate search action per lookup respects that, an input-driven query-per-keystroke would not.
- **A results *list*, not "jump to the top hit"** — place names are often ambiguous in BC (more than one "Bridge Lake", plenty of duplicate road names across regions), so an unreviewed top-hit jump risked silently taking Karim to the wrong location. Showing every match's full display name and letting him click the right one avoids that.
- **Icon button + modal, not a new permanent header input** — this app's header has a real history of wrapping and overlapping at narrow widths once it accumulates too much permanent content (v58, v60). Adding another always-visible text input was avoided in favour of the same "icon opens a modal" pattern already used everywhere else on this page (disclaimer, sources, coordinate help). Confirmed via headless-browser screenshots at 1440/820/390px that the new button doesn't reintroduce any wrapping/overlap at any of them.

**Verification.** Full-file syntax check and div-balance check passed clean (228/228, no dangling `getElementById` targets). `buildNominatimSearchUrl()` — the pure URL-building logic — was extracted and unit-tested in isolation (8/8 assertions: correct endpoint, query encoding, `countrycodes=ca`, `bounded=0`, `limit`, `format`, viewbox present, handles a numeric place name). End-to-end behavior was exercised in a headless browser with Nominatim's response mocked (real network access to it isn't available from this sandbox, same limitation as the CDN blocks noted in v61): opening the modal, searching, rendering two mock results, clicking one, confirming the modal closes and the Lat/Lng fields update to the selected coordinates. Zero real JS exceptions (`pageerror`) at any point in that flow.

## v62: self-review pass — basemap-toggle/map-actions overlap with the checklist tab, fixed
Karim's ask, after picking address search as the next feature: *"Review your own work and flag anything you'd fix before I ship this."*

**What the review covered.** Re-ran the full syntax/id/div-balance check (clean). Then, since the last several rounds were all layout-adjacent (v58/v60's header fixes), ran the real app in a headless browser (with a generic stand-in for Leaflet so it doesn't depend on network access) across several window widths — 1440, 1280, 1024, 820, and a phone-width 390 — and looked at the actual rendered screenshots rather than just trusting the code, since the header-overlap bugs earlier this session were exactly the kind of thing that's obvious in a screenshot and easy to miss by reading CSS alone.

**What that surfaced.** The basemap toggle (Satellite/Street/Topo) and the map-action buttons (Reveal Crown Land / Show Potential Spots / Clear Potential Spots) have both been pinned `right:10px` from the viewport edge — and the collapsed side-checklist tab, flush to that same edge at roughly 32px wide, has sat directly on top of that 10-32px strip this whole time. Confirmed with exact bounding-box measurements: a constant ~23px overlap at every width tested, not something that only appears when the window gets narrow — this predates any of this session's changes (the checklist tab dates to v41, the Topo button to v36) and isn't something the header-height work touched or introduced. In practice it clipped the right edge of the "Topo" label and the top portion of the map-action button box under the checklist tab.

**Fixed.** Both `.basemap-toggle` and `.map-actions` moved from `right:10px` to `right:48px` (10px original margin + the tab's ~32px width + a small gap), clearing the tab with room to spare. Confirmed via the same bounding-box check across all five widths: a clean ~15px gap everywhere now, and a screenshot at 1280px confirms "Topo" reads in full with visible space before the checklist tab. This doesn't touch the checklist's *open* state, where its much wider panel covering this same area is expected drawer behavior, not a bug.

**Flagged, not changed — worth Karim's own call.** The map legend has grown to 27 rows over the course of this project's history. It's still collapsed by default (v22), so it doesn't cost anything until opened, but it's genuinely long once it is. Not touched this round since trimming or reorganizing it is a real editorial decision (what's still worth a legend entry vs. what's redundant with a popup) rather than a bug fix — worth raising if Karim ever finds himself scrolling through it more than glancing.

**Verification.** Full-file syntax check and div-balance check passed clean. The overlap fix itself was verified empirically (bounding-box measurements before/after across 5 widths, plus a visual screenshot), which is a stronger check for a pure-CSS layout fix than a Node unit test would be — there's no function here to extract and assert against, the "test" is the actual rendered geometry.

## v61: a real error message instead of a silent blank map when Leaflet fails to load
Karim's report: *"The app seems to be blank, no map showing up."*

**Investigation.** This app loads Leaflet and leaflet.draw live from cdnjs.cloudflare.com (see `<head>`) — there was no local copy to fall back to, and no code anywhere that checked whether those loads actually succeeded. Since this session's own sandboxed network blocks that exact CDN (a policy restriction of the environment this work happens in, unrelated to Karim's own network), the real page couldn't be loaded end-to-end for direct testing — so this was investigated with two more targeted checks instead of guessing: (1) the full-file syntax/id/div-balance pass (clean, as every round), and (2) actually running the app's real script in a headless browser with a generic "swallow everything, never throw" stand-in for the `L` object standing in for Leaflet (so the test doesn't depend on cdnjs at all) — confirmed the app's own code runs start-to-finish with zero errors and correct layout (including v60's one-line header). That combination rules out a bug in this app's own logic as the cause and points squarely at Leaflet itself failing to load in whatever conditions Karim hit — most plausibly a transient CDN hiccup, or a network/ad-blocker filtering cdnjs.cloudflare.com. Confirmed directly: intentionally blocking just the Leaflet script request reproduces exactly what Karim described -- every other UI element (header, buttons, checklist tab, bottom drawer) renders completely normally, while the map area is silently empty, with only a raw, easy-to-miss `ReferenceError: L is not defined` in the browser console and nothing at all visible on the page itself.

**Fixed the underlying gap, not just this one instance.** Whatever caused this specific occurrence, the app had zero visible failure mode for it — worth fixing regardless, since a live CDN dependency can fail for all kinds of reasons outside this app's control, and "blank page, no explanation" is the worst possible outcome for anyone who hits it. Both Leaflet `<script>` tags now carry an `onerror` handler that flags the failure the instant it happens; a guard placed at the exact first line that actually needs Leaflet (`const map = L.map(...)`) checks for that flag (or `L` simply being undefined) and, if either is true, replaces the map area with a clear, styled on-page message explaining what happened (a live CDN dependency didn't load, here's why that might be, try reloading) instead of continuing on to a bare, silent `ReferenceError`. Everything declared *before* that point in the script — the header-height sync (v58), the About/disclaimer/checklist-toggle buttons, the new coordinate-info button (v60) — still works even if Leaflet itself never loads, since none of it actually depends on `L`.

**Same gap existed for leaflet.draw, fixed the same way.** leaflet.draw is a second, separate script tag that can fail independently of core Leaflet — since nothing after its setup code (`new L.Control.Draw(...)`) referenced the resulting variable, a failure there would have silently skipped every remaining line in the entire script (location lookups, Potential Spots, the checklist, all of it) for a failure that only actually affects the personal-markup drawing toolbar. That setup is now wrapped in a check for `L.Control.Draw`/`L.Draw` actually existing; if they don't, the app logs one console warning and simply skips the drawing toolbar, leaving the map and every other feature fully working.

**Verification.** Full-file syntax check, `getElementById` id-resolution check, and a div-balance check all passed clean (59 declared ids / 48 used / 0 missing, no duplicates; 221/221 div tags, depth never negative). Ran the real app script twice in a headless browser: once with the Leaflet stand-in succeeding (zero errors, map container sized correctly, one-line header confirmed by screenshot) and once with the real Leaflet script request deliberately blocked (confirmed via screenshot: the new on-page error message renders correctly in place of the map, the header and all its buttons stay fully visible and functional, and the only console output is the intentional, descriptive `throw` this round added — not a bare `ReferenceError`).

**Not yet field-confirmed** — whether this was in fact a transient CDN/network issue on Karim's end (which this fix can't prevent, only explain clearly when it happens) is worth him trying again; if the blank-map symptom recurs, the new on-page message should now say so directly instead of leaving it a mystery.

## v60: the header's coordinate-usage blurb moved behind an "i" icon
Karim's report: *"The header is still wrapped improperly just put the blurb beside the coordinates function in an 'I' icon."*

**Why v58's fix wasn't enough on its own.** v58 made the checklist drawer (and the map buttons) track the header's *real* rendered height instead of a hardcoded guess, so they'd stop sitting under it once it wrapped to two lines — but it never addressed *why* the header was wrapping in the first place. `#coordDisplay`'s permanent inline text ("No location selected yet — click the map or enter coordinates. Once placed, the pin can be dragged to move it, even over Potential Spots shapes.", added in v55) is long enough on its own, combined with the title, coordinate inputs, Check button, and disclaimer button already sharing the same row, that the bar kept wrapping regardless -- v58 stopped that wrap from *overlapping* other things, but the wrap, and the header taking up more screen than it needed to, was still happening.

**The actual fix: shrink the header's permanent content.** The long explanatory sentence moved into a new "i" button (`coordInfoBtn`) sitting right next to the Lat/Lng inputs -- same "high-yield text inline, full detail behind an 'i'" pattern this app already uses everywhere else (the header's own `headerInfoBtn`, every map popup's detail button, etc.). `#coordDisplay` itself now just shows a short status line ("No location selected yet." / "Checking …" / "Last checked: …"), which is far less likely to force a wrap on its own. v58's ResizeObserver-driven `--tb-h` fix stays in place as a safety net underneath this -- if the bar ever does wrap again for some other reason (a narrow window, browser zoom, a future addition), the checklist and map buttons still won't overlap it -- but this is the fix that addresses the wrapping itself, not just its consequence.

**Verification.** Full-file syntax check, `getElementById` id-resolution check, and a div-balance check all passed clean (59 declared ids / 47 used / 0 missing, no duplicate id attributes; 216/216 div tags, depth never negative).

**Not yet field-confirmed** — whether the header now reliably stays on one line across Karim's own real window sizes is worth his own look; if it still wraps somewhere, the next lever would be trimming `.tb-controls`' remaining content (the disclaimer button is the next-largest single item) rather than another pass at the checklist's own offsets, which are already root-caused as far as v58 could take them.

## v59: purple gap fill matched to teal's transparency
Karim's request: *"Reduce the purple fill colour transparency to 0.16 as well."*

Straightforward follow-on to v57's teal fillOpacity reduction (0.35 → 0.16): `gapStyle()`'s purple "no parcel record, presumed Crown" fill drops from 0.3 to the same 0.16, so Potential Spots' two "this looks viable" colors (teal = confirmed Crown/untitled, purple = no record at all) read as the same visual weight instead of the purple looking noticeably more solid than the teal next to it. The restricted-red fill (0.4) and the two cutblock-related fills are untouched — not asked for, and each has its own reasoning already on record (see v52 and v57).

**Verification.** Full-file syntax check passed clean (all 3 script blocks OK). A one-line, self-contained style-constant change with no logic behind it -- not worth a separate unit-test round on top of that.

## v58: header/checklist overlap fixed at the root; side roads now drawn in Potential Spots
Karim's request: *"The header is also overlapping on the check list lets fix that. As well lets have all side roads highlighted when using the potential shooting spot function."*

**Header/checklist overlap — root cause.** The fixed top bar (`.top-bar`) uses `flex-wrap:wrap`, so it grows to two lines whenever its contents don't fit one row. The side checklist drawer's own offsets (`.sc-tab`'s `margin-top`, `.sc-head`'s `padding-top`) — and, it turns out, the map's basemap-toggle and map-action buttons too — were all hardcoded to 64/66/110px: baked-in guesses at the header's *single-line* height, not anything that actually tracked it. The header's `coordDisplay` text got noticeably longer in v55 ("...the pin can be dragged to move it, even over Potential Spots shapes."), which is enough on plenty of ordinary window widths to push the bar onto two lines — at that point the header is taller than any of those hardcoded numbers ever assumed, so it visually sits on top of the checklist's top edge (and, less visibly until now, could do the same to the map buttons).

**Fixed at the root, not with another magic number.** A `syncHeaderHeight()` function keeps a `--tb-h` CSS custom property in sync with the top bar's *actual* rendered height via a `ResizeObserver` on `#topBar` (with a `window resize` fallback for engines without `ResizeObserver`), firing once on load and again on every future height change from any cause — window resize, content getting longer again, even a font-rendering difference. Every offset that used to guess the header's height (`.sc-tab`, `.sc-head`, `.basemap-toggle`, `.map-actions`, `.leaflet-top`) now reads from `var(--tb-h)` via `calc()` instead. This can't quietly recur the way the original bug did — there's no fixed number left to go stale.

**Side roads, now actually drawn.** Potential Spots was already fetching every DRA (Digital Road Atlas — BC's general public road network, separate from the FTEN forest-service-road layer the single-click report already draws) road in view, purely to compute the road buffer behind the scenes; the roads themselves were never rendered, so the buffer's effect was only visible as its consequence (a red excluded strip) with no way to see the road that caused it. `renderNearbyRoads()` now draws every one of those roads as a line — slate (`#546e7a`) by default, a bolder rust (`#b1440e`) for a listed major highway where the wider 400/600m buffer applies (`SPECIAL_400M_ROUTES`, from v25) — with a hover tooltip giving the road's class and exactly which buffer distance applies, same sticky-tooltip pattern (`road-tooltip`) the forest-service-road layer already uses elsewhere. Gated behind the same zoom level (`INFRA_MIN_ZOOM`) the road buffer itself already requires, since that's exactly when this data is fetched; the status note now says how many roads were drawn, and the map legend got two new rows for the two road colors.

**Verification.** Full-file syntax check, `getElementById` id-resolution check, and a div-balance check all passed clean (58 declared ids / 46 used / 0 missing; 214/214 div tags, depth never negative). Confirmed by grep that no leftover hardcoded header-relative offset (`margin-top:64px`, `padding:64px`, `top:66px`, `top:110px`, `margin-top:56px`) remains anywhere in the stylesheet. 18 Node `vm` assertions against the actual extracted `draRoadStyle()` and `draRoadTooltip()`: the default and special-route styles use visually distinct colors and weights; the tooltip shows the road class, route number (only when present), and correct buffer distance; a special route's tooltip includes its named-highway label and the "listed major highway" callout, a non-special route's doesn't; missing/null `props` doesn't throw and falls back to a generic "Road" label; and a road-class value containing markup-significant characters comes back HTML-escaped rather than injected raw. All 18 passed.

**Not yet field-confirmed** — the general standing caveat: whether the header now reliably clears the checklist across real window sizes (rather than just the specific two-line case reasoned through here), and how the new road lines read visually at different zoom levels/road densities, are worth Karim's own look.

## v57: no more silent gaps — restricted portions are drawn red, with a reason
Karim's request: *"In shooting spot finder there should not be any gaps in parcels. If they are excluded/within the radius of restricted areas set out in the rules they should be highlighted red and a reason given why (if a parcel partial sits in a restricted area just the portion sitting in that area should be highlighted red not the entire parcel, the portion of parcel not in restricted area should be the same colour as other allowed areas). Also let's make the parcels highlighted in that function more transparent."*

**What "gap" actually meant.** Potential Spots only ever queried Crown/untitled parcels, so a candidate parcel that overlapped a park or a municipal boundary was checked with a single whole-parcel `geomsIntersect()` test and, if it hit, dropped from the map *entirely* — nothing drawn, no popup, no visual difference from a spot that was never queried in the first place. This is exactly the "what does it mean when parcels are not highlighted" question Karim asked back in v53; the honest answer at the time was "not a bug, it's excluded" — but an invisible exclusion with no on-map explanation is a real usability gap, not just a surprising-but-correct result. The road/building/private-land buffer (v24/v25) already had a *better* answer to the same problem — it grid-samples each parcel and only clips out the specific portion within the buffer distance, keeping the rest — but that machinery was never extended to park/municipality overlap, so the two hard-exclusion rules behaved completely differently from each other.

**The fix: one unified per-point classifier for every hard-exclusion rule.** `classifyParcelRestrictions()` replaces the old `clipParcelByInfrastructure()` (same grid-sampling approach, same adaptive spacing) but checks park overlap, municipality overlap, and — when zoomed in enough — the road and building/private-land buffers all at once, for every sample point in a parcel, and records *which* of those applied at each point (a point can be tagged with more than one reason at once, e.g. both inside a municipality and too close to a road). A parcel now comes back as one of three shapes:
- **Fully allowed** — drawn as one teal shape, same as before.
- **Fully restricted** — drawn as one red shape (previously: nothing at all), with every reason that applied anywhere in it available on click.
- **Mixed** — split into small squares, teal for the allowed portion and red for the restricted portion, each red square carrying only the reason(s) that actually apply at that specific spot — so a parcel that's part park, part clear shows exactly where the line falls, not a guess.

Nothing is ever silently omitted any more. Every reason maps to a plain-language explanation (`restrictionReasonLabel()`) shown in a "🚫 Restricted" popup with the same "high-yield line, full detail behind an 'i'" pattern the teal spots already use (`restrictedPopup()`/`restrictedDetails`, mirroring `spotPopup()`/`spotDetails`).

**Color choice, deliberate.** The restricted-portion red (`#c0392b`) reuses this app's existing "prohibited / closed / private" red — the same one already used for private-parcel fill and the click report's park-prohibited wording — rather than inventing a new one, so the meaning ("you can't shoot here, and here's the specific rule") stays consistent with how red already reads everywhere else in the app. It's kept visually distinct from `CUTBLOCK_WARN_COLOR` (`#d13438`, the *separate*, non-exclusionary active-cutblock-proximity warning added in v45–v54) deliberately: cutblock proximity is still advisory, not a hard exclude, so it stays its own shaded-zone-plus-outline feature, untouched by this round. That's a scoping call worth being explicit about: Karim's phrase "restricted areas set out in the rules" could be read to include the cutblock buffer too, but this app has treated cutblock proximity as a "check before you go" advisory since v45 (deliberately, at Karim's own direction — nearby Crown land very often has *some* forestry activity nearby, and excluding on it would leave almost nothing shown), so it was left out of the new hard-exclusion/red system. Worth flagging in case that's not the read Karim intended.

**More transparent teal, as asked.** `spotStyle()`'s fill opacity dropped from 0.35 to 0.16 — a bigger cut than a first guess might make, chosen because red restricted shapes are now drawn far more often (right next to teal ones, sometimes touching), so a less-opaque teal keeps the map readable rather than two solid-looking colors competing for attention next to each other. The restricted-red fill was set to 0.4 (more solid than teal, on purpose — it's meant to read as the more attention-grabbing signal of the two) and can be tuned in either direction on request.

**Status note rewritten to match.** The bottom bar's after-run summary used to report "excluded (overlaps a park)" etc. for parcels that vanished; it now reports how many parcels are shown teal (and how many of those are only partially teal), how many are shown fully red, and a breakdown of how many parcels each restriction reason touched — phrased as "touching a park" / "touching a municipality" etc. rather than "excluded," since a parcel counted there is still on the map, just (in whole or in part) marked as off-limits.

**Verification.** Full-file syntax check, `getElementById` id-resolution check, and a div-balance check all passed clean (58 declared ids / 45 used / 0 missing; 211/211 div tags, depth never negative). 34 Node `vm` assertions against the actual extracted `classifyParcelRestrictions()`, `restrictionReasonLabel()`, and `restrictionReasonShort()`: a parcel fully clear of every rule comes back fully allowed; a parcel fully covered by a park (or, separately, a municipality) comes back fully restricted with the correct reason tag; a parcel half-covered by a park splits correctly, with every allowed square genuinely testing clear of that park geometry; a point covered by both a park and a municipality at once carries both reason tags; the road and building buffers only ever contribute a reason when `infraChecked` is true, and are cleanly skipped (not just weakly applied) when it's false; a degenerate (ringless) geometry falls back to fully-restricted rather than being silently dropped, matching the "no gaps" intent even in that edge case; and every reason code has a real, non-empty, correctly-worded label and short form. All 34 passed.

**Not yet field-confirmed** — the general standing caveat: whether the specific opacity/color choices read well in practice, especially with several red causes stacked in one dense view, is worth Karim's own look at a real spot before calling the tuning final.

## v56: Potential Spots runs faster
Karim's request: *"How can we make the potential shooting spots function faster?"*

Two concrete bottlenecks were found by reading the actual code path Potential Spots runs, not by guessing — both fixed, both accuracy-neutral (neither changes which spots get shown or how they're classified, only how long that takes to compute).

**1. The gap-detection sweep was doing a full point-in-polygon walk against every parcel, for every sample point, with no cheap way to skip the obviously-irrelevant ones.** That sweep (added in v44, the "no ParcelMap record at all" logic) checks up to ~500 sample points across the view against up to 3,000 parcel records (`GAP_PARCEL_MAX_FEATURES`) to find genuinely empty gaps. `pointInAnyGeom()`, the function doing that checking, called the expensive ring-walk (`pointInGeometry()`, which loops every vertex of every ring) for *every* point/parcel pair — even for a parcel on the other side of the view from the point being tested. Other intersection checks already in this app (`ringsIntersect`, for the road/building buffer clipping) bbox-prefilter before doing their expensive check; this one didn't. Fixed by precomputing each parcel's bounding box once (`bboxOfGeom()`, already existed and was already used elsewhere), then having `pointInAnyGeom()` do a cheap 4-comparison bbox containment check (`bboxContainsPoint()`, new) before ever calling `pointInGeometry()`. Same true/false answer for every point — a parcel's bbox containing a point is necessary (though not sufficient) for the point to genuinely be inside that parcel, so nothing that would have matched before stops matching now — just far fewer expensive ring-walks, especially in a parcel-dense view (urban/suburban lots), which is exactly where this sweep was slowest.

**2. The Crown-parcel query was needlessly run alone before the other six-to-eight layer queries, creating an avoidable network waterfall.** The original code awaited the parcel query by itself, then fired parks/municipalities/tenures/woodlots/cutblocks/cutblock-plans/gap-detection-parcels/roads/buildings as a batch via `Promise.all` only after the parcel query finished. The reasoning at the time (never written down, reconstructed from the code's shape) was presumably to avoid competing for the browser's per-domain connection limit — but that reasoning doesn't actually hold up, because the batch of other queries already fires up to 8 requests at the same DataBC host concurrently on its own. Adding the parcel query as a 9th concurrent request changes nothing about connection contention; running it separately just meant its full round-trip time was paid *in addition to*, not *overlapping with*, every other query's round-trip, on every single successful run. Fixed by merging it into the same `Promise.all` batch as everything else. Since a government WFS server's response time dominates this feature's wall-clock time far more than the browser-side computation afterward (fix #1 notwithstanding), overlapping that round-trip instead of stacking it is the single biggest lever on how long a Potential Spots run takes.

**Trade-off, disclosed rather than left implicit.** On the rare run where the parcel query itself fails, the other 6-8 queries — now already in flight alongside it — still run to completion before the failure message shows, instead of bailing out immediately the way the old sequential version did. That's a slightly slower failure path in exchange for a faster success path on every run, successful or not. Worth it, but real.

**Ideas considered but not implemented this round** (lower priority, or need Karim's input before touching):
- Batching the many individually-added, non-interactive Leaflet layers (buffer-zone squares, perimeter-line segments) into fewer `L.geoJSON()` calls — a rendering-side optimization, separate from the query/computation fixes above.
- Caching each cutblock buffer grid cell's in-zone status once instead of recomputing it on every one of its neighbors' own four-direction lookups (v54's perimeter-tracing currently does some redundant repeat work across adjacent cells).
- Reducing the sample-grid density constants — a genuine accuracy-vs-speed trade-off (coarser grid = faster but less precise gap/buffer detection), flagged rather than touched, since that one needs Karim's own call rather than a default.

**Verification.** Full-file syntax check, `getElementById` id-resolution check, and a div-balance check all passed clean (58 declared ids / 45 used / 0 missing; div tags balanced, depth never negative). 21 Node `vm` assertions against the actual extracted `bboxContainsPoint()` and rewritten `pointInAnyGeom()` functions: bbox containment on and off each edge, a concave (L-shaped) test polygon confirming a point inside the bbox but outside the real ring is still correctly rejected (bbox alone is a pre-filter, never the final answer), a point genuinely inside a shape still found `true`, multi-entry lookups matching regardless of which entry in the list actually contains the point, and MultiPolygon geometries (two disjoint parts) both matching correctly while the empty space between them still correctly doesn't. All 21 passed.

## v55: the location pin is now draggable
Karim's report: *"I'm unable to move the location of the pin while the parcels are highlighted how should we make it so I can use both features at the same time?"*

**Root cause, not a new bug.** Every clickable shape this app draws — parcels, tenures, roads, and (relevant here) every Potential Spots shape — sets `bubblingMouseEvents:false`, which stops a click on that shape from also reaching `map.on('click', ...)` underneath (the handler that moves the pin and runs a fresh lookup). That's deliberate: it's literally v33's fix for the opposite bug, where clicking a plotted feature used to silently relocate the pin out from under you. Potential Spots' shapes can now cover most of the visible view (teal spots, purple gaps, cutblock outlines, buffer zones), so "click an empty patch of map" stopped being reliably possible — but simply reverting v33 so every shape click also moves the pin would bring that original bug straight back. Neither side of that trade-off is acceptable on its own, which is why this needed an actual design decision rather than a one-line toggle.

**The fix.** The marker is now draggable (`L.marker(..., {draggable:true})`), with a `dragend` handler that calls the same `runLookup(lat, lng)` a map click already calls — same code path, same query, same report, just triggered by drag instead of click. Because Leaflet renders markers in their own pane above every vector shape, grabbing and dropping the pin works regardless of what's drawn underneath it, and Leaflet's own drag handling already suppresses the map's click event from firing at drag-end (long-standing library behavior, nothing this app needs to guard against itself) — so dragging can't reintroduce the v33 bug either. Both goals held at once: Potential Spots can stay fully visible, and the pin can still go anywhere. The marker also got a native hover tooltip ("Drag to move this location") and the initial "no location selected yet" hint text now mentions it, so the feature is discoverable without needing to be told.

**Verification.** Full-file syntax check, `getElementById` id-resolution check, and a div-balance check (69/69) all passed clean. 12 Node `vm` assertions against the actual extracted `setMarker()` function, with a mock Leaflet marker standing in for the real one: the first placement creates a marker with `draggable:true` and the tooltip title, no `removeLayer` call (nothing to remove yet); simulating a drag calls `runLookup` exactly once, with the *dragged-to* coordinates, not the original placement; a second `setMarker()` call (as a later click or the "Check this location" button would trigger) correctly removes the old marker before creating an independently-wired new one; and dragging that second marker doesn't also fire a stale handler left over from the first — exactly one more `runLookup` call, with the correct new position. All 12 passed.

**Not yet field-confirmed** — the general standing caveat: whether dragging feels natural in practice (touch vs. mouse, drag sensitivity) is worth Karim's own hands-on check, same as every other interaction change in this app.

## v54: a solid perimeter line around the 400m buffer zone
Karim's request: *"Let's add a solid line around the perimeter of the 400m diameter of that cutblock."*

**What "perimeter" means here, and why it isn't a trivial ask.** The buffer zone isn't one polygon -- it's drawn as a tiled field of small translucent squares (`renderActiveCutblockBuffers()`, since v48), because true polygon offsetting/buffering is exactly the kind of "hard-to-verify-blind" geometry operation this project has deliberately avoided everywhere else (grid-sampling instead of polygon booleans is the whole reason `clipParcelByInfrastructure()`, the v44 gap sweep, and this buffer feature all work the way they do). So "draw a line around the perimeter" can't mean "compute the offset polygon's boundary" without breaking that pattern -- it has to mean something buildable with the same grid-sampling approach.

**The approach.** For every square already being drawn (every sample point within 400m of the cutblock), check each of its four neighbors -- one grid-spacing away, north/south/east/west -- by running the exact same distance test against a synthetic neighboring point (no need for that neighbor to have actually been enumerated by the sampling loop; whether a point is "in the zone" is just a distance check, well-defined anywhere). Wherever a neighbor fails the test (i.e. this square sits on the outer edge of the zone in that direction), draw a solid line on that one side. A square fully surrounded by other in-zone squares -- deep in the interior -- gets no line on any side. The result is a traced outline hugging the true outer boundary of the halo, without any spurious lines cutting across the interior between two adjacent in-zone squares, and without ever computing a real offset polygon.

**Visual distinction from the real outline, deliberate.** The cutblock's own actual polygon boundary (`activeCutblockOutlineStyle()`, from v46) stays dashed. The new buffer-zone perimeter (`cutblockBufferOutlineStyle()`) is solid, no dash, full opacity, same red. Dashed = the real, surveyed shape; solid = the approximate 400m boundary around it.
