## v34: moved the cutblock popup's sourcing blurb behind a small "i" button
Karim's request: *"Okay now let's take the blurb at the bottom of each cut block pop up and put it into a 'i' icon instead of having in the first pop up."*

Every cutblock detail popup (map click, report card "Details ›", and the v30 overlap chooser's "Details ›") ended with a fixed, entry-independent paragraph explaining where the planned/start/completion dates, logging-phase codes, and Life Cycle field each come from. It was always printed inline, pushing the actual per-block data (dates, area, licensee) further down every time.

**The fix.** The blurb text itself didn't change — it moved. It's now a single shared constant (`CUTBLOCK_SOURCE_INFO_HTML`), since it never varied by entry, and `cutblockPopup()` no longer prints it inline at all. In its place, a small button next to the block/opening ID uses the app's existing `.info-btn` "i" pattern (same style already used in the header, the checklist section head, and the footer sources) and opens the blurb through the app's existing shared `openInfoModal()` — the same modal mechanism the header/checklist/footer info buttons already use, via a new one-line helper, `showCutblockSourceInfo()`. This applies everywhere `cutblockPopup()` is rendered: the map click popup, the report card's "Details ›", and the v30 overlap chooser's "Details ›" — all three now show the condensed data first with the "i" button one click away, instead of the blurb always taking up space.

One known, minor UX wrinkle: when the "i" button is clicked from inside `showCutblockDetail()`/`showMapCutblockDetail()` (i.e. the blurb is opened from *within* the modal that's already showing that same block's full detail via "Details ›"), the shared single-instance modal simply replaces its own content with the blurb — there's no "back" to return to the block detail, only the modal's normal close (✕ / Escape / click-outside). This matches how the app's other info buttons already behave (no nesting/history anywhere else either), so it was kept consistent rather than adding new modal-stacking behavior nobody asked for.

**Verification.** Node `vm`-based rendering tests against the real extracted `cutblockPopup()`/`showCutblockSourceInfo()` source: confirmed the blurb text no longer appears inline in the popup HTML for two different entries (one with Life Cycle + normal fields, one oversized/flagged with no Life Cycle), confirmed the `.info-btn` button is present in both and wired via `onclick="showCutblockSourceInfo()"`, confirmed the per-entry rows (ID, logging phase, Life Cycle, dates, area, licensee, oversized warning) still render exactly as before, and confirmed calling `showCutblockSourceInfo()` triggers `openInfoModal()` exactly once with a title referencing data sources and a body containing the full original blurb text — **14 assertions, all passed**. `node --check` on the full extracted inline script confirmed no syntax errors.

**Not yet visually confirmed against real BC data** — same standing caveat as most features this session. Worth specifically checking: the "i" button reads clearly at the popup's `min-width:200px` size without crowding the ID text, and the modal-replaces-modal behavior described above doesn't feel jarring in practice when clicked from "Details ›".

## v33: fixed the real cause — clicking any plotted map feature was also re-selecting that point as a new location
Karim's follow-up, correcting v32: *"The location marker is still changing everytime i click a different area regardless of whether it's a cutblock or not. If there is a marker placed and I'm clicking on a cut block, a new marker should not be placed. It has nothing to do with pan or zoom."*

**v32 was a real fix for a real (but minor) issue, and also a misdiagnosis of the main one.** Karim's phrase "regardless of whether it's a cutblock or not" was the key clue: the marker-relocation bug wasn't cutblock-specific and had nothing to do with popups auto-panning the viewport — it was happening on a click to *any* plotted map feature (parcels, parks, tenures, woodlots, recreation sites/trails, cutblocks, the Reveal Crown Land / Potential Spots layers), because of a genuine, previously-unexamined mechanism in Leaflet itself.

**Root cause, confirmed against Leaflet's actual source (not assumed from memory).** `raw.githubusercontent.com` is reachable from this sandbox even though `cdnjs.cloudflare.com`/`unpkg.com`/the npm registry are not, so the real Leaflet 1.9.4 source was fetched directly from `github.com/Leaflet/Leaflet` to confirm this precisely rather than guess a second time. Every interactive Leaflet layer (`L.Path`/`L.Polygon`/`L.CircleMarker`/etc., via the shared `Layer` base class) defaults to `bubblingMouseEvents: true`. In `Map.js`'s `_findEventTargets()`, a click's target list is built by walking DOM ancestors outward from the exact element clicked — and because the map itself is always registered as a target on its own container (`this._targets[stamp(this._container)] = this`, set in the constructor), that walk *always* reaches the map as the outermost target, provided nothing stopped it first. `_fireDOMEvent()` then fires each target in order and only stops early if `data.originalEvent._stopped` was explicitly set, **or** if `targets[i].options.bubblingMouseEvents === false`. Since every layer in this app was left at Leaflet's default (`true`), clicking, say, a cutblock polygon fired *both* that polygon's own click handler (opening its popup, correctly) *and then* the map's own `click` listener set up at the very top of the file (`map.on('click', e => runLookup(e.latlng.lat, e.latlng.lng))`) — which is exactly what calls `setMarker()` and re-runs the whole lookup at the newly clicked point. Every popup-bearing (or tooltip-bearing, or custom-click-bound) layer in the app had this same problem; cutblocks were just the layer Karim happened to be testing most, since they're this session's main focus.

**The fix.** Added `bubblingMouseEvents: false` to every clickable overlay layer's options object across the whole app — the parcel(s) actually under the pin and the faded nearby-parcels layer, the municipal boundary, the park, Crown tenures, woodlot/managed licences, forest service roads (hover-only, but still needed it — see below), every cutblock polygon (on top of v32's `autoPan: false`), all three recreation layers (sites/trails/areas), the Reveal Crown Land button's parcel layer, and every Potential Spots shape (whole-parcel and clipped-grid-square). Once set, `_fireDOMEvent()`'s loop stops immediately after firing that one layer's own click handler and never reaches the map's `click` listener — so the layer's popup/tooltip still opens exactly as before, but the marker, the lat/lng box, and the report no longer change. Clicking bare map background (no feature under the cursor) is unaffected: `_findEventTargets()` never finds a layer target there, so `targets = [map]` from the start and `runLookup()` still fires normally — that's still how a genuinely new location gets selected. The forest service road layer (hover-only, `bindTooltip` not `bindPopup`) needed the same fix for the same reason: even with no click handler of its own, an interactive layer with `bubblingMouseEvents` left at its default still lets a click on it reach the map underneath.

**Verification.** *Mechanism*, by re-running Leaflet's own dispatch algorithm — copied verbatim from the fetched `Map.js` source, not reconstructed from memory — against synthetic target lists in a Node sandbox: 4 assertions confirming that without the fix a layer click also fires a stand-in "map click" (runLookup) handler, that with `bubblingMouseEvents:false` only the layer's own handler fires and the map's does not, and that clicking bare background (no layer target) still correctly reaches the map — **all 4 passed**. *Coverage*, via a regex scan of the real file confirming every one of the 14 clickable-overlay-layer call sites (found independently of any helper function, so a call site can't silently inherit the fix from elsewhere) sets `bubblingMouseEvents:false`, with a self-check confirming the scan actually flags a deliberately-broken snippet missing the option — **2 assertions, both passed**. *End-to-end*, via the real extracted `renderMapOverlays()` run with every layer type populated (parcel, nearby parcel, municipal boundary, park, tenure, woodlot, road, cutblock, and all three recreation kinds) against hand-rolled Leaflet stubs recording the exact options object each layer was actually created with — confirmed the function runs without throwing, draws every layer type, that literally every layer object it creates carries `bubblingMouseEvents:false` in its real (not just source-text) options, and that popup/tooltip content is unchanged (a spot-checked parcel popup, the road's tooltip, and the cutblock's custom click handler all still present and correct) — **6 assertions, all passed**. `node --check` on the full extracted inline script confirmed no syntax errors throughout.

**Not yet visually confirmed against real BC data** — same standing caveat as most features this session, though the mechanism here is unusually well-grounded: verified against Leaflet's actual fetched source rather than documentation or memory, and tested against a faithful copy of Leaflet's own dispatch algorithm rather than a black-box assumption. What's unconfirmed is only whether this reads as fully resolved once Karim is clicking around a real report in a live browser — worth specifically re-checking that clicking a parcel, a park, a tenure, and a recreation site all leave the marker untouched too, not just cutblocks.

## v32: stopped the map auto-panning (and appearing to move the selected location) when a cutblock is clicked
Karim's bug report: *"The location selector should not change places every time a cut block is selected."*

**Diagnosis.** Nothing in this app's own code moves the marker, the lat/lng box, or re-runs a lookup when a cutblock is clicked on the map — `setMarker()` (which is what actually relocates the selected point) is only ever called from `runLookup()`, and none of the cutblock click/popup/detail-modal code paths call it. The actual cause is a Leaflet default that has nothing to do with this app's own logic: every `L.Popup` auto-pans the map (`autoPan: true` is Leaflet's own default) to keep itself fully inside the viewport whenever it opens, silently calling `map.panBy(...)` if it would otherwise spill off an edge. The cutblock click handler (`renderMapOverlays()`'s polygon `.on('click', ...)`, added in v30 to fix the overlapping-cutblock bug) opens a brand-new `L.popup()` at the exact clicked pixel on every single click — and since overlapping/adjacent cutblocks are, by definition, clustered close together and often inspected one after another while zoomed in tight (exactly the workflow v30 exists for), each click's popup is a good candidate to nudge past an edge and trigger this auto-pan. The marker and the actual selected coordinates never move — only the viewport does — but visually, with the pin no longer where it was on screen, it reads exactly as "the location selector changed places."

**The fix.** Added `autoPan: false` to the single `L.popup({...})` call cutblock clicks use (the same call serves both the ordinary single-record popup and the v30 overlap chooser, so one change covers both). No other behavior of that popup changed — same `maxWidth`, same content-selection logic, same click-to-open-detail-modal flow. Scoped to cutblocks specifically, matching Karim's report; every other layer on the map (parcels, parks, tenures, woodlots, municipal boundaries, recreation sites/trails, Potential Spots) still uses Leaflet's default popups and therefore still auto-pans on open — if that turns out to bother Karim elsewhere too, it's the same one-line fix (`autoPan: false`) applied to that layer's `bindPopup()`/`L.popup()` call.

**Verification.** *Integration*, via the real extracted `renderMapOverlays()`, `cutblockOverlapPopupHtml()`, `cutblockPopup()`, and their real dependencies (`pointInGeometry()`, `pointInRing()`, `outerRings()`, `geomsIntersect()`, the status/life-cycle code lookups) run together in a Node `vm` sandbox with hand-rolled Leaflet stubs recording every `L.popup()` call's options — 9 assertions: all three drawable cutblock entries in a 3-record fixture (two genuinely overlapping, one disjoint) get their own click handler; clicking at a point inside both overlapping polygons opens exactly one popup, whose options include `autoPan: false` alongside the existing unchanged `maxWidth: 280`, with the correct 2-record chooser content; clicking the disjoint record's own layer separately also opens a popup with `autoPan: false`, this time with the plain single-record detail content (not a chooser) — confirming the fix applies identically on both the single-match and multi-match paths — **all 9 passed**. `node --check` on the full extracted inline script confirmed no syntax errors. Confirmed via `grep` that `autoPan` now appears exactly once in the file, at this one call site.

**Not yet visually confirmed against real BC data** — same standing caveat as most features this session, though this one is a well-documented, stable Leaflet default (`L.Popup`'s `_adjustPan()`), not a guess about undocumented behavior, so the mechanism itself is on solid ground; what's unconfirmed is only whether this was in fact what Karim was seeing (rather than some other, not-yet-identified cause) and whether the fix reads as fully resolved once he's clicking around real overlapping cutblocks in a live browser.

## v31: condensed cutblock list now shows Life Cycle instead of raw status; raw status moved to Details
Karim's request: *"Also in the list of cut blocks let's replace status and with the life status info and put the status in other details."*

**Scope.** Two places in the app show a condensed, one-line-per-record cutblock list using an identical `phaseMeaning`/`statusBit` pattern: the report card's "Forestry activity within 1 km" section (the literal "list of cut blocks" Karim's message refers to), and the v30 overlap chooser (the popup shown when a map click lands on two or more overlapping cutblock polygons, added last round using the same pattern). Both were updated for consistency, since the overlap chooser was modeled directly on the report card's line to begin with.

**What changed in the code.** In both places, the `phaseMeaning`/`statusBit` computation (which decoded and displayed `entry.statusLabel`, the raw logging-phase code, via `statusCodeLabel()`) was replaced with an equivalent `lifeCycleMeaning`/`lifeCycleBit` computation using `entry.lifeCycleStatus` and `lifeCycleLabel()` (both added in v29) — same "raw code, with a dotted-underline hover tooltip showing the decoded meaning when known" presentation, just swapped to the Life Cycle field, and the visible label changed from "— status" to "— life cycle". Since `entry.lifeCycleStatus` is only ever populated for FTEN-matched entries (`null` for a pure-RESULTS entry — see v29), a new explicit fallback renders "n/a" with a hover tooltip explaining why (no matching FTEN cutting-permit record) rather than showing a blank or misleading value. The raw logging-phase status doesn't disappear: `cutblockPopup()` — reached from either condensed list via its existing "Details ›" click-through — already shows both a "Logging phase" row (the raw status, decoded via `statusCodeLabel()`) and a "Life Cycle" row (added in v29) side by side, so no change was needed there to satisfy the "put the status in other details" half of the request.

**Verification.** *Math/rendering*, via the real extracted `esc()`, `CUTBLOCK_LIFECYCLE_CODES`, and `lifeCycleLabel()` source plus faithful copies of both changed rendering lines, run in a Node `vm` sandbox — 16 assertions: a known code (ACTIVE) renders the raw code plus its tooltip meaning and uses "— life cycle" rather than "— status" in both the report-card line and the overlap-chooser row; a lower-case code (`pending`) still resolves a tooltip meaning via the case-insensitive lookup while the raw code displays exactly as given (not upcased); RETIRED resolves correctly; a `null`/missing `lifeCycleStatus` (the RESULTS-only case) renders the "n/a" fallback with an explanatory tooltip and produces no `undefined` in the output; an unrecognized code still displays raw with no bogus tooltip; and an HTML-injection-shaped code value is correctly escaped — **all 16 passed**. `node --check` on the full extracted inline script confirmed no syntax errors after both edits. Confirmed via `grep` that no `— status ${statusBit}` cutblock-list pattern remains anywhere in the file (the two changed locations were the only two; a same-named `statusBit` in the unrelated forest-service-road card was left untouched, as it was never part of this request).

**Not yet visually confirmed against real BC data** — same standing caveat as every other feature this session: logic/rendering-tested via Node `vm`, not eyeballed in a real browser against a real cutblock record yet. Worth checking together with the still-outstanding v29/v30 real-world confirmations (limitations 31/32 below): does the "n/a" fallback read sensibly for a RESULTS-only entry, and does the tooltip-on-hover Life Cycle meaning work as expected on both the report card and the overlap chooser.

## v30: fixed overlapping cutblock polygons so a click shows every record, not just the topmost one
Karim's request: *"If there are layered cut blocks make sure if they are clicked on that both come up as an option to see information about."*

**The underlying bug.** Every cutblock polygon in `renderMapOverlays()` was drawn with a plain `L.geoJSON(...).bindPopup(cutblockPopup(entry))` — standard Leaflet, but Leaflet vector layers have `bubblingMouseEvents` off by default, so a click is only ever delivered to whichever single layer happens to be drawn on top at that exact pixel. Where two cutblock records genuinely overlap in BC's own data (a RESULTS opening and a matching FTEN cutting-permit block covering the same physical ground is one common real-world case, per this app's own `buildCutblockList()` merge logic; two separate, independently-recorded blocks whose boundaries happen to touch is another), the bottom one's popup was completely unreachable by clicking — silently hidden, not just visually underneath.

**The fix doesn't rely on Leaflet's event routing at all.** Every drawn cutblock polygon still gets its own click listener, but instead of a fixed, pre-bound popup, the handler re-tests the *exact click point* against **every** drawn cutblock's real geometry (new `pointInGeometry()`, a straightforward point-in-polygon test reusing the same `pointInRing()`/`outerRings()` math this app already uses for the Potential Spots park/municipality overlap check — same "outer rings only, holes ignored" simplification, same reasoning for hand-rolling it rather than an unverified external library). This means the result is correct regardless of which polygon Leaflet happened to route the click to: clicking record A's layer or record B's layer at the same overlapping point produces the identical result. New `cutblockOverlapPopupHtml(latlng, drawableEntries)` does the matching and decides what to show: exactly one match behaves exactly as before (the single record's full popup, via the existing `cutblockPopup()`); two or more matches shows a small chooser — "N overlapping cutblock records here," each one a clickable row (same `.field.clickable`/`.chev` styling the report card's condensed cutblock lines already use, for visual consistency) — clicking a row opens that specific record's full detail in the existing info modal via a new `showMapCutblockDetail(i)`, reading from a new `currentMapCutOpenings` array kept deliberately separate from the report card's own `currentCutOpenings` (different subset, would collide on index otherwise). Oversized cutblocks (v26) are unaffected — still excluded from `drawableCutEntries` and never drawn or clickable, same as before. As of v31, each chooser row's own "— status" text was replaced with "— life cycle" — see the v31 section above.

**Verification.** *Math*, via the real extracted `pointInGeometry()`/`cutblockOverlapPopupHtml()` source in a Node `vm` sandbox — 23 assertions: point-in-polygon correctness for a simple polygon, a MultiPolygon, and a point genuinely inside two overlapping polygons at once; the single-match case returns byte-identical output to calling `cutblockPopup()` directly (no regression for the non-overlapping, common case); the two-match case returns the correct chooser with the right count, the right two records listed by name, the disjoint third record correctly excluded, and `currentMapCutOpenings` populated with exactly those two entries in order; and — caught *by* this verification pass, not before it — an empty entries list was found to crash (`cutblockPopup(undefined)`) rather than degrade gracefully, fixed with an explicit fallback before shipping — **all 23 passed after the fix**. *Integration*, via the real extracted `renderMapOverlays()` with hand-rolled Leaflet stubs (a chainable `L.geoJSON()` capturing whichever handler was registered via `.on('click', ...)`, and a chainable `L.popup()` capturing `setContent()`/`openOn()` calls) — 23 assertions: every drawn cutblock layer gets a real click handler rather than a bound popup; oversized entries are still correctly excluded from drawing (v26 regression check); clicking a layer at a point covered by only one polygon shows that record's own content with no chooser; clicking either overlapping layer (A's or B's) at the same shared point produces the identical two-record chooser — the actual regression this feature exists to fix, confirmed byte-for-byte identical regardless of which layer received the click; clicking a fully disjoint record's layer still shows just its own popup; and an empty or undefined `cutList` draws nothing without throwing — **all 23 passed**. `node --check` on the full extracted inline script confirmed no syntax errors throughout.

**Not yet visually confirmed against real BC data** — same standing caveat as every other map-drawing feature this session: logic/math-tested via Node `vm`, not eyeballed against a real pair of overlapping BC cutblock records (RESULTS + FTEN for the same block, or two adjacent/overlapping blocks) in an actual browser yet. Worth a specific real-world check: click a spot known to have a RESULTS-and-FTEN-matched opening right where it touches an unrelated neighbouring block, and confirm the chooser appears with both and each row opens the correct full detail.

## v29: added the cutblock "Life Cycle" field (LIFE_CYCLE_STATUS_CODE)
Karim's request: *"Can we pull the LIFE_CYCLE_STATUS_CODE data from Geo.ca and include that in the cut block information. Lable it 'Life Cycle'."*

**Researched before coding, following this project's standing discipline.** Confirmed live via `DescribeFeatureType` against `WHSE_FOREST_TENURE.FTEN_CUT_BLOCK_POLY_SVW` (the FTEN cutting-permit layer this app already queries as `cutPlanR`) that `LIFE_CYCLE_STATUS_CODE` is a real field on that layer. Also checked `WHSE_FOREST_VEGETATION.RSLT_OPENING_SVW` (the RESULTS layer, `cutR`) the same way — it has no such field at all, so `LIFE_CYCLE_STATUS_CODE` is exclusively an FTEN-side attribute, same pattern as `PROJECT_TYPE`/`LIFE_CYCLE_STATUS_CODE` being exclusive to the v28 recreation polygon/line layers and absent from the recreation points layer.

**On "Geo.ca":** the live BCGW data this app queries is served from `openmaps.gov.bc.ca` (DataBC), the same endpoint this app has always used — there's no separate Geo.ca-hosted copy of this layer with different data. What does exist is a federated catalogue listing for this exact dataset on Canada's Open Government Portal (`open.canada.ca` — the same family of federal geospatial portals as geo.ca), confirmed via web search and fetch. That listing turned out to be genuinely useful: it's the first source found all session that spells out the field's **exact, complete three-value domain in plain English**, rather than requiring the "best sourced option found, not confirmed as exact" hedging v27's `CUTBLOCK_STATUS_CODES` needed: *"PENDING - the cut block has been submitted as a new cut block or an amendment, but is not yet approved or rejected, ACTIVE - the cut block is approved and activities may be taking place on the cut block, or RETIRED - all activities, with respect to the harvesting authority, have been completed for the cut block."* No new query, endpoint, or network call was needed to get the actual field value — WFS `GetFeature` already returns every property on a feature by default (this app has never restricted to a subset via `PROPERTYNAME`), so `LIFE_CYCLE_STATUS_CODE` was already arriving in `cutPlanR`'s feature properties every time this app has ever queried that layer; it just wasn't being read into an entry's fields or shown anywhere.

**What changed in the code:** a new `CUTBLOCK_LIFECYCLE_CODES` lookup (3 entries, this time with a real, complete, plainly-documented domain rather than a partial/best-effort one) and a `lifeCycleLabel(code)` helper, following the same shape as v27's `CUTBLOCK_STATUS_CODES`/`statusCodeLabel()`. `buildCutblockList()` now reads `LIFE_CYCLE_STATUS_CODE` off each FTEN (`cutPlanR`) feature into a new `entry.lifeCycleStatus` field — populated directly for an FTEN-only entry, merged onto an existing RESULTS-matched entry (falling back to the existing value if the FTEN record happens to lack it, never overwriting a real value with a blank one), and left `null` for a pure-RESULTS entry (RSLT_OPENING_SVW has no such field, confirmed above — nothing to read). `cutblockPopup()` gained a new **"Life Cycle"** row, right after the existing "Logging phase" row, shown only when `entry.lifeCycleStatus` is present — following the exact same "raw code + `—` + decoded meaning when known" formatting v27 already established for the logging-phase row, so the two sit consistently next to each other. The popup's closing sourcing note was extended to explain Life Cycle's provenance and that it's FTEN-only. As of v31, this field is now also the headline value shown in the condensed report-card and overlap-chooser lists — see the v31 section above (originally, in v29, it was deliberately scoped to the detailed popup only, since Karim's v29 request was specifically about "the cut block information" as shown there).

**Verification.** *Math*, via the real extracted `CUTBLOCK_LIFECYCLE_CODES`/`lifeCycleLabel()`/`buildCutblockList()` source in a Node `vm` sandbox — 19 assertions: `lifeCycleLabel()` handles null/undefined/empty/unknown codes safely (all `null`, no throw), all three real codes (PENDING, ACTIVE, RETIRED) resolve correctly, case-insensitivity holds, and the lookup table has exactly 3 entries (confirming nothing was silently over- or under-populated); `buildCutblockList()` correctly leaves `lifeCycleStatus` `null` for a RESULTS-only entry (no matching FTEN record), populates it directly for an FTEN-only entry, carries it over correctly for a merged RESULTS+FTEN entry, and doesn't overwrite it with a blank when the FTEN side happens to lack the field — **all 19 passed**. *Integration*, via the real extracted `cutblockPopup()` — 19 assertions: each of the three known codes renders the correct raw code plus its decoded meaning in a row labeled "Life Cycle:"; a `null` or entirely-absent `lifeCycleStatus` produces no Life Cycle row at all (no throw); an unrecognized code still shows the row with the raw code but no bogus decoded meaning; the new row sits correctly between the existing "Logging phase" and "Planned harvest date" rows and coexists correctly with the oversized-cutblock warning (v26); and a value containing HTML is properly escaped rather than injected raw into the popup — **all 19 passed**. `node --check` on the full extracted inline script confirmed no syntax errors throughout.

**Not yet visually confirmed against real BC data** — same standing caveat as every other feature this session: logic-tested via Node `vm`, not eyeballed against a real cutblock record's actual `LIFE_CYCLE_STATUS_CODE` value in a live browser yet. This one carries less uncertainty than most prior rounds, though, since the field's domain is directly and completely documented (not inferred or best-guessed) and the code path for reading/merging/rendering it closely mirrors v27's already-verified pattern for a near-identical field.

## v28: auto-checking nearby recreation sites & trails (Recreation Sites and Trails BC)
Karim asked, broadly: *"What else can we automate now?"* Rather than propose candidates from memory, spent a research pass confirming real, live BC/federal open-data sources before presenting anything as an option. Eight candidates came back confirmed as real, queryable services: closing the Crown-tenure gap (guide-outfitter areas, traplines, range/grazing agreements, mineral tenures — `WHSE_WILDLIFE_MANAGEMENT.WAA_GUIDE_OUTFITTER_AREA_SVW`, `WAA_TRAPLINE_AREAS_SP`, `WHSE_FOREST_TENURE.FTEN_RANGE_POLY_SVW`, `WHSE_MINERAL_TENURE.MTA_ACQUIRED_TENURE_SVW`), wildfire closures/bans (BC Wildfire Service's own ArcGIS Online feeds), federal land flags (Indian reserves / National Parks, via NRCan — not on DataBC WFS at all), and nearby recreation sites/trails (Recreation Sites and Trails BC, RSTBC). Presented all as a menu; **Karim chose recreation sites & trails specifically**, over the tenure gap (flagged as the recommended option), wildfire closures, and federal land flags — those three remain unbuilt, available for a future round if he wants them.

**Data source, researched not guessed.** RSTBC is split across three separate DataBC WFS layers by geometry type, each confirmed live via `DescribeFeatureType` plus a sample `GetFeature` (not assumed from documentation alone): `WHSE_FOREST_TENURE.FTEN_REC_SITE_POINTS_SVW` (rec sites/campsites — points), `WHSE_FOREST_TENURE.FTEN_RECREATION_POLY_SVW` (recreation reserves/areas — polygons), `WHSE_FOREST_TENURE.FTEN_RECREATION_LINES_SVW` (trails — lines). All three carry `PROJECT_NAME` and `GEOMETRY`; the points layer additionally has `NUM_CAMP_SITES`, `RECREATION_MAINTAIN_STD_CODE`/`MAINTAIN_STD_DESC`, `SITE_LOCATION`, `FOREST_FILE_ID`, and **no lifecycle/status field at all** — a real, confirmed gap in the source data, not an omission in this app; polygons and lines additionally carry `PROJECT_TYPE`, `LIFE_CYCLE_STATUS_CODE`, `FILE_STATUS_CODE`, `RECREATION_MAP_FEATURE_CODE`; lines also carry `FEATURE_LENGTH_M` and `DISTRICT_NAME`.

**What changed in the code:** three new `LAYERS` entries (`recSite`, `recPoly`, `recLine`), each `DWITHIN` the same 1 km `SEARCH_RADIUS_M` every other proximity layer in this app already uses, queried alongside everything else in `runLookup()`'s existing `Promise.all` batch. A new `buildRecreationList(recSiteR, recPolyR, recLineR)` merges the three independent layers into one flat list — no cross-layer join, just each feature tagged with a `kind` (`"site"`/`"trail"`/`"area"`) and its relevant fields, following the same "flag, never silently drop" style as `buildCutblockList()`. `renderMapOverlays()` draws each kind distinctly in a new pink (`#c2255c`), visually distinct from every existing layer colour: sites as small filled circle markers, trails as dashed lines, areas as filled polygons — each with a popup (`recreationPopup()`) explaining what it is and, for polygons/lines, its lifecycle/file status. A new report card ("Recreation sites & trails within 1 km") follows the exact `cardHTML()` pattern used by the existing Crown Tenures/Woodlot cards, lists up to 6 records with a "+N more" overflow line and a kind breakdown, and adds an amber advisory reason to the verdict: a hit here doesn't mean the spot is off-limits, but it does mean people could realistically be nearby, which raises the practical bar for a safe backstop. A new AUTO checklist item reports the count (or that retrieval failed). The `"park-buffer"` MANUAL item's text was updated, following the precedent already set by the `"tenure"` item's wording, to say the report now partly auto-checks this, while being explicit that RSTBC has no open/closed or occupancy feed so a hit is a "people could be nearby" signal, not a legal restriction by itself. The map legend and `SOURCES_HTML` were both updated with the three new layers and an explanatory note about the missing status field.

**Verification.** *Math*, via the real extracted `buildRecreationList()` source in a Node `vm` sandbox — 23 assertions: all three layers merging correctly with the right `kind`/`source` tags and fields per kind, missing/failed layers skipped gracefully without throwing, a mixed ok/empty/failed combination only contributing from the layer(s) that actually succeeded, missing individual fields becoming `null` rather than `undefined` or throwing, and `NUM_CAMP_SITES: 0` preserved rather than falsy-coerced to `null` — **all 23 passed**. *Integration*, via the real extracted `renderMapOverlays()` with hand-rolled Leaflet stubs recording every draw call — 19 assertions: an empty or undefined `recList` draws nothing; a site draws exactly one `circleMarker` at the correctly lat/lng-flipped coordinate (GeoJSON's `[lng,lat]` vs. Leaflet's `[lat,lng]`) in the new pink colour; a trail draws a dashed `geoJSON` line; an area draws a filled (non-dashed) `geoJSON` polygon; a mixed list of all three kinds plus one entry with no geometry draws exactly the three valid ones; and a site with a malformed (too-short) coordinates array is skipped rather than thrown — **all 19 passed**. A second integration pass, via the real extracted `renderReport()` (recreation-card section, checklist item, and `park-buffer` text) with a hand-rolled `document`/`getElementById` stub — 18 assertions: all-layers-failed shows "no data" and the checklist says retrieval failed; layers-ok-but-empty shows "none nearby" without a spurious "no data"; layers-ok-with-data shows the correct total count, each entry's name, the site's campsite count, the amber advisory reason, and the checklist count; the card is non-collapsible when empty and collapsible once it has data (matching the cutblock/tenure card precedent); more than 6 records triggers the "+N more" overflow line while the pill still shows the true total; and the `park-buffer` MANUAL item's text was confirmed to mention the new auto-check — **all 18 passed**. `node --check` on the full extracted inline script confirmed no syntax errors throughout.

**Not yet visually confirmed against real BC data** — same standing caveat as every other map-drawing feature this session: this is logic/math-tested via Node `vm`, not eyeballed against a real BC recreation site, trail, or reserve in an actual browser yet. Specifically unconfirmed: how dense RSTBC's real coverage is at a typical backcountry shooting spot (a feature that returns nothing for most real clicks would be technically correct but low-value); whether `PROJECT_TYPE`/`LIFE_CYCLE_STATUS_CODE`'s real values are as clean and consistent as the sample `GetFeature` response suggested; and whether the missing status field on the points layer (rec sites/campsites) turns out to be a practical problem in the field (an old, informally-decommissioned site still showing as a live hit).

## v27: decoded the abbreviated "logging phase" codes into a plain-English glossary
Karim's request: *"Let's make a legend for what the abreviated logging phases mean."* The report card and cutblock popups have always shown the raw `statusLabel` code (e.g. "FG", "RET", "APP") verbatim — meaningful to someone who works with BC forestry data, opaque to anyone else, and flagged as unverified in this project's own limitations since v13/v22 (see limitation 13's earlier text).

**Research, not a guess.** Rather than invent plausible-sounding expansions, spent a research pass looking for BC's own published code tables. Two of the three underlying database fields (RESULTS' `OPENING_STATUS_CODE` and FTEN's `HARVEST_AUTH_STATUS_CODE`/`BLOCK_STATUS_CODE`) have **no complete published domain** — confirmed directly via the live BCGW ArcGIS service metadata for the RESULTS layer (`"domain":null`) and via web search turning up no comprehensive table for either field, consistent with this project's pre-existing limitation 13. Given that, the goal was to source as many real, confirmable codes as possible rather than decode nothing, while being explicit that the result is a partial glossary, not a complete one:
- **APP, FG, RET** (RESULTS `OPENING_STATUS_CODE`) — confirmed via BC's own "RESULTS Information Submission Specifications" document: FG is explicitly "Set to 'FG'" once an opening's free-growing obligations are met, RET permanently stops an opening from receiving further updates, and APP is the opening-approval status.
- **PE, PP, PI, HI, LC, HC** — no field-level table exists for FTEN's status codes either, but the closest documented match found is the Forest Tenures Branch's own **"Cutting Permit and Road Tenure Administration Manual"**, section 2.2.4 ("FTA Codes and Definitions" / "Normal File Status Sequence"), which describes the status of agreements, cutting permits and cutblocks generally: Pending (electronic application submitted) → Proposed (cruise/appraisal underway) → Pending Issuance (document prepared for signature) → Issued (permit signed) → Logging Complete → Closed. This is flagged in the code as *not confirmed as an exact 1:1 match* to the specific `HARVEST_AUTH_STATUS_CODE`/`BLOCK_STATUS_CODE` field names — the best sourced option found, not a certainty.

**What changed in the code:** a new `CUTBLOCK_STATUS_CODES` lookup (9 entries, each with a `label` and a `source` citation) and a `statusCodeLabel(code)` helper. `cutblockPopup()`'s "Logging phase" line now appends the decoded meaning after the raw code when known (e.g. "FG — Free Growing — opening has met its free-growing obligations"). The report card's condensed cutblock line kept the raw code compact but added a dotted-underline hover tooltip with the full meaning when known through v30 — as of v31 the condensed line shows Life Cycle instead, with the raw status moved to Details (see the v31 section above). The map legend's existing cutblock-colouring note gained a new "Logging phase codes" glossary block, **generated directly from `CUTBLOCK_STATUS_CODES` at page-load** (not a hand-duplicated second copy) so the two can never drift out of sync, grouped by source (RESULTS vs. the FTA document) with an explicit closing note that any other code is shown as-is because BC hasn't published one complete table. The old, now-stale "Status/logging-phase meaning is a best guess, not verified against BC's own code tables" line in the cutblock detail popup was rewritten to point at the sourced legend instead of disclaiming everything uniformly.

**Verification.** *Math*, via the real extracted `CUTBLOCK_STATUS_CODES`/`statusCodeLabel()`/`renderStatusCodeLegend()` source in a Node `vm` sandbox — 16 assertions: null/empty/unknown codes all return `null` without throwing, all three RESULTS codes and all six FTA codes resolve correctly, case-insensitivity (`fg` matches `FG`), the legend auto-renders from the same table with every one of the 9 codes present in its output plus both source-group labels and the "not matched" caveat text, and the legend-render function doesn't throw when its target element is missing from the DOM — **all 16 passed**. *Integration*, via the real extracted `cutblockPopup()` and the report card's row-rendering logic — 10 assertions: a known code (FG) shows both the raw code and its decoded meaning in the popup, an unknown code (ZZZ) shows only the raw code with no bogus meaning appended, a missing `statusLabel` falls back to "?" without throwing, a known code in the condensed report-card row gets a hover `title` tooltip with the full meaning while an unknown code gets none, and the oversized-cutblock flag (v26) and the new status tooltip correctly coexist on the same row — **all 10 passed**. `node --check` on the full extracted inline script confirmed no syntax errors throughout.

**Not a complete glossary, and said so in the UI itself** — per the research above, this covers 9 codes across two BC documents; any code outside that list still displays exactly as it always has (a bare, undecoded string), with the legend's own closing line explaining why rather than implying the list is exhaustive. The FTA table's exact field-name match (limitation 13, carried forward) also remains unconfirmed — it's the best documented option found, described that way in both the code comments and the legend copy, not asserted as certain.

## v26: implausibly large cutblock polygons flagged and excluded from the map draw, kept in the report
Karim's bug report, with two screenshots: a click near Hwy 99 north of Squamish popped up a "Cut block 1" record claiming **187,690 ha** (~1,877 km²) — large enough to fill nearly the entire visible map and hide everything else. His read was exactly right: *"I don't think the cut block is relevant it looks too big."*

**Investigation, before assuming an app bug.** Read through `buildCutblockList()` line by line to rule out the obvious suspect — a cross-feature merge/indexing bug that might have attached the wrong geometry to the wrong record. It's not that: each list entry's `geometry` and its `grossArea`/`PLANNED_GROSS_BLOCK_AREA` attribute both come directly from that entry's own single WFS feature, never merged or indexed across features. So the 187,690 ha figure is either a genuine anomaly in BC's own FTEN data (most likely a licence or tenure-operating-area boundary mislabeled under a single `CUT_BLOCK_ID`, rather than an actual one-block harvest unit) or, at minimum, a real oversized polygon whose edge legitimately satisfies the `DWITHIN 1000m` query despite covering a huge area overall. Either way, not fixable by correcting the app's query/merge logic — but very much fixable by adding a plausibility check before drawing.

**Grounding the threshold in real data, not a guess.** Researched actual BC cutblock sizing via BC's own Forest and Range Evaluation Program (FREP) report (`frep_report_03.pdf`): the province-wide average cutblock (1996–2002 data) was ~23.1 ha, and the Forest Practices Code's "40/60 rule" (1995) caps cutblock size at 40 ha (Coast/most Southern Interior) or 60 ha (Northern Interior/North Coast). 98–99% of real cutblocks complied even during the study period, and the documented exceptions (insect-salvage blocks after events like the mountain pine beetle outbreak, wind/snow damage, wildfire) don't come anywhere near five-figure hectares. `CUTBLOCK_AREA_SANITY_HA` is set to **7,500 ha** — 125× the larger 60 ha regulatory cap, generous enough to never flag a real, even unusually large, single cutblock, while catching Karim's 187,690 ha example by a wide margin.

**The fix, following this project's "never silently omit" pattern.** Added `ringAreaM2()`/`geomAreaHectares()` — plain shoelace-formula polygon-area helpers using the same equirectangular local-meters projection already used elsewhere in the file (`metersPerDegree()`/`distanceMeters()`) — so each cutblock's *actual geometry* area can be checked independently of its `grossArea` attribute (a bad attribute value wouldn't be caught by trusting the same field back). `buildCutblockList()` now computes `entry.computedAreaHa` for every entry and sets `entry.oversized = true` if either the computed geometry area or the claimed `grossArea` attribute exceeds the 7,500 ha threshold — catching both a bad geometry and a bad attribute independently, since either one alone could carry the false 187,690 ha figure. `renderMapOverlays()` skips drawing any `oversized` entry's polygon (the actual fix for "blocking everything from the view"), but `buildCutblockList()`'s returned list still includes it — nothing is silently dropped. The report card's "Forestry activity within 1 km" section now shows a second pill ("N flagged large") when any are present, marks each flagged line with "⚠ unusually large, not drawn", and adds an explicit amber reason to the verdict explaining what happened and why. `cutblockPopup()` (which backs both the map popup — unreachable for a skipped entry, since it's never drawn — and the report card's `showCutblockDetail()` modal, which *is* reachable via a click) gained an oversized-specific note citing the area, the 23 ha/40-60 ha regulatory context, and the fact that its shape wasn't drawn on the map, framed as "likely a mislabeled licence/operating-area boundary in BC's data, not a single harvested block."

**Verification.** *Math*, via the real extracted `ringAreaM2()`/`geomAreaHectares()`/`buildCutblockList()` source run in a Node `vm` sandbox — 15 assertions: known-area reference squares (a 100m×100m square computes to ~1 ha, a 1000m×1000m square to ~100 ha, a two-part MultiPolygon sums to ~2 ha), degenerate/null-geometry safety, a normal ~20 ha entry correctly *not* flagged, a huge-geometry entry with no `grossArea` at all still flagged from geometry alone, a small-geometry entry with a huge claimed `grossArea` (reproducing Karim's exact case — the source data itself claims an implausible area) still flagged, and a missing-geometry entry handled without crashing and without being flagged purely from the missing value — **all 15 passed**. *Integration*, via the real extracted `renderMapOverlays()` with hand-rolled Leaflet stubs recording every `L.geoJSON(...).addTo(overlayLayer)` call — confirmed an oversized entry alongside a normal one results in exactly one drawn layer (the normal one), two normal entries both draw, an empty `cutList` draws nothing without throwing, and a missing-geometry entry never reaches `L.geoJSON` regardless of its `oversized` flag — **all 5 passed**. `node --check` on the full extracted inline script confirmed no syntax errors throughout.

**Not yet visually confirmed against real BC data** — same standing caveat as every other map-drawing feature this session: this is logic/math-tested via Node `vm`, not eyeballed against Karim's actual reported location (or a genuinely large but legitimate cutblock, if one exists) in a real browser yet. Also worth flagging: `cutList.slice(0,5)` in the report card takes the *first* five entries returned by the query, not sorted by size or relevance — if several flagged-large records and several normal ones are all within 1 km of the same point, an unlucky ordering could show mostly flagged entries in the condensed view (the "+N more nearby" line still covers the rest, and nothing is hidden from `showCutblockDetail()`, but it's a minor display-ordering rough edge, not addressed in this round since Karim didn't report it).

## v25: corrected the v24 buffer to the real Wildlife Act distances, from a source document
Right after v24 shipped, Karim uploaded the actual **2026-2028 BC Hunting and Trapping Regulations Synopsis** (a real BC government PDF reproducing the Wildlife Act's "No Hunting and No Shooting Areas" provisions) and asked to implement it. Reading it immediately surfaced that v24's flat 400 m buffer around "major roads/highways and buildings" was this session's own invented placeholder, not a cited number — the real rule is three-tiered and considerably more specific:

1. **100 m from occupied buildings** — "It is unlawful to hunt or discharge a firearm within 100 m of a church, school building, school yard, playground, regional district building, dwelling house, or farm or ranch building that is occupied by persons or domestic animals" (an owner/occupier/employee is exempt near their own building for slaughtering livestock — not modelled, since the tool has no way to know who's asking).
2. **25 m road allowance, as the *default* for every numbered highway or 2-lane+ public road** — not just "major" ones. The literal rule is 15 m either side of the road's midline for roads under 3 lanes, or 15 m from the paved edge for 3+ lane highways; since BC's Digital Road Atlas only has centreline geometry (no paved-edge polygon), 25 m is used uniformly as a documented, slightly conservative stand-in so a wide highway is never *under*-buffered.
3. **400 m only on a specific, named list of highways/roads** — some technically only ban a single projectile (rifle/slug) while still permitting shotgun-fired shot; others (BC-Parks-adjacent corridors) are a full ban. This app doesn't ask what the user plans to shoot, so it applies 400 m to both categories as the conservative default, same "never under-match" philosophy already used for `CROWN_OWNER_CQL`.

**Scoping the correction.** Given the source document is 84 pages and each of BC's 8 regions also lists its own additional named local No Shooting Areas (Region 1 alone has three: Cowichan River, Nanoose Bay, Westcan Terminals Road), asked Karim how far to take it. He chose the middle of three options: fix the core building/road distances **and** add the specific named 400 m highway list, but not attempt to hand-code all ~50+ remaining pages of region-specific named areas (many of which are described only relative to small-scale maps not included in the synopsis itself, making them unverifiable to geocode reliably).

**The named 400 m highway list** (`SPECIAL_400M_ROUTES`, keyed by `HIGHWAY_ROUTE_NUMBER`): Hwy 5 (Coquihalla), 6, 16 (Yellowhead), 27, 29, 35, 37 (Stewart-Cassiar), 37A, 39, 97, 97C (Okanagan Connector), 99 (Sea-to-Sky), 113 (Nisga'a Highway), and 3 (Crowsnest, only the Manning Park corridor). For most of these the synopsis's named segment is effectively the highway's whole BC extent (e.g. Hwy 16 "between the boundary of Prince Rupert and the B.C.-Alberta interprovincial boundary" *is* all of Hwy 16), so the full queried route gets 400 m with no further narrowing. Four are the exception, where the named segment is a much shorter piece of a much longer highway — Hwy 3 (Hope-Princeton/Manning Park only, not the whole Kootenay-spanning route), Hwy 5 (Hope-Kamloops only), Hwy 97 (Cottonwood River north to the Yukon border only — excludes the entire Okanagan/Thompson/Cariboo-south portion), and Hwy 99 (West Vancouver-Whistler only). Rather than blanket-apply 400 m to these highways' *entire* length (which would have been "safe" in the sense of never under-restricting, but would have made Potential Spots uselessly conservative across hundreds of unrelated kilometres of two of BC's busiest highways), each carries an approximate lat/lng **clip window** built from well-known BC landmark coordinates (Hope, Princeton, Kamloops, Quesnel, West Vancouver, Whistler — Lower Post and Aspen Grove were checked via web search since they were less certain) — a road segment's bbox centre has to fall inside that window for the 400 m buffer to apply; outside it, that route gets the ordinary 25 m default like any other road. This is explicitly an approximation of a landmark-described boundary, not a surveyed legal line, and is documented as such in the code, the map legend, and here.

**Deliberately left out**: Twelve Mile Road (Road 190) and Callaghan Road, two more 400 m corridors named in the synopsis — neither is a numbered highway route, so neither is reliably matchable against the DRA layer's `HIGHWAY_ROUTE_NUMBER` field. Left as an explicit gap (noted in the map legend) rather than guessed at. Also not modelled: the asymmetric 400 m-west/1 km-east buffer the synopsis gives specifically for Hwy 99's West Vancouver–Squamish leg (a separate, more restrictive named "No Shooting Area" than the plain 400 m Squamish–Whistler segment) — simplified to a uniform 400 m across the whole West Van–Whistler span, noted in the popup/legend copy; and the ~50 pages of per-region named local No Shooting Areas, per the scoping decision above.

**What changed in the code:** `LAYERS.majorRoad` renamed to `LAYERS.draRoad` (its old name collided with the pre-existing FTEN forest-service-road layer's `road` key) and its CQL class filter dropped entirely — the query now fetches every DRA road in the viewport rather than a highway-only subset, since the real rule covers any 2-lane+ public road, not just arterials/highways (feature cap raised from 600 to 1500 accordingly). New constants `BUILDING_BUFFER_M` (100), `DEFAULT_ROAD_BUFFER_M` (25), `SPECIAL_ROUTE_BUFFER_M` (400), `SPECIAL_400M_ROUTES` (the highway table with clip windows), plus `normalizeRouteNumber()` and `routeSpecialInfo()` to classify each returned road feature. `clipParcelByInfrastructure()`'s signature changed from one global `bufferM` to a `nearbyRoads` array of `{geometry, bufferM}` (each road now carries its own distance) plus a separate fixed `buildingBufferM`. `SPOT_GRID_MIN_SPACING_M` lowered from 25 m to 15 m so the sampling grid has enough resolution to actually see the new, much narrower 25 m default road buffer (at the old 25m-minimum spacing, a 25m-wide excluded strip could fall entirely inside the gap between two adjacent sample columns and never register). `spotPopup()` and the map legend rewritten to explain the tiered distances and cite the source document instead of a bare "400m major-road/building buffer" line.

**Verification:** the same two-pass pattern as v24, re-run against the new logic. *Math*, via the real extracted source in a Node `vm` sandbox — 26 assertions, including `normalizeRouteNumber()` (trimming/case/leading-zero handling), `routeSpecialInfo()` (a route on the list matches; the same route number outside its clip window doesn't; an unlisted route never matches), and `clipParcelByInfrastructure()` with per-road buffer distances (a small parcel survives an ordinary 25 m-buffer road nearby but is fully excluded when that same road is reclassified as a 400 m special route; mixed ordinary+special roads on the same parcel each enforce their own distance) — **all 26 passed**. *Integration*, via the real extracted `revealPotentialSpots()` with hand-rolled Leaflet/DOM stubs — 13 assertions across 9 scenarios, including the key regression check that the same road/parcel geometry is excluded when numbered Hwy 16 (on the special list) but only partially trimmed when numbered an unlisted route like Hwy 11, and that Hwy 97 north of the Quesnel clip window gets 400 m while the same route number south of it (Okanagan) only gets 25 m — **all 13 passed**. `node --check` on the full extracted inline script confirmed no syntax errors throughout.

**Not yet visually confirmed against real BC data** — same standing caveat as every other map-drawing feature this session, now carrying extra weight given how much more specific this feature's numbers are: the coordinate clip windows for Hwy 3/5/97/99 are built from landmark knowledge and a couple of targeted web searches, not a surveyed boundary or a live check against the real DRA `HIGHWAY_ROUTE_NUMBER` values for those routes.

## v24: 400 m major-road/building buffer for Potential Spots, with partial-parcel clipping (superseded by v25 — see above)
*The buffer distance this section describes (a flat 400 m for both roads and buildings) was this session's own placeholder, not a cited regulation — v25, above, replaced it with the real, tiered Wildlife Act numbers after Karim supplied the actual regulations document. The clipping mechanism, data sources, and zoom-gating approach described below are otherwise unchanged.*

Karim's request: *"Potential shooting spots also cannot be within 400m of major infrastructure aka main roads, highways businesses etc. So if there is a portion of crown or untitled provincial that's outside the 400m just get rid of the portion that's within 400m not the entire parcel."* Two things made this the most involved feature added this session: finding a live, all-of-BC data source for "buildings," and actually trimming a parcel down to just its clear portion instead of an all-or-nothing overlap test.

**Scope clarification.** Asked Karim whether "businesses" meant literal commercial POIs; he redirected: *"It's more just buildings in general so use buildings."* That broadened the search from a business-POI dataset to a building-footprint dataset.

**Data sources, researched and confirmed live:**
- **Major roads/highways**: `WHSE_BASEMAPPING.DRA_DGTL_ROAD_ATLAS_MPAR_SP` (BC's Digital Road Atlas). Confirmed via a live `DescribeFeatureType` WFS call against this exact layer (not just documentation) that it carries real `ROAD_CLASS` (free-text classification) and `HIGHWAY_ROUTE_NUMBER` (populated only for numbered highways/routes) fields. The full `ROAD_CLASS` value domain isn't published anywhere reachable this session, so `MAJOR_ROAD_CQL` leans on `HIGHWAY_ROUTE_NUMBER IS NOT NULL` as the primary, reliable signal, OR'd with a loose substring match on `ROAD_CLASS` ('%highway%'/'%freeway%'/'%arterial%') as a safety net — the same "safe superset" philosophy already used for `CROWN_OWNER_CQL`: broad enough server-side to never miss a real highway, with no further client-side narrowing needed since any false positive here only makes the buffer *more* conservative.
- **Buildings**: BC has **no province-wide government building-footprint WFS/API** — the only candidate found (Parks Canada's own ArcGIS FeatureServer) is confirmed scoped to Parks Canada properties only, not general use. Asked Karim directly whether to skip buildings or use OpenStreetMap instead; he chose **OpenStreetMap**. Buildings are now fetched live per-viewport from the **Overpass API** (`overpass-api.de`, falling back to the `overpass.kumi.systems` mirror, then this app's existing `CORS_PROXY` wrapping the primary URL) via a `way["building"]`/`relation["building"]` query over the current bounds, `out geom` for inline coordinates, capped at 3000 buildings. OSM is community-sourced, not government-authoritative — every popup that used building data says so explicitly.

**Clipping approach.** Karim's requirement is unambiguous: a partially-affected Crown/untitled parcel must be trimmed down to its clear portion, not dropped wholesale. Exact polygon buffer + boolean difference is a real algorithm, but hand-rolling one correctly (self-intersections, multi-ring holes, buffer-arc approximation) was judged too error-prone to trust without a tested library — and no library could be verified in this sandbox: `npm install @turf/turf` was attempted first and failed outright (`registry.npmjs.org` returns 403 from this sandbox's network), and a follow-up check confirmed pypi.org, unpkg.com, and cdnjs.cloudflare.com are all blocked the same way. Rather than ship an untested exact-geometry algorithm, or a CDN dependency that could never be exercised locally before sending it to Karim, the feature instead uses a **grid-sampling approximation**: `sampleGridPoints()` lays an adaptive grid of points across a candidate parcel (`gridSpacingForRing()` targets ~350 points regardless of parcel size, clamped to 25–150 m spacing), keeps only the points that are both inside the parcel and ≥400 m from every nearby road segment and building footprint (`clipParcelByInfrastructure()`), and draws the survivors as small tiled squares (`squareAroundPoint()`) — a close visual approximation of "the parcel minus the excluded area," rather than an exact clipped polygon. This is a deliberate, documented trade-off (commented in the code), consistent with this project's established preference for hand-rolled, locally-unit-testable pure-JS math over an external dependency that can't be verified before shipping.

**Two-tier zoom gating.** The existing `SPOT_MIN_ZOOM` (9) still gates Potential Spots generally. A new, stricter `INFRA_MIN_ZOOM` (12) specifically gates the Overpass query plus the per-parcel grid-sampling compute cost, since both need a bounded viewport to stay fast. Below zoom 12, Potential Spots still works exactly as it did in v19–v23 — full, unclipped parcels, no 400 m filter — with an explicit note saying the buffer check hasn't run yet at this zoom, rather than silently skipping it.

**Fail-safe, not fail-open.** A failed major-road or OSM-buildings fetch at a good zoom must not be silently treated as "nothing nearby" — that would *under*-exclude, the opposite of what this feature exists to do. `infraChecked` requires the zoom to be tight enough **and** both infrastructure queries to have actually succeeded; if either fails, `revealPotentialSpots()` falls back to the same unfiltered full-parcel behaviour as being zoomed out, with its own explicit status-bar note ("Couldn't load major-road and/or OpenStreetMap building data for this view, so the 400m check didn't run this time...").

**What changed in the code:** `LAYERS.majorRoad` + `MAJOR_ROAD_CQL` added; a new ~290-line block of pure-JS math (`metersPerDegree`, `distanceMeters`, `pointToSegmentMeters`, `pointToRingMeters`, `pointToLineGeomMeters`, `bboxOfLineGeom`/`bboxOfGeom`/`expandBboxByMeters`, the Overpass query/parse functions, and the grid-sampling/clipping functions themselves); `spotPopup()` extended to explain, per spot, whether the buffer was checked, and if so whether the shown shape is the whole parcel or a trimmed portion; `revealPotentialSpots()` rewritten to conditionally fire the two extra queries only when zoomed in enough, compute `infraChecked`/`infraDataFailed`, and for each surviving Crown/untitled candidate either draw it whole (clear or unchecked), skip it entirely (`clip.empty`), or draw its kept grid-squares (`clip.clear === false`); the status-bar message extended with exact counts of parcels excluded entirely for infrastructure vs. trimmed down. The map legend gained a note explaining the solid-shape-vs-speckled-squares visual and the 400 m buffer.

**Verification, following this project's established two-pass pattern:**
1. *Pure math*, via the real extracted source run in a Node `vm` sandbox (not a hand-copied duplicate) — 25 assertions covering `distanceMeters`, `pointToSegmentMeters`, `pointToRingMeters`, `pointToLineGeomMeters`, `bboxOfGeom`/`expandBboxByMeters`, `gridSpacingForRing`/`sampleGridPoints`, and four realistic `clipParcelByInfrastructure` scenarios (a real partial trim by a road running through the west third of a rectangular parcel, a tiny parcel fully within 400 m of a road on every side, a parcel far from all infrastructure staying fully clear, and a partial trim driven by a building polygon instead of a road) — **all 25 passed**.
2. *Integration*, via the real extracted `revealPotentialSpots()` run the same way, with hand-rolled Leaflet/DOM stubs (chainable no-op `L.*`, a controllable mock `map` with settable zoom/bounds, a real `spotLayer` capturing every drawn shape+popup) and `queryLayer`/`queryOsmBuildings` swapped for scripted mocks per scenario — 20 assertions across 7 scenarios (below `SPOT_MIN_ZOOM` fires no queries; between `SPOT_MIN_ZOOM` and `INFRA_MIN_ZOOM` shows full unfiltered parcels with the right note; a parcel partially overlapping a road draws multiple kept squares, all genuinely ≥400 m away, with the right "N trimmed" count; a parcel entirely within 400 m of a road draws nothing and is counted as excluded; a parcel far from all infrastructure draws as one whole clear shape; a major-road fetch failure falls back to unfiltered with the explicit failure note; a park-overlapping parcel is still hard-excluded regardless of the infra buffer) — **all 20 passed**.

`node --check` on the full extracted inline script also confirmed no syntax errors after every edit.

**Not yet visually confirmed against real BC data** — same caveat as every other map-drawing feature this session: this is math/logic-tested (Node `vm` + Playwright-style mocks), not eyeballed against a real BC road, a real OSM-mapped building, and a real partially-clipped Crown parcel in an actual browser yet. Worth a real-world check together with the still-outstanding v17–v23 real-browser confirmations (see Known limitations).

## v22: minimalist redesign — report cards, legend and checklist collapse behind "Details"
Karim's request: *"Let's make it a bit more minimalist. Not removing any of the information but making it hidden behind 'Details' buttons and other pop ups that can be activated."* Applied across the three places on the page that were showing the most permanent text:

- **Report cards.** Each of the 8 cards renderReport() draws (Parcel, Municipal boundary, Bylaw lookup, Park, Forestry, Crown tenures, Woodlot/managed licence, Road access) already led with a compact one-line "hit-pill" summary (e.g. "inside", "2 nearby", "no overlap"). The fuller field-by-field breakdown below that pill now starts collapsed behind a small "Details ▾" button next to the pill, expanding in place on click (button becomes "Hide ▾", chevron rotates); the underlying data and copy is unchanged, just hidden until asked for. A new `cardHTML(icon, title, pillHtml, bodyHtml, collapsible)` helper builds every card now, replacing the old per-card manual string concatenation. **Genuinely single-line cards skip the toggle entirely** — Municipal boundary (outside/inside + name, one line) and Park (no-overlap/inside + name, one line) render their one line directly with no button, since hiding a single sentence behind a click would just be an extra click for nothing. The other five cards (Parcel, Bylaw lookup, Forestry, Crown tenures, Woodlot, Road access) do collapse, since their bodies run 2–6 lines. All toggles are wired through one delegated click listener on `#report`, so re-rendering the report after a new click doesn't need to re-bind anything.
- **Map legend.** The 16-row swatch key (bottom-left over the map) now starts collapsed behind a "Map key ▾" header click, so it no longer permanently occupies map real estate — same rows, same content, just hidden by default.
- **Checklist helper text.** The paragraph explaining the AUTO/MANUAL tag convention and the "nothing is saved" note (previously a permanent `<p>` above the checklist) is now behind a small "i" info button next to the "✅ Full checklist" heading, using the same modal component already used for the header blurb, disclaimer, and footer sources.
- **Deliberately left alone:** the verdict box (colour-coded conclusion + reasons list) stays fully visible always — it's the actual answer the tool exists to give, not supplementary detail. The two map-action buttons and their status note also stay visible, since they're controls, not information to hide.
- Verified via Playwright with a full mocked `renderReport()` payload (all 7 data layers populated, one multi-parcel case): 8 cards rendered, exactly 5 carried a Details toggle (Parcel, Bylaw lookup, Forestry, Crown tenures, Road access — Woodlot had 0 records in the mock so it also skipped the toggle, correctly), all toggles started collapsed, clicking one correctly expanded its body and flipped its label to "Hide"; separately confirmed the legend starts collapsed and opens on click, and the checklist info button opens the modal with the relocated helper text. Also `node --check` on the extracted inline script confirmed no syntax errors from the refactor.
- **Not yet visually confirmed by Karim** — same category of caveat as the button features: logic- and Playwright-tested in this sandbox, not eyeballed in a real browser yet.

## v23: surrounding parcels within 1 km highlighted for context
Karim's request: *"Surrounding parcels for 1km also need to be highlighted when you place a pin."* Until now, `renderMapOverlays()` only ever drew the parcel(s) returned by the exact-point `INTERSECTS` query used for the report/verdict -- clicking near a boundary gave no visual sense of what was on the other side of it.

Added a second, parallel parcel query in `runLookup()`'s existing `Promise.all` batch: `DWITHIN(SHAPE, <clicked point>, 1000, meters)` on the same `WHSE_CADASTRE.PMBC_PARCEL_FABRIC_POLY_SVW` layer, using the same `SEARCH_RADIUS_M` (1000 m) every other proximity layer here already uses, capped at a new `NEARBY_PARCEL_MAX_FEATURES` (400) so a dense urban block of small lots doesn't try to render an unbounded number of shapes. `renderMapOverlays()` draws this result *before* the exact-point parcel(s), de-duped against them by `PARCEL_FABRIC_POLY_ID` (falling back to `OBJECTID` if that field is ever absent) so the clicked parcel never gets a second, dim copy drawn underneath its own highlighted one. Surrounding parcels are drawn thin and faded (`weight:1, fillOpacity:0.07, opacity:0.55`) in the same `OWNER_COLORS` ownership palette the primary parcel already uses, each still clickable for its own popup (`parcelPopup()`, unchanged) -- the parcel actually under the pin keeps its existing full-strength styling (`weight:2, fillOpacity:0.22`) so it still reads as *the* answer at a glance.

Deliberately scoped to the map only: the verdict, reasons list, and checklist all stay based purely on the parcel at the exact clicked point, same as before -- a neighbouring private parcel 400 m away has no bearing on whether shooting is legal *here*. The one addition to the report itself is a single status line in the (already-collapsible) Parcel card body -- "N more parcels within 1 km shown faded on the map for context" -- so the faded shapes on the map are explained rather than left as an unlabelled visual change; a matching line was also added to the map legend.

Verified via Playwright with a mocked payload containing four parcel records: the clicked parcel (private), a duplicate of the clicked parcel by `PARCEL_FABRIC_POLY_ID` (correctly deduped -- not drawn a second time), a Crown-provincial neighbour (drawn faded, in green), and a neighbour with no `PARCEL_FABRIC_POLY_ID` at all (correctly deduped/matched via the `OBJECTID` fallback). Confirmed the drawn styles split correctly into one full-strength shape (`fillOpacity:0.22, weight:2`) and two faded ones (`fillOpacity:0.07, weight:1`), and that the report card's new status line reads "2 more parcels within 1 km shown faded on the map for context." for that fixture. `node --check` on the extracted inline script confirmed no syntax errors.

**Not yet visually confirmed against real BC data** -- same caveat as every other map-drawing feature this session: logic/DOM-tested via Playwright mocks in this sandbox, not eyeballed against a real dense subdivision or a real backcountry Crown/private boundary in a real browser yet.

## Crown land presumption: five iterations, now settled on on-demand buttons (v14–v19)
This is worth recording as one continuous thread since each step directly followed feedback on the previous one:

1. **v14** — "make provincial crown automatically there for all of BC" was built as: a click with no parcel record reads as presumptive Crown land in the report. Reverted after Karim clarified this wasn't what he meant.
2. **v15** — Karim clarified he meant it on the map itself, always visible, independent of clicking. Built as an **inversion**: tint the whole viewport green, draw real parcels on top so gaps read as Crown. Gated to zoom 12+, capped at 1500 features.
3. **v16** — tuned per feedback: lower zoom gate, better satellite contrast, basemap-toggle restyle from cache.
4. **v17** — Karim asked to remove the feature entirely: *"Let's remove 'Assumed crown land' entirely."* Every piece deleted, verified via Playwright.
5. **v18** — Karim asked again, this time explicit about mechanism: *"Let's make it so all the untitled provincial and crown land are highlighted permanently... Don't make any assumptions just take the data from the parcel maps."* Built as a genuine **positive query** of ParcelMap BC's real `OWNER_TYPE` attribute (researched via LTSA's own documentation: 9 official categories including Crown Provincial and Untitled Provincial), refreshed automatically on every pan/zoom via a `moveend` listener, gated to zoom 8+, capped at 2000 features.
6. **v19 (current)** — Karim reported v18's auto-refresh-on-every-pan/zoom "is making the app very slow", and asked for two things in the same message: (a) make Crown-land highlighting load "for a specific area" via a button labeled "Reveal Crown Land" that shows Crown/untitled land for the current view, and (b) a second button, built "in the same way," showing "potential shooting spots" for the current view.
   - Before building the spots feature, asked Karim directly what should count as a "spot," since parks/municipal boundaries don't always align with parcel ownership type (a Crown-owned parcel can still sit inside a park or municipality). **Karim's answer: "We want to make it satisfy all of the conditions of the checklist."**
   - `refreshCrownParcelLayer()` was renamed to `revealCrownLand()`, the `moveend` listener and its 300ms debounce timer were removed entirely, and the function is now wired to a new "🗺️ Reveal Crown Land (this view)" button instead — it queries once per press, for whatever the view is at that moment; panning afterward leaves the last result on screen until pressed again. Same zoom-8 gate, same 2000-feature cap, same stale-request guard (now guarding against a double-click racing itself rather than rapid pan events).
   - New "🎯 Show Potential Spots (this view)" button runs `revealPotentialSpots()`: queries Crown/untitled parcels **plus**, in parallel, parks, municipal boundaries, Crown tenures, forest managed licences, and forestry openings/cutblocks — all viewport-BBOX, all for the same view. A Crown/untitled parcel is excluded if it overlaps a park or a municipal boundary (hard, matches the click report's red logic); otherwise it's drawn as a potential spot, with tenure/woodlot/cutblock overlaps surfaced as ⚠ advisories in its popup (soft, matches the click report's amber logic) rather than excluding it.
   - DataBC's WFS only answers point-in-polygon and viewport-BBOX queries, not polygon-vs-polygon, so testing whether two *fetched* polygons (a Crown parcel and a park, say) overlap has to happen client-side. Rather than pull in an external geometry library (turf.js) sight-unseen — this sandbox can't load a CDN script against real network conditions to confirm its exact API surface before shipping — a small hand-rolled ray-casting + segment-intersection test (`geomsIntersect()`, `pointInRing()`, `segmentsIntersect()`, with a bounding-box pre-check for speed) was written instead, specifically so it could be verified locally with plain Node unit tests (10 cases: overlap, disjoint, full-containment both directions, symmetry, MultiPolygon, null-safety) with zero network dependency — a stronger verification path in this sandbox than trusting an unexercised CDN library would have been. Known simplification: it treats only the outer ring of a Polygon/MultiPolygon (holes ignored) — a donut-shaped park excluding an inner enclave is the one realistic case this gets wrong.
   - Fixed a related gap surfaced while building this: the click report's per-parcel reasoning (`renderReport`) had no branch for the `untitled-provincial` category added in v18 — an actual click on untitled Crown land was falling into the generic gray "not automatically classifiable" bucket instead of being treated as green/permitted like Crown provincial land. Added the missing branch (green reasoning, `off`/green pill) so the click report and the new button-driven highlighting agree with each other.
   - Verified via Playwright: no query fires on `moveend` any more (button-only); the Reveal Crown Land button fires exactly one query per press and doesn't re-fire on a subsequent pan; zoom gating still blocks both buttons below their thresholds with an explanatory note; the Potential Spots button correctly excludes parcels overlapping a mocked park/municipality while still showing a clean parcel and a tenured-but-otherwise-clear parcel (with its advisory); the `untitled-provincial` fix was confirmed against `renderReport` directly. Plus a separate, network-free Node unit-test pass on the polygon-overlap math itself.
   - **Not yet visually confirmed against real BC data in a real browser** — same caveat as v18, carried forward: the sandbox can't reach `openmaps.gov.bc.ca` directly, so this is logic-tested via mocks only, not confirmed end-to-end with real parcel/park/municipality geometry. **This caveat turned out to matter — see the v20 bug fix below.**

## v20 bug fix: BBOX axis order was backwards
Karim's first real-world test of the v19 buttons: *"The shooting spots button just says it can't load the parcel data."* After a first (wrong) guess that it was query-load/concurrency (sequencing the parcel query to run alone first, and surfacing the real underlying error string instead of a generic message), Karim tried again and got a more specific error: *"It says Couldn't load parcel data (network error)"* — meaning the JSONP `<script>` tag itself failed to load, i.e. the server was rejecting the request outright, not just being slow.

With the parcel query now running alone (no concurrency to blame) and failing with a load-level error rather than a timeout, the CQL itself became the prime suspect — specifically `BBOX()`, since it's the one spatial predicate this app had never used before v18 (every earlier feature used `INTERSECTS`/`DWITHIN` with a WKT point instead) and was flagged as unconfirmed-against-real-data in every version's limitations since v15.

Researched GeoServer's own axis-ordering documentation (`docs.geoserver.org/.../wfs/axis_order.html`, plus a GeoServer Enterprise mirror since the canonical page 404'd): WFS 2.0.0 resolves an explicit CRS code against the EPSG registry's own defined axis order, and EPSG:4326's registry-defined order is **lat/lon** — the opposite of the lng/lat order this app uses everywhere else (matching GeoJSON/WKT convention, and matching what already works for every point-based `INTERSECTS`/`DWITHIN` query via `srsName=EPSG:4326` set at the top level of the request). `viewportBboxCql()` was passing `'EPSG:4326'` as `BBOX()`'s own explicit sixth argument — a context where, per that documentation, GeoServer was likely reading the coordinates back to front. Since BC longitudes (~ -114 to -139) are outside the valid ±90 latitude range, a swapped BBOX would be flatly invalid, which fits a hard server-side rejection (not a timeout) far better than any other explanation.

Couldn't confirm this against the live server directly — the WebFetch tool's own proxy 403-rejects any URL containing raw parentheses (confirmed by testing: a `CQL_FILTER` with no parens went through and reached the real server, replaced with a straightforward Read timeout on an enormous unfiltered query; anything with `BBOX(...)` or `strToLowerCase(...)` was blocked before it ever left this session, regardless of encoding) — so this is a reasoned fix from documentation and the failure's shape, not a confirmed root-cause. **Fix**: dropped the explicit `'EPSG:4326'` argument from `BBOX()` entirely, so it inherits the same already-proven lon/lat context every other query in this file already relies on, rather than re-asserting its own (evidently wrong) axis order. This is a single change in `viewportBboxCql()`, shared by both `revealCrownLand()` and `revealPotentialSpots()` — meaning **Reveal Crown Land was likely also broken this whole time**, just not yet reported.

**Still not confirmed against real BC data** — same limitation as before, now doubly important to check: this fix is reasoned from documentation, not verified live.

## v21 bug fixes: over-strict CQL match, a false-alarm syntax scare, and BBOX replaced with INTERSECTS/WKT
Three more rounds of real-world feedback after the v20 fix shipped, in order:

**1. "It's saying no qualifying spots when there should be.
