What is the criteria for a qualified spot?"** Answered the question directly (Crown/untitled ownership, hard-excluded by park/municipality overlap, tenure/woodlot/cutblock as advisories only — per Karim's own "satisfy all of the conditions of the checklist" direction from v19), then kept digging since the report implied a real bug, not just a misunderstanding. Found one: `CROWN_OWNER_CQL` required the server-side match to contain **both** "crown" *and* "provincial" as substrings — but the client-side `ownerCategory()` guard (the thing that does the final, precise classification) was already looser, matching "crown" alone. If a live `OWNER_TYPE` value said something like "Crown Land" without the word "provincial" in it, the server-side filter would silently drop a genuine Crown parcel before the client guard ever saw it. **Fix**: loosened `CROWN_OWNER_CQL` to `crown OR untitled` server-side, leaning on the already-correct client-side guard to do the precise filtering afterward — the server-side filter's only job is to be a safe superset, never to under-match. Also fixed a real UX ambiguity in `revealPotentialSpots()`'s status message: it used to conflate "zero Crown/untitled parcels found at all" with "some found but every one got excluded by a park/municipality overlap" into one confusing sentence. Now `candidates.length`, `excludedPark`, and `excludedMuni` are tracked and reported separately, so the message says exactly which of those happened.

**2. "The crown land button is no longer working."** First chased a false alarm: a Grep result appeared to show a mangled `//` comment rendered as a single `/`, which would be a fatal unterminated-regex syntax error if real. Verified directly against the file's actual bytes (`cat -A` plus `node --check` on the extracted inline script) that this was a tool-output rendering artifact only — the file was never broken. With no real lead and the report too vague to diagnose blind a third time, brought `revealCrownLand()`'s diagnostic messaging up to the same precision already built for the Spots button (distinguishing "load/network failure" from "query succeeded, 0 parcels" from "query succeeded, N parcels, none classified as Crown/untitled"), and asked Karim directly for the exact on-screen wording instead of guessing again.

**3. "No Crown provincial / untitled provincial parcels found in this view (query returned 0 parcels). ... It should be returning many parcels."** This was the exact wording asked for in fix #2 above, and it revealed the v20 fix (dropping the explicit `'EPSG:4326'` argument from `BBOX()`) had traded one bug for another rather than resolving it: a syntactically valid query that legitimately returns zero rows, not a load/network failure. Reasoned explanation: with no explicit CRS argument, GeoServer's `BBOX()` most likely falls back to interpreting the four raw numbers in the **layer's native storage SRS** — almost certainly BC Albers (EPSG:3005), which is in *metres*, not degrees — rather than defaulting to lon/lat degrees. A viewport bounding box expressed in degrees (~ -123, ~49) but evaluated as BC-Albers metres describes a location far outside BC, or an internally-invalid box, either of which would legitimately return 0 rows without erroring.

Rather than guess at `BBOX()`'s CRS-handling a third time — two guesses in a row (axis-order swap in v20's first attempt, then CRS-argument removal) had each turned out to be wrong or incomplete — abandoned the `BBOX()` CQL function entirely. **Fix**: replaced it with `INTERSECTS(geomField, SRID=4326;POLYGON((...)))`, reusing the exact same EWKT (`SRID=4326;...`) convention that every point-based query in this app (via the pre-existing `pt(lat,lng)` helper) has used successfully since long before this feature existed. New `viewportPolygonWkt(bounds)` builds the viewport rectangle as a closed WKT ring in lon/lat order; `viewportBboxCql()` now wraps it in `INTERSECTS(...)` instead of calling `BBOX(...)`. This was framed to Karim explicitly as the more defensible fix precisely *because* it reuses a mechanism already proven to work, rather than continuing to reason about `BBOX()`'s undocumented default behaviour.

As with the v20 fix, this couldn't be confirmed against the live server directly — the WebFetch proxy's parenthesis-blocking (limitation 22) rules out testing `INTERSECTS(...)` from this sandbox too. **Not yet confirmed by Karim as of this writing** — this is the single most important open item carried into the next session (see Known limitations #17 and Next steps below).

## Live bylaw fetch + parse in the app (v13) — confirmed working
After v12 shipped the search-link version, Karim asked directly: **"Can it not parse data live in the app"** — i.e. have the app itself go get the real bylaw text, not just a link to search for it.

The Claude Artifact runtime (checked via the `artifact-capabilities` skill) offers no "page asks an AI a live question" capability, so the app can't research an arbitrary bylaw on its own — but a plain browser-side script can fetch a specific, known document URL and parse it. `BYLAW_SOURCES` is a small, hand-verified table (jurisdiction name → direct URL of the real bylaw document + PDF/HTML) covering **Chilliwack** (Bylaw No. 2740), **Abbotsford** (Discharge of Firearms Regulation By-law), **Kelowna** (Bylaw No. 9779). `renderBylawLookup()` fetches (direct, falling back to the `api.allorigins.win` CORS proxy), parses via **pdf.js** or `DOMParser`, and pattern-matches the operative prohibition clause. Every failure mode degrades gracefully with an explicit message. **Karim tested this live in his own browser and confirmed the fetch works** — the CORS-proxy fallback and pdf.js's in-browser parse both function outside the sandbox.

## On-the-spot bylaw search link, generic fallback (v12, extended by v13)
Karim declined pre-compiling bylaws region-by-region and asked to "query on the spot ... based on what place you've chosen on the map." `bylawSearchLink(jurisdictionName, kind)` builds a Google search URL from whichever jurisdiction name came back on that click, regenerated fresh every time — still the fallback for any jurisdiction not yet in `BYLAW_SOURCES`.

## Satellite hybrid: roads + labels over imagery (v11)
`BASE_LAYERS.satellite` is an `L.layerGroup` of three stacked Esri tile layers — World_Imagery, Reference/World_Transportation, Reference/World_Boundaries_and_Places. Tile *images* still not visually confirmed in this sandbox.

## Planned cutblocks: merging in FTEN's cutting-permit data (v9)
RESULTS Openings only exists once there's silviculture activity, so a merely-authorized/planned block was invisible. Fixed by adding `FTEN_CUT_BLOCK_POLY_SVW`, merged with RESULTS by `OPENING_ID`.

## UI condensation: info-button modals + collapsed cutblock lines (v8)
A generic modal backs the header blurb, the disclaimer, the footer's data-source list, and each cutblock report line. (Extended in v22 — see above — to also collapse the report cards' field-level detail, the map legend, and the checklist helper text.)

## Parcel z-order fix (v7)
Parcels draw first (bottom of the click-report stack) so they never steal clicks/hovers from smaller layers inside them.

## Search radius widened to 1 km + road network + hover-for-roads (v6)
`SEARCH_RADIUS_M` = 1000; connected road network via a `MULTIPOINT`-based `DWITHIN` query; roads use hover tooltips.

## Ethical data sourcing (v5, resolved)
Source the same open government data backroadstatus.com uses directly, rather than scrape it; link out to it for its community-reported layer instead.

## Data sources (confirmed via DataBC WFS, live GeoJSON queries — plus a few other free services)
Base endpoint: `https://openmaps.gov.bc.ca/geo/pub/{LAYER}/ows` (OGC WFS 2.0, GeoServer, `CQL_FILTER` with `INTERSECTS`/`DWITHIN` — both map-action buttons used `BBOX` through v19/v20, but as of v21 they use `INTERSECTS` with an explicit `SRID=4326;POLYGON(...)` WKT viewport rectangle instead, the same EWKT convention every point-based query already relied on -- see the v21 bug fixes above; `BBOX` is no longer used anywhere in this app).

| Purpose | Layer (typeName) | Geometry field |
|---|---|---|
| Parcels / ownership type (click report + both map-action buttons) | `WHSE_CADASTRE.PMBC_PARCEL_FABRIC_POLY_SVW` | `SHAPE` |
| Municipal boundaries | `WHSE_LEGAL_ADMIN_BOUNDARIES.ABMS_MUNICIPALITIES_SP` | `SHAPE` |
| Parks / ecological reserves / protected areas | `WHSE_TANTALIS.TA_PARK_ECORES_PA_SVW` | `SHAPE` |
| Forestry cutblocks / openings, silviculture record (1 km radius on click; viewport BBOX for Potential Spots; `OPENING_STATUS_CODE` partly decoded to plain English as of v27) | `WHSE_FOREST_VEGETATION.RSLT_OPENING_SVW` | `GEOMETRY` |
| Harvest completion dates (joined by `OPENING_ID`, 1 km radius) | `WHSE_FOREST_VEGETATION.VEG_CONSOLIDATED_CUT_BLOCKS_SP` | `SHAPE` |
| Cutting-permit blocks incl. planned harvest date (1 km radius, merged by `OPENING_ID`; an implausibly large record is flagged and skipped when drawing as of v26; `LIFE_CYCLE_STATUS_CODE` shown as "Life Cycle" in the cutblock popup as of v29, and as the headline value in the condensed report-card/overlap-chooser lines as of v31) | `WHSE_FOREST_TENURE.FTEN_CUT_BLOCK_POLY_SVW` | `GEOMETRY` |
| Crown land tenures (leases/licences/permits) | `WHSE_TANTALIS.TA_CROWN_TENURES_SVW` | `SHAPE` |
| Forest managed licences (woodlot/community forest/etc.) | `WHSE_FOREST_TENURE.FTEN_MANAGED_LICENCE_POLY_SVW` | `GEOMETRY` |
| Forest service road tenure sections (1 km radius + connected network, hover for info) | `WHSE_FOREST_TENURE.FTEN_ROAD_SECTION_LINES_SVW` | `GEOMETRY` |
| Roads (all, not just major), for Potential Spots' building/road buffer (v24, distances corrected in v25) | `WHSE_BASEMAPPING.DRA_DGTL_ROAD_ATLAS_MPAR_SP` (BC Digital Road Atlas) | `GEOMETRY` |
| Recreation sites / campsites (1 km radius, v28) | `WHSE_FOREST_TENURE.FTEN_REC_SITE_POINTS_SVW` | `GEOMETRY` |
| Recreation reserves / areas (1 km radius, v28) | `WHSE_FOREST_TENURE.FTEN_RECREATION_POLY_SVW` | `GEOMETRY` |
| Recreation trails (1 km radius, v28) | `WHSE_FOREST_TENURE.FTEN_RECREATION_LINES_SVW` | `GEOMETRY` |
| Checked and ruled out as a Crown-land-inventory source: `WHSE_TANTALIS.TA_CROWN_INCLUSIONS_SVW` | narrow tenure mechanism, not a Crown land inventory | — |

Plus non-DataBC free services: **DriveBC's Open511 API** for live highway events; **Esri's `server.arcgisonline.com`** for the satellite hybrid basemap; a plain Google Search URL for the generic bylaw fallback; direct fetches (via `api.allorigins.win` as a CORS proxy) of specific municipal bylaw documents, confirmed working live; **pdf.js** for client-side PDF text extraction; **LTSA's ParcelMap BC help documentation** (`help.ltsa.ca`) researched for the real `OWNER_TYPE` category list behind the v18/v19 features; **OpenStreetMap's Overpass API** (v24), for building footprints province-wide since no BC government equivalent exists — community-sourced, not government-authoritative, and every popup using it says so. No external geometry library is used for either the v19 polygon-overlap check or the v24 buffer/clipping math — both hand-rolled instead (see the v19 and v24 sections above).

Known **not yet integrated**: guide-outfitter territories, traplines, grazing/range agreements, mineral claims (all confirmed as real, queryable DataBC layers during v28's research — see the v28 section above — but not built, since Karim chose recreation sites/trails instead); Indian reserve / federal land boundaries; wildfire closures & bans (BC Wildfire Service's ArcGIS Online feeds, also confirmed real during v28's research but not built); Wildlife Management Unit boundaries; DataBC's BC Geocoder (address search); a real bylaw-text dataset (none exists province-wide). ParcelMap BC itself (per LTSA's own documentation, researched when Karim asked whether private parcels were missing from coverage) describes itself as "the current, complete and trusted mapped representation of titled and Crown land parcels across British Columbia" — i.e. a single unified fabric covering private, Crown, municipal, federal, First Nations and mixed-ownership parcels together, not something with a separate "private parcels" layer that could be missing. It is explicitly not the legal authority (LTSA's Land Title Register is), so very recent survey plans/subdivisions can lag a few business days behind actual registration.

## Road access & conditions feature (v5, extended in v6)
FTEN road network query + `queryOpen511(lat,lng)`. FTEN road layer is administrative/tenure status only, not physical drivability; DriveBC only covers highways.

## Cutblock completion date (resolved, v4; extended v9)
See "Planned cutblocks" above.

## CORS fix (resolved, v2/v3)
DataBC's WFS server doesn't send `Access-Control-Allow-Origin`. Fixed with a JSONP fallback. (Open511 has no JSONP option. The v13 bylaw fetch uses a CORS-proxy fallback instead.)

## Layout bugs fixed (v4)
Two `flex-wrap`/`position:absolute` height-collapse bugs, fixed with scoped `flex-wrap:nowrap` and an explicit mobile height on `#mapWrap`.

## Regulatory framework researched
- No comprehensive **provincial** restriction on recreational target shooting location outside of parks/protected areas and Wildlife Act "Closed Areas" / regional "no-shooting special areas". Source: Wildlife Act, [Closed Areas Regulation](https://www.bclaws.gov.bc.ca/civix/document/id/complete/statreg/76_84_01), and [Silvercore's "Where Can You Legally Shoot in BC"](https://www.silvercore.ca/blog/legal-shooting-areas-in-british-columbia-and-canada1).
- **Municipal bylaws are the critical, most restrictive layer** — no central registry/API exists (v13 fetches real text for a handful of jurisdictions, confirmed working).
- BC has no single positive province-wide "Crown land" *dataset*, confirmed via `TA_CROWN_INCLUSIONS_SVW` — but ParcelMap BC's per-parcel `OWNER_TYPE` attribute does classify individual parcels as Crown Provincial / Untitled Provincial, which both map-action buttons rely on.
- ParcelMap BC's official owner-type categories (per LTSA's own help docs, `help.ltsa.ca/parcelmap-bc/owner-type`): Private, Federal, Crown Provincial, Crown Agency, Local Government, First Nations, Mixed Ownership, Untitled Provincial, Unclassified.
- ParcelMap BC covers "over two million" active titled and Crown-surveyed parcels province-wide, per LTSA's own "About ParcelMap BC" page, and is positioned as the complete spatial representation of titled land (private included) — not a partial or Crown-only product. It's a snapshot, though: new survey plans enter a maintenance queue with a publish target of roughly two business days after registration.
- Some regional districts (e.g. Thompson-Nicola, Fraser Valley RD district-wide) don't appear to have a single dedicated discharge-of-firearms bylaw — may live in electoral-area-specific bylaws instead.
- Squamish-Lillooet RD regulates firearms **per electoral area**, not district-wide — left out of the `BYLAW_SOURCES` table.
- **Provincial Parks / Ecological Reserves**: discharge prohibited under the Park Act except as authorized.
- Federal: PAL required, Criminal Code careless-use provisions apply everywhere.
- `bclaws.gov.bc.ca`, `canlii.org`, and `for.gov.bc.ca` all block automated fetching (robots.txt).
- **RESULTS `OPENING_STATUS_CODE` domain values are not fully verified** — colouring only trusts `FG`/`RET`. (v27: three RESULTS codes — APP, FG, RET — and six FTA cutting-permit/cutblock file-status codes are now sourced and decoded in the UI; see the v27 section above. Colouring logic itself is unchanged.)
- **BC has no province-wide government building-footprint dataset** (v24) — only a Parks-Canada-scoped ArcGIS FeatureServer was found, not general-use. OpenStreetMap's Overpass API is the only live, all-of-BC building source found, and was adopted with Karim's explicit sign-off.
- **The real Wildlife Act discharge-of-firearm distances** (v25, replacing v24's invented 400 m placeholder), per the 2026-2028 BC Hunting and Trapping Regulations Synopsis Karim supplied: 100 m from an occupied church/school/dwelling/farm-ranch building/playground/regional district building; a 15 m road allowance by default for any numbered highway or 2-lane+ public road (15 m from the midline under 3 lanes, or from the paved edge at 3+ lanes); and 400 m specifically on a named list of highways (some single-projectile-only, some a full ban) plus a handful of named local No Shooting Areas per region not yet implemented here (see Known limitations).
- **Recreation Sites and Trails BC (RSTBC) has no lifecycle/status field on its rec-site/campsite points layer** (v28) — confirmed via live `DescribeFeatureType`, not assumed; the polygon (recreation reserves/areas) and line (trails) layers do carry `LIFE_CYCLE_STATUS_CODE`/`FILE_STATUS_CODE`, but the points layer doesn't, so an inactive or decommissioned rec site can still appear as a hit with no way for this app to know.
- **Cutblock `LIFE_CYCLE_STATUS_CODE`'s exact three-value domain is directly documented** (v29), unlike most of this app's other status/code fields: BC's own Open Government Portal listing for the Forest Tenure Cutblock Polygons dataset spells out PENDING/ACTIVE/RETIRED in plain English. Confirmed via live `DescribeFeatureType` that the field exists on FTEN's cutting-permit layer (`FTEN_CUT_BLOCK_POLY_SVW`) but not on RESULTS' opening layer (`RSLT_OPENING_SVW`).

## Known limitations / open items for next session
1. Municipal bylaw coverage is real (and confirmed working) but partial: only Chilliwack, Abbotsford, and Kelowna have a live-fetched source; everywhere else falls back to the search link.
2. No address search yet (lat/lng only).
3. Persistence/hosting unresolved: Artifact tool's CSP blocks live `fetch`/JSONP/Leaflet-CDN/tile requests, and no "ask an AI" capability exists either. Desktop-bridge sync has been flaky.
4. Guide-outfitter territories, traplines, grazing/range agreements, and mineral claims remain unintegrated — confirmed as real, queryable DataBC layers during v28's research (Karim's recommended option), but not built since he chose recreation sites/trails instead. Available for a future round.
5. Consolidated Cutblocks' historical coverage window isn't confirmed current-day.
6. Open511/DriveBC CORS behaviour not confirmed against the live API from a real browser session.
7. FTEN road layer is administrative/tenure status only, not physical road condition.
8. The connected-road network query is one hop only.
9. The `MULTIPOINT` endpoint list for the connected-road query is capped at 40 points.
10. The parcel z-order fix (v7) was verified via a Playwright draw-order mock, not a real-browser visual check.
11. The v8 modal UI hasn't had a real mobile-device pass yet.
12. The FTEN cutting-permit merge (v9) hasn't been checked against a real block Karim can verify by eye.
13. `BLOCK_STATUS_CODE`/`HARVEST_AUTH_STATUS_CODE`/`OPENING_STATUS_CODE` values from RESULTS/FTEN are only partially verified against authoritative BC documentation — as of v27, 9 codes (APP, FG, RET, PE, PP, PI, HI, LC, HC) are sourced and decoded in the UI (see the v27 section above); any other code that appears is still shown as-is, unmatched, because BC hasn't published one complete table covering every value these fields can hold.
14. The satellite hybrid basemap (v10/v11) hasn't been visually confirmed with real tile images in this sandbox.
15. The v13 `BYLAW_SOURCES` table currently covers 3 jurisdictions. Growing it happens incrementally, as-needed.
16. `extractBylawExcerpt()`'s keyword pattern-matching is best-effort — already caught and fixed one wrong-match case; other real bylaws may still defeat it.
17. **Neither the Reveal Crown Land nor the Potential Spots button has been visually confirmed against real BC data.** Both were logic-tested via mocks (Playwright + plain Node unit tests for the polygon-overlap math). Three real-world bug reports (v20, v21) traced through a concurrency fix, a CQL over-strict-match fix, and finally replacing `BBOX()` entirely with `INTERSECTS(...)`/WKT — see the v20 and v21 sections above. Real-browser confirmation that the v21 fix actually returns real parcels is now the single most important open item; two prior fixes to this same code path (v20's axis-order guess, then its CRS-argument-removal guess) each turned out to be wrong or incomplete, so treat this as still unconfirmed until Karim says otherwise.
18. **The hand-rolled `geomsIntersect()` overlap test ignores polygon holes** (treats only outer rings) — a donut-shaped park excluding an inner enclave would be treated as fully solid. Realistic but uncommon case.
19. **Potential Spots' advisories only check for overlap, not the 1 km proximity radius the click report uses for cutblocks.** The click report flags cutblocks *within 1 km* of the exact point; the viewport-wide Potential Spots check only flags a cutblock that directly overlaps the candidate parcel's own polygon — a practical approximation, not identical logic, and worth knowing if the two ever seem to disagree at the same location.
20. If Karim asks for further changes to Crown/untitled land or shooting-spot highlighting, note this is now the 10th round of work in this general area (v14 → v15 → v16 → v17 → v18 → v19 → v20 → v21 → v24 → v25) — check what specifically he wants changed about the *current* approach rather than assuming a return to an earlier design.
21. **If the v21 `INTERSECTS`/WKT fix doesn't actually resolve the "0 parcels" / "should be returning many parcels" issue, don't guess again blind.** This code path has now had three fix attempts in a row reasoned from documentation rather than confirmed live (v20's axis-order swap, v20's CRS-argument removal, v21's BBOX→INTERSECTS replacement) — the first two both turned out wrong or incomplete. If v21 also doesn't work, the right move is to stop reasoning from documentation and ask Karim to open the browser's dev tools (F12) → Network tab, click the button, find the failing/empty request to `openmaps.gov.bc.ca`, and share the actual request URL and response body — a real GeoServer error message (or a real returned feature count) beats a fourth guess.
22. **The WebFetch tool's proxy 403-rejects any URL containing raw parentheses**, discovered while debugging limitation 21 -- this blocks testing *any* `BBOX(...)`/`strToLowerCase(...)`/other function-call-shaped CQL_FILTER against the live server from this sandbox, not just this specific query. A paren-free `CQL_FILTER` (e.g. a bare `OWNER_TYPE IS NOT NULL`) does get through the proxy and reach the real server, so simple attribute filters remain testable if that's ever useful, but no spatial-function filter is verifiable this way.
23. **The v22 collapsible-details redesign (report cards, legend, checklist info button) hasn't been visually confirmed by Karim in a real browser either** — logic/DOM-tested via Playwright with a mocked full report only. Worth a look together with limitation 17 next session.
24. **The v23 surrounding-parcels-within-1km highlighting hasn't been visually confirmed against real BC data either** — logic/DOM-tested via Playwright mocks only (dedup logic, styling split, report status line). Worth checking against both a dense urban subdivision (does the 400-feature cap ever actually bite, and does it look cluttered?) and a sparse backcountry area (does a Crown/private boundary read clearly at a glance) next session.
25. **The v24/v25 building/road buffer hasn't been visually confirmed against real BC data either** — Node `vm`-based unit and integration tests only (26 math assertions + 13 integration assertions after v25, both against the real extracted source, all passing). Specifically unconfirmed: whether the DRA `HIGHWAY_ROUTE_NUMBER` field's real values actually match the exact strings `normalizeRouteNumber()`/`SPECIAL_400M_ROUTES` expect (a route stored as e.g. `"5 "` with trailing text, or a different format entirely, would silently fall through to the 25m default instead of 400m — the field was only confirmed to exist and be populated for numbered routes via DescribeFeatureType, not its exact value format), whether Overpass's real building coverage in a given rural area is dense enough to be useful, and whether the grid-sampling approximation *looks* right (speckled squares reading as a sensible clipped shape) rather than just being mathematically correct in isolation.
26. **npm/pip/most CDN registries are blocked from this sandbox's Bash environment** (`registry.npmjs.org`, `pypi.org`, `unpkg.com`, `cdnjs.cloudflare.com` all return 403; `raw.githubusercontent.com` and `api.github.com` do work) — discovered while evaluating turf.js for v24. Relevant to any future feature that might consider an external JS/Python library: it can't be installed or CDN-loaded and verified locally in this sandbox before shipping, which is why v24 (like v19 before it) hand-rolled its geometry math instead.
27. **The v25 correction covers the province-wide rules and the named 400 m highway list, but explicitly not everything in the source document** (per Karim's own scoping choice): the ~50 remaining pages of per-region named local No Shooting Areas (e.g. Region 1's Cowichan River, Nanoose Bay, and Westcan Terminals Road areas) aren't implemented at all; Twelve Mile Road (Road 190) and Callaghan Road are named 400 m corridors that couldn't be matched to a numbered route and are skipped entirely; the four coordinate-clipped highways (3, 5, 97, 99) use landmark-based lat/lng windows, not a surveyed boundary, and could be off by a meaningful margin right at the clip edge; and Hwy 99's West Vancouver–Squamish leg is simplified from the source document's actual asymmetric 400m-west/1km-east buffer to a uniform 400 m. All of this is intentional, documented scope, not an oversight — but worth knowing if a future request assumes full document coverage.
28. **The v26 oversized-cutblock threshold (`CUTBLOCK_AREA_SANITY_HA = 7500`) hasn't been visually confirmed against real BC data either** — Node `vm`-based unit (15 assertions) and integration (5 assertions) tests only, all against the real extracted source, all passing. Specifically unconfirmed: whether Karim's actual reported Hwy 99 location, reloaded, now shows a normal map (the fix addresses the general mechanism, not a re-check of that exact spot); whether any real, legitimate BC cutblock ever approaches the 7,500 ha threshold and would be wrongly flagged (the FREP-report research found nothing close, but that report only covers 1996-2002 data); and whether `cutList.slice(0,5)`'s unsorted-by-size ordering ever surfaces mostly-flagged entries in the condensed report-card view at a real location with several large records nearby (see the v26 section's closing note).
29. **The v27 logging-phase code glossary covers only 9 codes, sourced from three different BC documents with varying confidence** — see the v27 section above for exactly which codes came from where and how directly each was confirmed. Any code outside that list (a real possibility — BC's own service metadata confirms neither field publishes a complete domain) still displays as a bare, undecoded code in the detail popup ("Logging phase" row); it no longer appears at all in the condensed report-card/overlap-chooser lines as of v31, which now show Life Cycle instead — see the v31 section above.
30. **The v28 recreation sites/trails/areas layer hasn't been visually confirmed against real BC data either** — Node `vm`-based unit (23 assertions) and two integration passes (19 map-drawing assertions + 18 report/checklist assertions), all against the real extracted source, all passing. Specifically unconfirmed: real-world density of RSTBC records at a typical BC backcountry shooting spot (could turn out to rarely fire); whether `PROJECT_TYPE`/`LIFE_CYCLE_STATUS_CODE`'s real values are as clean as the one sample `GetFeature` response suggested; and the practical impact of the points layer's missing status field (an old/decommissioned rec site showing as a live hit with no way to flag it).
31. **The v29 cutblock "Life Cycle" field hasn't been visually confirmed against real BC data either** — Node `vm`-based unit (19 assertions) and integration (19 assertions) tests only, both against the real extracted source, all passing. Lower-risk than most prior unconfirmed items though, since the field's domain is fully documented (not inferred) and only ever populated for entries with a matching FTEN record — worth a quick real-world spot-check (click a known active cutblock and confirm "Life Cycle: ACTIVE" shows up) rather than a deep investigation.

Specifically unconfirmed: whether the hand-rolled point-in-polygon test performs acceptably (no perceptible click lag) against a cutblock with an unusually complex/high-vertex-count real polygon, and whether the chooser's row styling (borrowed from the report card's condensed cutblock lines) reads well inside a Leaflet popup's narrower width in practice.

Specifically unconfirmed: whether the "n/a" fallback (shown when a condensed-list entry has no matching FTEN record, so no Life Cycle value) reads sensibly in practice given RESULTS-only entries are common in this app's own merge logic, and whether the raw status remaining reachable via "Details ›" is discoverable enough, or whether Karim would rather see both status and life cycle on the condensed line together.

Worth specifically confirming: clicking a parcel, a park, a Crown tenure, a woodlot, a recreation site/trail/area, and a cutblock all leave the marker and the report untouched, while clicking bare map background still correctly selects a new location.

38. **The v36 Topo basemap hasn't been clicked on inside the actual running app yet** — the tile source itself was confirmed live (real `fetch()`, HTTP 200, real tile bytes) and the code change is small/additive with `node --check` passing, but nobody has actually opened the app and pressed the new "⛰️ Topo" button to confirm it renders and toggles correctly end-to-end.
39. **The Life Cycle "n/a" discrepancy Karim reported for Opening 1721162 was investigated in real depth (v36 section above) but not resolved** — every individual piece (BC's live data, the app's own DWITHIN query against both source layers, the merge function against that exact real data) checked out correctly in isolation, and Karim couldn't reproduce the original report afterward. Left as an open question rather than a confirmed bug; the working theory is a boundary/precision edge case in how close the original click was to the 1 km search radius, given RESULTS' and FTEN's independently-traced polygons for the same block differ by a few meters at their edges. Only worth chasing further if it recurs with a specific, reproducible set of coordinates.
40. **The v37 pre-1990 cutblock filter hasn't been visually confirmed against real BC data inside the running app either** — the CQL syntax and field presence were confirmed live against BC's real server at every step (not assumed), and the app's own `cqlFor()`/`wfsUrl()` functions were unit-tested and their generated URL fetched live end-to-end, all passing -- but nobody has yet clicked a real location with known older logging history and watched the report/map actually reflect the filter in the browser.
